# Multi-model platform — design spec (2026-09-25)

> **Status: PROPOSED — awaiting owner review.** Nothing in this document is
> implemented. It supersedes the scope of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-10-model-router.md`
> (APPROVED 2026-08-17, never started) and folds that plan's R0–R5 into phases P3–P5 below with the
> amendments in §0a–§0b. The 2026-08-17 owner decisions (subscription-first, Codex on ChatGPT Free,
> Gemini unpaid key, OpenRouter free-tiers-only / no metered spend, INV-21) are honoured; where the
> research shows a decision's premise has changed, the change is flagged as a decision (§12), never
> silently reversed. **Review rounds 1, 2 (2026-09-25) and 3 (2026-09-27) are applied** — see "Review log" at the end; round 1
> added the D0 branch decision, D21–D24, the §12a owner runbook, and re-based every cost figure on the prod ledger;
> round 2 corrected D0 against the approved plan (a Free-plan Codex `research-read` writer canary is in-scope for P4),
> made the `_aicodex` containment executable (sudoers wrapper, stream homes outside the checkout), re-priced the
> ledger at the 1-h cache-write rate, replaced the plan-price hold with an alarm, added D25, and re-cut the runbook;
> round 3 closed the tracked `.claude/settings*.json` hole, made protected-path status mechanical in the dev repo,
> replaced the token-cap denominator with a dollar calibration from the 28-reading utilization series, corrected the
> alpha-valve and cache-write-leg claims, made Codex job-copy materialisation executable, and **froze the spec**
> (Appendix A §5: round 3 is terminal; later findings go against the implementation plans).
>
> **Owner ask (verbatim intent, 2026-09-24):** "integrate usage of the other main models so that I can
> schedule tasks, interface with them, deliver projects, automate trading and watch their progress …
> give me both ease of use across multiple interfaces as well as visibility and ultimately deliverable
> effectiveness. Do not be afraid to offer a total overhaul + refactor if you think that would be best."
>
> **How this was produced (Appendix A has the detail):** 28 web-researched, adversarially fact-checked
> provider/runtime/cross-cut docs (`docs/research/llm-landscape-2026-09/`, index `00-comparison-matrix.md`);
> a code-level current-state map of this repo (8 readers + 40 spot-checked claims); six independent
> architecture proposals (incremental, kernel overhaul, interface-first, outcome-first, vendor-native hub,
> multi-bot chat) scored by three judges (owner-UX, safety/feasibility, cost/effectiveness); a synthesis
> that starts from the two co-winners and grafts the rest; then round 1 of the adversarial review loop
> (its log is the last section of this file; later rounds append to it). Citations: "state map §n" =
> `docs/research/llm-landscape-2026-09/design-panel/current-state-map.md` (its `file:line` cites were
> re-verified against the working tree on 2026-09-24; its §3 table C1–C26 is the compliance checklist);
> "interface-first §n", "outcome-first §n", "kernel §n", "incremental §n", "hub §n", "multi-bot §n" =
> `design-panel/design-<name>.md` in the same folder (the synthesis is `design-synthesis.md` there);
> `<doc> §n` = a research doc; "plan §n" = the 2026-08-10 router plan.
>
> **Verdict on "total overhaul":** not warranted. The vendor-neutral spine (job row + queue, per-job JSONL
> audit, text-marker lifecycle, workspace clone + ff-sync, guard predicates, in-session review gate) is
> load-bearing and stays; the accidental parts (one vendor-shaped executor, an alias table doubling as a
> CI allowlist, a global quota key, dropped cost/turn data, thin text-shaped surfaces) are replaced. The
> self-hosted orchestrator option was evaluated and rejected (§0b, `workflow-orchestrators.md §7–8`).


**Judging outcome.** Mean scores tie `design-interface-first` and `design-outcome-first` at 7.71; judge 1 named interface-first, judges 2 and 3 named outcome-first, and all three said "ship one, graft the other". The two are complementary, not rivals: interface-first is the only design that phases in the order the owner *feels* (persisted origin + DMs + ghost fixes in week 0-1, phone control plane by week 3, hot-path refactor last behind a replay gate); outcome-first is the only design whose core is a runner-verified definition of done and a grader that is never the writer's vendor. Outcome-first's own fatal flaws were about phasing (a two-week big-bang P0, contracts in observe mode until week 7, INV-13 blocking fleet-wide); interface-first's were about mechanisms (transcript-parse done checks, `ai-mcp` mutating tools, `owner_edited` on atlas rows, Gemini-free classifying owner text). So: **interface-first's control plane, phasing and lanes are the spine; outcome-first's Deliverable Contract, verdict ledger, provider ledger and redacted projection are the effectiveness core**, with the grafts from the other four listed in §0 and marked `[graft: <design>]` where they land.

## 0) Decision summary

**Read this first — the five things the owner is being asked to approve** (added in round 3, because the proposition
was buried under two rounds of review bookkeeping):

1. **What ships in the approvable unit (P0–P1, ≈2.5 agent-weeks).** P0: observability columns (migration 007), the
   `src/notify/` outbox with persisted origin so every human launch DMs and every scheduled failure DMs within 60 s,
   credential canaries, the secret belts (`os.environ` assertion, `.claude/settings*.json` refusal, audit redactor),
   the cost-reconcile script, the trading tokens + interim `provisioning_gap` pre-check, and the ops debt (R2 backup,
   `pmset`, Ollama cleanup). P1: one grammar and one control plane, the live JobCard, `approvals` rows so a deploy is
   a button, `deliverables` as Telegram documents, durable cancel, schedule run/pause/resume.
   **What P0–P1 do not include: any other vendor.** They are Anthropic-only hygiene and control-plane work. The first
   non-Anthropic lane of any kind is P3 (`utility_call`, `ask local|cerebras`), the first non-Anthropic *writer* is P4
   (the Codex `research-read` canary), the first cross-vendor second opinion is P4 after D21, and the red-window hedge
   lands at P5. The owner's ask begins ≈6 weeks after sign-off; P0–P1 are what makes it observable and controllable.
2. **The sign-off set is six decisions**, not twenty-six: **D1** (scope = P0–P1 with a re-approval gate), **D3**'s
   P0-marked rows, **D4** (training toggles), **D10** (the auth PR — now a P2-entry gate, §4.3), **D12** (ops debt) and
   **D25** (the ToS posture on a seat loaded at ≈8–10× its price). **D21** joins the set in round 3 because it halves or
   doubles P4 (§12 D21, §9 P4). Everything else in §12 is the **deferred register** — it carries a trigger condition,
   not a decision the owner makes now.
3. **The owner's time for the sign-off set**: §12a rows 0, 1, 2, 2b, 4, 4b, 5, 6, 6b, 7, 7b, 8 ≈ **194 min ≈ 3.2 h**.
   The §12a footer's ≈11.5 h + 4.5 h covers all six phases, not the approvable unit.
4. **The honest cost line**: **$0 incremental cash** (Max 5x as today, ChatGPT Free, Gemini unpaid, local, R2 free tier),
   against a measured **≈ $963/month list-equivalent** on the trailing 30 d — cache-write-led in dollars, ≈8–10× the
   seat (D25) — and a post-flywheel run-rate that would be ≈ $2,050–3,400/month if sustained (§2.8, §13).
5. **Review provenance**: three adversarial rounds, applied; the correction narrative lives in the Review log, not here.

**D0 — resolved on 2026-08-17, kept only as a pointer.** The branch question is not a decision the owner makes now:
plan R3 (`:229`) and decision 2 (`:285-288`) already approved a Free-plan Codex `research-read` writer canary, and
branch B (pay) is a **measurement-triggered contingency inside D5**, not a separate choice. Its costed delta is §0c.

**What the 2026-08-17 decision actually allows (§12 D0; corrected in review round 2).** Round 1
read the 08-17 rules (Codex on ChatGPT Free, unpaid Gemini key, no metered spend) as "the other models can
only grade, critique and classify until P6". That misstated the approved plan: R3 of
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-10-model-router.md` (`:229`) is "Codex agentic lane … ChatGPT account (owner
picks plan — Free tier suffices for probing); qualify on `research-read`, then `code-project` canary", and
decision 2 (`:285-288`) is "Start Free. $0, probing only. Revisit after R3 canaries show whether Codex actually
carries load." A Free-plan Codex **writer canary on `research-read`** was therefore approved five weeks ago,
and the research permits it (`chatgpt-openai.md §3` rule (a): owner-only, owner-triggered or owner-read output
→ plan auth acceptable; rule (b) — *anything scheduled at volume, anything unattended for hours, and anything
a second person consumes* → `CODEX_API_KEY` — makes a **scheduled** canary an accepted risk, not a permitted
pattern, exactly as it does for the grader belt; §3 Codex row, D5). The ff-sync-before-review hole that
motivated deferring writers is closed by `publish.py` + belt 3b, both in P4. So: **(A) — 08-17 as approved
and recommended** — Codex enters `research-read` as a canary *writer* in P4 on Free plan auth: owner-only
memos; `codex exec --sandbox read-only --output-schema` produces the artefact text; the runner's `publish.py`
writes and pushes it only after a belt-3b `lgtm`; `skipped` on quota; ≥20 canary jobs before any schedule row
is `routable` to it; the `code-project` canary follows at P5 exit (D15 moves P6 → P5) under the same gate.
**(B) — pay** (ChatGPT Plus $20, or a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` for scheduled volume) only if P4
measures `skip_reason=quota` > 30 % on the canary + grader belt, or if the owner wants the rule-(b)-clean lane
now; B's cost side is in §0c so the two branches are weighed on the same evidence. The measured metered cost
of the whole scheduled load at the *dev-sample* shape was ~$8–14/month (matrix §4), but the prod ledger says
the real load is ≈ $963/month list-equivalent at the 1-h cache-write rate the subscription actually uses
(§2.8), which changes the premise of plan §10.3 in the other direction: the seat is loaded at ≈ 8–10× its
price (D25), and the research's fix for that is a second *lane*, never a second seat (`crosscut-tos §4.3`).
The text below is written for branch A as corrected; every place round 1 wrote "P6" for the first
non-Anthropic writer now reads "P4 (`research-read`) / P5 exit (`code-project`)".

What we are building (ten bullets):

1. **One control plane, one grammar, every surface a view** — `src/control/` (grammar → typed intents → view models → per-channel renderers) fronting Telegram, a static PWA, an `ai` CLI and a *read-only* `ai-mcp`; `/task` and its `--kind/--model/--effort/--project` flags stay the canonical launch (no `--as/--on` relearning; judge 1), bare text = `/task`. *[spine: interface-first §1.2, §2.1]*
2. **Every human launch DMs, every job is watchable (`watch`), from week 1** — persisted `origin_*` on jobs and tasks replaces the in-process `_job_to_chat` dict (`telegram_bot.py:38, 1028`), a `notifications` outbox gives alert history, scheduled failures DM within 60 s, and a single self-editing JobCard walks `queued → held → starting → working → blocked → done (verdicts pending) → reviewed | failed` with a DoD tick-list (live from P2, §2.7; card edits are silent; Done/Failed/Approval cards are new reply messages; machine-launched jobs get a card only on `watch`, on failure, or on `notify=always` — the card-eligibility rule in §4.2). *[interface-first §1.3, §2.2; graft: outcome-first §2.1 DoD ticks; multi-bot §2.1 edit coalescing + `/mute`]*
3. **Definition of done is a runner-executed contract, not a transcript vibe** — frontmatter `done:` blocks with deterministic checks (`file_exists`, `git_pushed`, `url_200`, `db_row`, `tests_green`, `budget_within`, …), a default contract for every job, and an honest `done_verdict=unverified` on cards; graders receive an evidence bundle, never the writer's self-report. *[core: outcome-first §3.1-3.2]*
4. **Cross-vendor review is additive dissent, recorded structurally; Anthropic stays the only LGTM** — belts: evidence checks → in-session `code-review` gate (verdict finally stored) → Anthropic post-review (timeout now stamps a verdict) → cross-vendor grader/critic on a *redacted projection* with `grader.provider != writer.provider` enforced in code and fallback `skipped`, never Anthropic. For non-Anthropic writer lanes (the P4 `research-read` canary, the P5-exit `code-project` canary) the merge gate is the Anthropic `code-review` skill protocol run in-process in the parent's post-step over the clone diff, fail-closed and bounded, followed by a hardcoded path allowlist, all **before the runner publishes** (§5.3 belt 3b); on Anthropic lanes post-review stays post-push and flag-only (D8). Cross-vendor dissent is *adjudicated* (`verdicts.adjudication`, §5.3), so the scoreboard measures precision, not only agreement. Non-Anthropic *graders* on any class reverse the approved plan's §7 deferral — its words are "review-gate diversity experiments (Claude reviewing GPT code is in-plan; GPT reviewing anything is not)", R6+, separate decision — so they are an explicit owner decision, **D21**; until D21 is decided `code-project` has no grader. *[outcome-first §3.3, §6.3; kernel §3.2 (its `REVIEW_PRE_PUSH` switch is dropped — D8 defers what it would toggle); plan §7 quoted verbatim]*
5. **Provider registry + policy + per-provider quota land behind the plane** — `providers.yml`/`routing-policy.yml` replace the accidental allowlist (`telegram_bot.py:122-134` → `tests/test_skill_contracts.py:24`), quota keys go per provider, fan-out starts 2/2/1/1, and the Claude window is read from what the SDK's typed `RateLimitEvent` actually carries — `rate_limit_type ∈ {five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_opus, seven_day_sonnet, overage}` (the full `RateLimitType` literal set in `claude_agent_sdk/types.py:1181-1183` of the pinned 0.1.81; **all five are recorded verbatim and `quota_snapshots.family` is taken from the event type, never inferred**), `status` + `resets_at` on transitions, `utilization` when a transition carries it (732 prod events to 2026-09-27 10:32Z: 702 `five_hour` + **28 `seven_day`**, utilization 0.25 → 0.58, every one carrying `resets_at` 2026-10-02T00:00Z ⇒ a **fixed** weekly period, §2.8) — `source=inferred` applies only *between* readings, calibrated by the owner's own interactive sessions' `statusLine` feed (a one-time settings entry, D22); no figure is ever shown as vendor-sourced unless a vendor event or the status-line file carried it, and no runner-driven interactive probe session exists. *[plan §6a-f; interface-first §2.6; incremental §0a row 6]*
6. **Executor seam extracted last, behind a golden-file + audit-replay gate** — `ClaudeSdkExecutor` is `_build_options` + `_run_in_process` (`session.py:650-816, 1048-1132`) moved byte-for-byte; `ExecSpec.to_claude_options()` must match today's `ClaudeAgentOptions` on 10 recorded skills as a *normalised projection* (scalars and lists verbatim; `hooks` as `{event: [(matcher, [hook.__qualname__])]}`; `mcp_servers` as `{name: sorted(tool names)}`; `agents` as dataclass dicts — dataclass `==` on fresh hook closures and `create_sdk_mcp_server` objects is identity and would never pass) and the replay gate compares the executor-emitted event subsequence with `ts` stripped (§9 P3) before the seam goes live; typed `terminal_reason` (incl. `auth_expired`, `unrecognized_model` with the `model_usage` assertion) replaces the banner regex. *[incremental §8 R2 gates; kernel §1.2 events]*
7. **Non-Anthropic lanes are text-only by default and env-scrubbed always** — Codex runs `exec --json --ephemeral --sandbox read-only` **as a dedicated unprivileged macOS user `_aicodex`**, reached through a root-owned fixed-argv wrapper under a command-restricted sudoers grant (the only privilege mechanism a user-level LaunchAgent has — §2.6 "User separation mechanism"), over a tracked-files-only materialisation of the clone (**`git -C <clone> archive HEAD | tar -x -C /Users/Shared/ai-codex/<job8>`, run by the runner as the owner**, never `cp -R` and never a `git clone --shared` as `_aicodex` — the source clone lives under `~/Library/Application Support`, which is `drwx------`, so `_aicodex` cannot open it; tracked files only, so a gitignored `.env` and the `.git` dir never ride along; Seatbelt confines writes and network, not reads — §2.6), with a persistent per-stream `CODEX_HOME` under `/Users/_aicodex/streams/` (never a per-job copy of `auth.json`; every process that opens it — exec, the rate-limit RPC, the daily canary — holds the stream lease) and `EnvScrubber` default-deny (PATH/HOME/CODEX_HOME/LANG/TMPDIR), `features.goals=false`, `otel.metrics_exporter="none"`, no MCP, no `env_files`; utility HTTP lanes get no tools; the non-Anthropic *writers* — the `research-read` canary in P4 and the `code-project` canary at P5 exit (D0 as corrected) — sit behind the fail-closed pre-publish `code-review` gate, a hardcoded per-target path allowlist, and the runner's `publish.py` (§5.3 belt 3b, §2.6). *[kernel §1.6; incremental §1e; multi-bot §1.6]*
8. **Lanes with an owner reservation and visible holds** — `BLPOP jobs:queue:owner jobs:queue:kernel jobs:queue:atlas jobs:queue:utility jobs:queue` with legacy `jobs:queue` kept as `background`; the loop always drains every lane key (so `LANES_ENABLED=0` only changes where `enqueue_job` pushes and is safe in both directions), a held job is parked per lane with its slot released (never re-popped ahead of other lanes), and the nine sites that address `jobs:queue` by name (§6) move to `db.queue_key(lane)`/`ALL_QUEUE_KEYS`; `job_held{reason}` + `/queue` so a stalled job is never silently stuck. *[interface-first §4; kernel §1.7/§2.1]*
9. **Scheduling stays on one scheduler of record, made honest** — commit-before-RPUSH, slot-based `next_run_at`, `misfire_policy`, `notify ∈ {never,failures,always}`, `expects.within` late/missing alarms, phone edits on non-governed rows only (atlas loop rows keep LOOP.md §7), `/schedule add "<preset or cron>"` → a deterministic preset parser (**P2**, with `schedule edit`; through P1 `add` accepts a raw 5-field cron only) with a confirm button; free-text phrases go through the local model only from P3. Vendor-native schedulers are not producers. *[interface-first §4; hub §3-4 `expects`; multi-bot §2.1 phrase→cron]*
10. **Trading = research, critic, evaluation, paper loops; never an order path** — `atlas-critic` (Codex read-only, no atlas env, redacted memo) after the Anthropic validator; local Qwen for mechanical checks; server-side broker-host deny in `guards.py` (owner PR); per-vertical `env_files`; a trading-health "is any of this working" page; the cheap blockers fixed in named phases (tokens + an interim `provisioning_gap` scheduler pre-check in P0, `weekly.py:316` and the Alpaca fallback as atlas-front-door patches in P1 — §9). The Max/subscription lane is never the decision step for a live order (Anthropic Consumer Terms §3, §8.2/§8.3). *[interface-first §6; outcome-first §6; kernel §2.2 health page]*

What we are explicitly **NOT** building: a council/persona room, puppet bots or a supergroup in `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS` (multi-bot D1); an IMAP mailbox collector, vendor-side crons as producers, ChatGPT Plus or SuperGrok by default (hub §11); metered xAI/Perplexity/OpenRouter spend (declined 2026-08-17, plan §10.3); a runner-driven interactive Claude statusLine probe session (the `statusLine` file written by the owner's *own* interactive sessions, D22, is a calibration feed, not a probe); `--as/--on`, `--lane/--priority`, `--class/--done` launch flags or `ai job run` verb nesting; mandatory `<<<EVIDENCE>>>` blocks; a sorted-set queue; a `src/kernel/` rename, a `job_attempts` table or dropping `Task.chat_id` this cycle; four migrations in one phase; INV-13 blocking on Anthropic lanes by default; Codex grading `code-server` diffs; Web Push/CF Access/SSE-cookie changes outside one owner-approved auth PR; a LiteLLM or Langfuse daemon (`runtimes-aggregators.md §3(c)`: 4 GiB / 16 GiB floors); replacing the runner with a self-hosted orchestrator (n8n, Temporal, Prefect, Hatchet, Windmill — evaluated in `workflow-orchestrators.md §7–8`: they replace only the scheduler/queue/state machine and none of the parts that are hard to buy; a Prefect shadow pilot stays optional, D20); a mutating tool of any kind inside a model-driven front door; an SDK pin bump outside the replay gate; any order tool; direct conversational chat with non-Anthropic models on Telegram/PWA (a bounded one-shot `ask <provider>` verb is the substitute, D23); data MCPs with order tools mounted in any session (read-only data MCPs are D24, default no).

| §7 "GPT reviewing anything is not in-plan" | Codex/Gemini rank **high** independence for reviewing Claude output; Kimi/MiniMax/DeepSeek/Qwen named in distillation disclosures (`crosscut-subscription-automation-tos.md §5.2a`) | Proposed, **not yet decided — D21** (it reverses the plan's R6+ "separate decision" deferral): Codex/Gemini graders as *additive dissent* on `research-read` and redacted `trading-research` from P4, on `code-project` only after 30 shadow grades; `code-server`/`review-gate`/`_evaluate` stay Anthropic-only with **no** advisory grade (judge 2 condition 1); until D21 is decided `code-project: grader: none` |
| §8 INV-21(b) Codex Seatbelt | confirmed A− rating; network off by default; goal mode + `statsig` metrics on by default (`chatgpt-openai.md §3, §7`; `crosscut-tos §3.8a`) | lane contract: `--ephemeral`, `features.goals=false`, `otel.metrics_exporter="none"`, `analytics.enabled=false`, `web_search="cached"`, `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false` pinned per job (the research marks the default unverified, `runtimes-aggregators.md` line 74), `stale_head → flagged`; **INV-21(b) gains "filesystem reads confined to the clone"** via a dedicated unprivileged user (§2.6), because Seatbelt `read-only` confines writes and network, not reads |
| — | `--bare` "will become the default for `-p`" and never reads OAuth; `claude setup-token` is a one-year token (`claude-anthropic.md §3`); in SDK 0.1.81 `ClaudeAgentOptions.env` is an **overlay** on the inherited `os.environ` (`subprocess_cli.py:430-436`), not a replacement | SDK/CLI pin gate + `claude -p ping` smoke through the runner's own path (P0: `scripts/credential-canary.sh` on a launchd timer using the SDK-bundled CLI from the runner venv; P3 onward: `ClaudeSdkExecutor`) — never the brew binary; the Keychain login stays the live credential; the setup-token is minted and sealed (0600, outside any workspace) as a fallback that is **never fleet-wide**: on canary-detected login expiry the runner holds every lane except `owner`, DMs an ApprovalCard, and injects the token via `options.env` (overlay wins for that key) only into jobs the owner explicitly resumes (§3, §6); isolation of the Claude subprocess rests on keeping the runner's `os.environ` free of secrets (fail-closed startup assertion beside `_check_subscription_auth`, §2.4), never on `env=`; never exported into the launchd plist (P0 exit test: `plutil -p` over `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.assistant.*` finds no `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`); never pass `--bare` |
| — | Consumer training toggles default on; theses 30-day retained at best; Fable always 30 d (`crosscut-tos §3.8`) | `sensitivity` on every skill (default `owner-private` for human-originated or transcript-derived text); quarterly training-toggle checklist card gates thesis-class routing — stored as `approvals(kind=attestation, subject=provider, payload={training_off: true}, expires_at)` and checked by `policy.select()`: `codex` is registered `data: {trains: true, max_sensitivity: public}` and the ceiling rises to `owner-private` (and to `proprietary` on the redacted projection) only while an unexpired `approvals(kind=attestation, subject=codex)` row exists — the same lint-hardcoded floor mechanism as `gemini-free` — so `ask codex`, the grader belt and the `research-read` canary all sit at `public` until D4 is stamped (card DM'd 14 days before expiry); the card also re-verifies Anthropic usage credits disabled, Codex credits $0 / `spendControlReached` unset, the nonessential-traffic env vars (hub §10), and that Devin's bundled `claude` still uses the owner's own `/login` (§12a). **Limitation stated honestly:** approval and attestation rows are ordinary Postgres rows; any of the 48 unhooked sessions with `DATABASE_URL` could INSERT one, so each decided row also carries a bot-only artefact that the deploy gate and `policy.select()` verify. **The artefact's secret is never in `.env`** (round 3): all three processes load the same `.env` through `Settings`, and an unhooked session that can INSERT the row can also read `.env` and compute the HMAC, so an `.env`-derived signature is no artefact at all. The two designs that survive that threat model: **(a)** the bot process holds the key in the macOS Keychain (`security add-generic-password`, ACL-bound to the bot's binary) and *signs on request* over `events:control`, so the runner verifies by asking the bot rather than by holding the key — `security find\|dump\|export\|delete\|unlock` is already Bash-hard-denied for hooked sessions (`guards.py:140-141`); **or (b)** the runner re-verifies the recorded `update_id`/`callback_query.id` against the Telegram Bot API before honouring a decision, so the artefact is unforgeable without the bot token. One of the two is named in the P1 PR; `test_intents` gains "an approvals row inserted with a `.env`-derived signature is refused". D16 (isolation flip) is still the real fix |

### 0c) Branch B / D5 options (b)–(c) — the costed delta (so both are weighed on the same evidence; this table is D5's cost input, §12 D5)

Branch A (the text of this spec) puts a Free-plan Codex `research-read` canary writer in P4 and a `code-project` canary at P5 exit. Branch B changes only *capacity and terms*, not shape; everything below is the delta from A.

| Item | Branch A (Free, as approved 08-17) | Branch B (pay) |
|---|---|---|
| Codex auth | ChatGPT Free, plan auth, `codex login --device-auth` per stream | ChatGPT Plus $20/month (same plan auth, Sol 15–150 msgs/5 h per `chatgpt-openai.md §4`), **or** a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` project key (Responses API; no-training by default, ZDR-eligible; a metered exception to 08-17 the owner must grant explicitly — §13) |
| ToS classification of the scheduled belt + canary | rule (b) accepted risk (scheduled at volume) | Plus: **same rule-(b) risk, more capacity** — moving to Plus is a capacity statement, not a terms statement; API key: rule-(b) clean |
| Codex cost at the measured shapes | $0 | Plus: $20 flat. API key at Sol list ($2/$10, cached $0.20/M): `research-read` canary at the class median (≈ 380 k in, ≈ 82 % cache, 9 k out) ≈ **$0.29/job** (311 k cached × $0.20/M = $0.062 + 68 k × $2/M = $0.137 + 9 k × $10/M = $0.090; round 2's "$0.15" priced all 380 k input at the cached rate) × ~36 jobs/month (~20 canary + 16 routable) ≈ **$10**; `code-project` at the atlas-build shape (≈ 7.3 M tokens/job) ≈ $1.5–2/job × ~1.5 canary jobs/month ≈ $3; grader belt 60–90 runs ≈ $1–2 (Luna-class) or $27–40 (Sol-class). **Option-(c) total ≈ $37–56/month, i.e. above Plus's flat $20 — (c) buys rule-(b) cleanliness and ZDR, not a lower bill** |
| Anthropic gate cost per non-Anthropic writer job (both branches) | one belt-3b `code-review` session per canary job, opus-4-7/high ≈ $1.2–1.6 list-equivalent at the review shape — ≥20 canary jobs ⇒ ≈ +$30/month on the Max window during the canary | same |
| Net Anthropic-window effect | `research-read` canary jobs move ≈ $0.7–3.4/job *off* the window and put ≈ $1.4/job *back* as the gate: roughly window-neutral at 10 % canary share, a real hedge only once the lane is `routable` and the gate is sampled (D8 territory) | same for `research-read`; `code-project` never hedges the window in either branch (every job still consumes an Anthropic gate session) — B hedges the red window for `research-read` only |
| What B unlocks that A does not | — | quota-skip rate expected < 5 % instead of the unknown Free rate; ChatGPT developer-mode door for `ai-mcp` (D19); ZDR/no-training for theses on the API key (D4's retention note) |
| Owner actions (§12a) | rows 14–16b | + Plus upgrade **or** `CODEX_API_KEY` minted into `.env` (auth config, owner-only), D15 target list re-confirmed |
| Phases re-cut | none | none — the same P4 entry criteria (≥20 canary jobs, INV-21(c) test green) and P5 exit ("routable lanes keep draining") apply. **Registry changes, stated (round 3 correction — round 2's "none" was wrong):** `providers.yml: codex.auth`; `codex.cost_class` `free-tier` → `plan-flat` under (b) or `metered` under (c); under (c) the id must also be listed under `routing-policy.yml: owner_approved_metered:` with an `approvals(kind=provider_promote, decided_via=telegram)` id **and** `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK=codex` added to the launchd plist (§12a row 13b), because the runtime belt refuses a metered provider otherwise; the `canary:` attribute needs no change (it is exempt from the chain-terminator rule, §2.5); and the cost line |

Z.ai is **not** an option in B: the research restricts the Z.ai Coding Plan to "officially supported tools" and puts theses on the metered Z.ai API only (matrix §2 Z.ai row, §5 #21); this server has no Z.ai executor and none is planned.

## 1) Goals and non-goals mapped to the owner's ask

| Owner ask | Goal (measurable) | Non-goal |
|---|---|---|
| Ease of use across interfaces | Same verbs on Telegram, PWA, `ai` CLI, `ai-mcp` (read-only); launch in one line; ack card ≤2 s; deploy approvals are a button, not a retype; `/schedule add` works from a phone (**P2** — through P1 it accepts a raw 5-field cron only, §6); `--model=<provider>/…` on an owner-originated launch picks any registered, non-metered provider that has an executor for the class's capability set on a non-pinned class (one rule, §2.5: `codex/*` is real on `research-read` from P4 and on `code-project` from P5 exit; refused `no_executor` before P4 and `pinned` on the **five** pinned classes) and `help [<verb>]` says exactly that per phase (§4.1: `help` is a grammar verb rendered from the same verb/flag table `grammar.py` parses, not a hand-written string); `--second-opinion=<provider>` on `task`/`retry` from P4; `lane hold\|release` gives the owner a fleet-level lever short of `/clear` (§4.1, §6) | Vendor apps as systems of record; email/Slack channels; a chat room of personas |
| Interface with the other models | An explicit `--model=<provider>/<id>` on an owner-originated launch (`origin_channel ∈ {telegram, pwa, cli}`) is honoured as an **owner override** (audited `provider_selected{reason: owner_override}`) under the same INV-21 contract as a policy pick — workspace clone, OS sandbox + dedicated user, env-scrub, fail-closed Anthropic gate + path allowlist before publish — irrespective of chain membership or ladder state, provided the provider is registered, non-metered and has an executor for the class's capability set; sensitivity, attestation, `max_sessions` and the belt-3b gate still apply; on a pinned class (`code-server`, `review-gate`, `_evaluate`, `chat`, `trading-research`) it is refused at parse time with a card naming the pin (the single rule lives in §2.5); **pre-D10, an owner override is honoured only for `origin_channel == telegram` or a request carrying a recorded Cloudflare Access `aud` claim** — the override regime widens authority (§2.5 (i)), so a `pwa`/`cli` launch naming a non-default provider is refused `needs_cf_access` and falls back to `policy.select()`; a bounded one-shot `ask <provider> "<text>"` verb (D23) returns a no-tools answer as a DeliverableCard — **P3 for HTTP/local lanes** (`ollama-local`, `anthropic-utility`; `cerebras-free`/`groq-free` after D18), **P4 for `ask codex`** (through `codex_cli.py` as `_aicodex` under the stream lease, only after Codex leaves `blocked`); `--second-opinion=codex` (after D21) and `jobs --provider=codex` are the other P4 touchpoints; `/providers` and the read-only `ai-mcp` inside the vendor apps complete the set | Conversational chat with non-Anthropic models on Telegram/PWA (Sonnet-low TTFT, owner-private text, training toggles — matrix §1 item 8); D23 records it as a later option |
| Visibility | Live JobCard (turn/tool/tokens/window %); `/queue` with hold reasons; `/quota` per provider from vendor signals (inferred figures labelled); per-provider live counts (running/queued/held, jobs/grades today, last error) on the ProviderPanel; `/cost` weekly; schedule adherence on the phone; deliverables with deep links; alert history; trading-health page | Per-tool latency decomposition beyond `queue_wait_ms`/`first_event_at`; host RSS series (later) |
| Deliverable effectiveness | `done_verdict` on 100 % of jobs; DoD pass-rate and grade per provider × task class; independence measured; scoreboard drives the ladder; retro replaces the opus/max idle auditor | Human ratings as a signal (2/1633); any grader as the merge gate |
| Scheduling across vendors | One scheduler of record with lanes, misfire policy, `expects.within`, per-row `provider_policy`; class-aware pause so an Anthropic red window pauses only pinned lanes. **Honest scope (D0 as corrected):** through P3 every scheduled *writer* is Anthropic; from P4 the Codex `research-read` canary writer (Free plan auth, ≥20 canary jobs, belt-3b gate, `publish.py`) is the first non-Anthropic writer a schedule row can resolve to once its lane reaches `canary` — `provider_policy: routable` on a `research-read` row then means "Anthropic, or Codex when the window is red and the canary is green"; `code-project` follows at P5 exit; pinned and every other class stay Anthropic, so an Anthropic red window still pauses the pinned and `code-*` lanes — utility, critic and the `research-read` canary keep draining. The plan's headline "Anthropic quota exhaustion no longer pauses the whole queue" is therefore met for `research-read` at P5, not for the fleet | Vendor-native crons as producers; minute-cadence anything on vendor lanes |
| Project delivery | Clone → work → evidence checks → gate → push → parked approval → gated deploy → DoneCard with URL + healthcheck; Codex `research-read` canary from P4 and `code-project` canary from P5 exit, each with a fail-closed Anthropic gate and a hardcoded path allowlist before the runner publishes (§2.6, §5.3 belt 3b); the canary's targets are owner-only repos on plan auth (atlas `research/**`, `docs/**`, report repos), or public-facing repos only on a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` (D15 rescoped) | **Any public surface that triggers a subscription-lane model call per viewer request** (a public chat widget, a public Telegram bot, a page whose views call Max) — `crosscut-tos §3.9` line 187, `claude-anthropic.md` line 87. **Batch-generated artefacts the owner publishes are in scope and permitted** (atlas reports, the pickem/bingo pages): the research says they are "not addressed either way", and forbidding them would dark existing publishing |
| Trading research (INV-22) | Critic stage, mechanical checks, constitution DoD, per-vertical env scoping, server-side broker deny, health page; blockers fixed first | Any order path, broker MCP with order tools, sentiment as a signal, metered feeds by default |

## 2) Architecture

### 2.1 Components

```
                                   OWNER'S HANDS
  phone: Telegram ───┐   laptop/phone: PWA /app ───┐   terminal: `ai` ───┐   Claude Desktop/Codex: `ai-mcp` (read-only)
   cards + buttons   │   views + streamed fetch    │   stream + tail     │   ai_jobs ai_job ai_job_stream ai_queue ai_quota ai_cost …
  ───────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────
                                    │  ONE GRAMMAR  src/control/grammar.py   (/task flags unchanged; verbs added)
                                    ▼
             CONTROL PLANE  src/control/  intents.py · views.py · policy.py (god/privilege strip written once, C10)
             ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
             │ intents: task watch jobs job tasks queue cancel retry reopen approve reject approvals  │
             │          reply ask mute alerts digest scoreboard send schedule_* providers quota cost   │
             │          deliverables atlas proposals project_* lane_* help clear(confirm)              │
             │ views:   JobCard DoneCard FailedCard ApprovalCard VerdictCard DeliverableCard           │
             │          ScheduleCard QuotaStrip ProviderPanel CostCard AtlasCard DailyDigest          │
             │          AlertHistory Scoreboard          (one name per object — §4.2 uses these)      │
             └──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                            │ enqueue_job (jobs.py:16-47, shape unchanged) │ read-only queries
                            ▼                                              ▼
  STATE  Postgres  jobs(+run/origin/contract columns)  tasks(+origin, awaiting_since)  schedules(+lane, misfire, notify, expects…)
                   notifications(outbox) approvals deliverables verdicts provider_ledger quota_snapshots provider_qualifications
  BUS    Redis     jobs:queue:<lane> (+legacy jobs:queue)  jobs:stream:<id> (rich)  jobs:done:<id>  jobs:reviewed:<id>  jobs:cancel
                   quota:<provider>:paused_until  quota:<provider>:counters:<day>  heartbeat:runner  events:control
                            │
                            ▼
  RUNNER src/runner/main.py   lane-ordered BLPOP · owner reservation · provider-window gate · job_held (slot-before-BLPOP kept)
     ├─ registry/models.py + providers.yml + routing-policy.yml + provider_qualifications → policy.select()
     ├─ ExecSpec (vendor-neutral) → executors/
     │     ├ claude_sdk.py   = _build_options + _run_in_process moved byte-for-byte (trust anchor; hooks, MCP, subagents)
     │     ├ codex_cli.py    = codex exec --json --ephemeral --sandbox read-only|workspace-write; as user _aicodex via sudo→fixed-argv wrapper; per-stream CODEX_HOME (/Users/_aicodex/streams); EnvScrubber; tracked-files clone copy only
     │     ├ completions.py  = utility_call(): ollama-local / cerebras|groq(owner-private) / gemini-free(public) / anthropic-utility; in-process, no tools, redactor first
     │     └ script.py       = no_llm skills (skills.py:55, dead today): fs/transcript checks, canaries, adherence — NO DB/Redis/HTTP (Seatbelt denies all network incl. loopback: Postgres and Redis are localhost TCP, so db_row, the retro SQL pre-pass and the autopilot's enqueue run in the runner process, §2.4)
     ├─ normalized ExecEvent → same audit kinds (C14) + jobs:stream + provider_ledger + heartbeat
     ├─ post-steps (each under asyncio.wait_for(_POST_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS), whole chain under a 900 s wall clock; order keeps the status flip ahead of anything that reads it, §2.7):
                    run_session returns → contracts.check + deliverable auto-detection (clone alive) → gate verdict capture
                    → [non-Anthropic lanes only: path allowlist → merge gate (in-process code-review under wait_for(600)) → publish.py (runner push)]
                    → _finish_job (status=completed, result, jobs:done) → refetch → promote_deferred_for (DAG) → _maybe_review (post-review)
                    → writeback → grader/critic (vendor ≠ writer) + critic_mirror (grading.py writes <vertical>.critic_verdicts)
                    → learning (proposal row) → task lifecycle (markers unchanged) → notify outbox → jobs:reviewed
     └─ finally, wrapping _process_job's try/except (NOT a success-chain step): finalize_workspace — ff-sync on the
                    success path only; the Codex copy under /Users/Shared/ai-codex/ removed on EVERY path (success,
                    quota requeue, timeout, cancel, resolution error); the per-job clone kept on failure as today
                            │
                            ▼
  NOTIFY src/notify/   outbox → renderers: Telegram · SSE · CLI · `python -m src.notify send` (launchd alerters, edge Worker keep working)
```

### 2.2 Control plane (`src/control/`, new) *[interface-first §1.2]*

- `grammar.py`: one parser. `/task <text> [--kind=<skill>] [--model=<provider/model|alias>] [--effort=] [--project=] [--timeout=] [--watch] [--quiet]` — the existing flags (`telegram_bot.py:140-172`) with unknown models rejected here against the registry (today `:157` passes them through unchecked). A *known* provider is rejected at parse time too when (a) the inferred class is pinned (the card names the pin), (b) the provider has no executor yet for that class's capability set (the card says "codex executor lands in P4"), or (c) the provider is `cost_class: metered` and not under `owner_approved_metered`; otherwise, on an owner-originated launch, it is an owner override — the one rule stated in §2.5, irrespective of chain membership or ladder state. `task`/`retry` gain `--second-opinion=<provider>` (alias `--critic=`, P4): one §5.3 grade on that job's evidence bundle under the pairing rule and sensitivity, rendered as the DoneCard's critic line. **`help [<verb>]` is a grammar verb, not a hand-written string** (round 3: ≈35 verbs whose legal set changes in four phases had an unowned discovery surface — today `telegram_bot.py:1297` → `cmd_help` is a literal block). It is rendered from the same verb/flag table `grammar.py` parses, with the per-phase provider sentence generated from the registry: through P3 "`--model` accepts Anthropic ids/aliases"; from P4 "plus `codex/<id>` on `research-read` (canary writer, D0) and as `--second-opinion=codex`"; from P5 exit "plus `codex/<id>` on `code-project`". P1 `test_grammar` gate: "help lists exactly the live verbs and exactly the live `--model`/`ask` provider set for this phase". The phrase "read-only classes" is retired — there is no such class; a Codex `--sandbox read-only` run writes nothing itself and the runner's `publish.py` materialises its `--output-schema` artefact after the gate. Replaces the three divergent parsers (Telegram leading-flags-only, `web.py` `CreateJobRequest`, `mcp_dispatch.py:35-40`).
- `intents.py`: pure functions `(actor, args) → Result | Error` over the models. The only mutation path into the runner stays `enqueue_job` (`jobs.py:16`). `cancel` gains a durable path for queued jobs (`LREM` + status flip; today `main.py:1368-1404` reaches running jobs only). `task` creates a `Task` + origin for every human launch, not only Telegram (`telegram_bot.py:231-256` vs `web.py:399-405`).
- `policy.py`: god/privilege strip and `kind=god` rejection written once (INV-18, C10); imported by Telegram, web, CLI, `ai-mcp`, `mcp_dispatch`.
- `views.py` + `renderers/{telegram,sse,cli,mcp}.py`: one view object per card, rendered per channel; renderers are the only code that knows Telegram's 4096 chars / 64-byte callback / ≤8 buttons (C23) and MarkdownV2 escaping (fixes `TROUBLESHOOTING.md:699-707`).
- **SPOF rule (judge 1/2 must-fix):** `telegram_bot.py` is not rewritten in P1. New handlers are registered *beside* the 12 legacy ones (`telegram_bot.py:1296-1313`); `CONTROL_PLANE_V2=0` restores legacy behaviour and `tests/test_grammar.py` asserts the legacy handler list is still registered with the flag off. Transport extraction happens in P5 after two releases of soak.

### 2.3 Data model (all additive, nullable; one migration per phase)

| Migration (phase) | Table | Columns / shape | Why (gap) |
|---|---|---|---|

Redis: `jobs:queue:<lane>` keys added, `jobs:queue` kept (`db.py` gains `queue_key(lane)` + `ALL_QUEUE_KEYS`; every site that names the list uses them, §6); `jobs:held:<lane>` ZSET scored by `next_check` (= `resets_at` when the hold reason carries one, else now+30) for window/budget holds — `requeue_stranded_queued` treats its members as present (§6); `quota:lane:<lane>:hold_until` for the owner's durable `lane hold` (§4.1); `codex:stream:<n>` lease (held by `codex exec`, the rate-limit RPC and the daily canary alike); `quota:codex:counters:<window>` for the grader sample cap; `quota:<provider>:*` and `quota:anthropic:<family>:*` (opus/sonnet) replace `quota:paused_until` (`quota.py:21`) in P3; `jobs:stream:<id>` gains `tool_result` previews, `guard_denied`, `rate_limit_status`, `heartbeat`, `dod_tick`; `jobs:reviewed:<id>` fires after post-steps *[graft: multi-bot §1.7]*; `events:control` carries fleet events for the Now panel.

### 2.4 Executors (`src/runner/executors/`, P3) *[plan §6b; kernel §1.8 adapter contract]*

- `base.py`: `ExecSpec{job_id, provider, model, effort, cwd, system_prompt, user_prompt, capabilities:set, guard_profile, mcp_servers, subagents, max_turns, timeout_s, output_schema, sensitivity}`; `Executor.start(spec) → AsyncIterator[ExecEvent]`, `interrupt()` (≤2 s, INV-8), `kill(grace_s)` (the timeout path finally calls it; today `main.py:376-380` never interrupts), `result() → ExecResult{final_text, usage, cost_usd_list, num_turns, duration_api_ms, model_served, terminal_reason ∈ ok|max_turns|interrupted|timeout|api_error{status}|rate_limited|auth_expired|unrecognized_model|**account_on_hold|oauth_revoked|billing_error**, permission_denials}`; conformance test with a fake executor. **Those last three are a round-3 addition, mapped from the vendor's own error *category* rather than an HTTP status:** the spec accepted enforcement risk on both vendors (D25; §3 "OpenAI may throttle or ban without notice") but had no class for an account suspension, a credential revocation or a billing event, so an account hold surfaced as a generic `api_error`, no lane was held, and `billing_error` — the direct vendor signal for the usage-credits path §2.8 is built to catch — was ignored in favour of a heuristic. `system/api_retry` carries `error ∈ {rate_limit, overloaded, account_on_hold, oauth_org_not_allowed, billing_error}` (`claude-anthropic.md` line 51; `crosscut-tos` line 71), the hook `StopFailure` matchers are the same set (`crosscut-tos` line 213), and `crosscut-tos` line 457 item 1(c) says to parse those events and **"back off the whole scheduler, not the job"**, with `autoContinueAtUsageLimit` "not offered at all" for `-p` runs. Runtime rule: `account_on_hold` or `oauth_revoked` (from `oauth_org_not_allowed`/`authentication_failed`) **holds every lane except `owner`**, queues `notice(severity=high, kind=provider_enforcement)` and DMs an ApprovalCard — the same fail-closed path §3 defines for canary-detected login expiry; `billing_error` becomes the **first** `possible_credit_overflow` trigger, ahead of `overage_status` and the ledger heuristic (§2.8). The mapping is a P0 `test_result_capture` case and §11 carries a "vendor enforcement action" row.
- `claude_sdk.py`: `_build_options` (`session.py:650-816`) split at its five seams (prompt 664-692, resolution 694-724, subagents 730-736, guards 752-755, MCP 757-796) into `ExecSpec` + `to_claude_options()`; `_run_in_process` (`:1048-1132`) + `_handle_message` (`:1138-1170`) moved verbatim. Two fixes ride along: read typed `api_error_status`/`stop_reason` (banner regex `:495-534` kept one release as a belt), and assert `model_usage ∋ requested model ∧ duration_api_ms > 0` (silent-empty-success trap, `claude-anthropic.md §7`). **Env isolation — stated for what the pinned SDK actually does.** In claude-agent-sdk 0.1.81 `ClaudeAgentOptions.env` is an *overlay*, not a replacement: `subprocess_cli.py:430-436` builds `inherited_env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k != "CLAUDECODE"}` and then `process_env = {**inherited_env, "CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT": …, **self._options.env, …}`, so every `claude` subprocess — and through it every Bash call in the 72 skills — inherits the runner's full `os.environ` (PATH, the plist's exports, `bash -lc` profile leakage, and anything a future patch exports, `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK` and the kill switches included). Isolation therefore rests on **keeping the runner's `os.environ` free of secrets**, not on `env=`: (1) vendor keys are read from `.env` by `Settings` in-process only (pydantic-settings does not export) and are never in `os.environ` or the plist; (2) a fail-closed startup assertion beside `_check_subscription_auth` (P0) refuses to start the runner when `os.environ` contains any key matching `GEMINI_*|CEREBRAS_*|GROQ_*|CODEX_*|OPENROUTER_*|OPENAI_*|XAI_*|*_API_KEY|*_TOKEN` or any of the four Anthropic credential names **`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`** — **named, not a blanket `ANTHROPIC_*` prefix** (round 3: a blanket prefix would refuse to boot the moment §12a row 13b puts `ANTHROPIC_MAX_SESSIONS=2` in the launchd plist at P2, taking the whole fleet down on a deploy; the P0 plan's narrower `VENDOR_KEY_PREFIXES` is the correct shape). The exact name list is a D13 pin-bump checklist item so the spec and the implementation cannot diverge again, and the P2 knob is read from `.env` by `Settings` — §2.8 states it as `ANTHROPIC_MAX_SESSIONS` and §12a row 13b as a plist edit, which the named list makes safe either way; (3) `env=` carries only `DISABLE_ERROR_REPORTING=1` and `DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` (`claude-anthropic.md §3` [68]; error reports and operational metrics are on by default for Pro/Max sign-ins) and, for a job the owner explicitly resumed during a login-expiry fallback (§3), `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` — the overlay wins for that key, so the sealed-token injection works; (4) the P4 env-scrub test observes the **actual child env**, not the dict the executor constructs: a `printenv` canary job whose `tool_result` is asserted to contain none of the names above; (5) "SDK `env=` semantics" joins the D13 pin-bump checklist. **Project-scope settings are a settings channel, not only an auth channel (P0; round 3 critical).** `session.py:716` passes `setting_sources=["project"]`, so for every clone *and for the ~48 `isolation: none` skills whose cwd is the server root itself* the files `<cwd>/.claude/settings.json` and `<cwd>/.claude/settings.local.json` are loaded as project scope. **Both of those files are tracked in this repo** (`git ls-files .claude/` → `settings.json` carrying `enabledPlugins`, `settings.local.json` carrying `permissions`), they are on no protected-path list (MISSION §M 1-8), in no lint rule, and the dev pre-commit hook only fires on `^src/` — so an INV-4 patch can edit them with no friction. A settings file carries far more than auth: `hooks` (arbitrary commands at SessionStart/PreToolUse), `permissions.allow`, `mcpServers`/`enableAllProjectMcpServers`, `apiKeyHelper` and `env` — i.e. arbitrary owner-privilege code execution on every subsequent session, bypassing `guards.py`, `lint_docs.py`, `restraints.py` and the whole §2.5 belt architecture in one commit. `apiKeyHelper`, `env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `env.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` additionally outrank both the setup-token and `/login` (`claude-anthropic.md` line 53), and atlas is GitHub-canonical with other machines committing. So the pre-session check in `run_session` (moving into `ClaudeSdkExecutor` in P3) refuses fail-closed with **`provider_refused{reason: settings_override}`** when either file at the session's cwd contains **any of `hooks`, `permissions`, `mcpServers`, `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, `apiKeyHelper`, `env`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`** — *unless* the file's content hash matches a value pinned in the protected `src/runner/restraints.py`. Two more belts ride along: **`.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` (this repo) join the protected-path list in PR #1a** beside `src/runner/restraints.py`, and they are named in the **D16** residual list for unhooked skills (`~/.claude` is a write-protected root only for *hooked* sessions, `guards.py:69-87`, while `.context/SYSTEM.md:126` records that `isolation: none` runs with no hooks). P0 exit tests: `test_settings_auth_override` (a fixture clone with `apiKeyHelper`/`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` → refusal) and **`test_settings_no_hooks`** (the two tracked files contain only `enabledPlugins`/`permissions` and hash-match the pinned values). Listed in the C1 row of the compliance checklist; §14 Q2 asks whether the pinned CLI honours the *auth* keys from project scope, and even a verified "no" removes only the auth half — `hooks`/`permissions`/`mcpServers` are honoured regardless, so the check stays. **Audit/stream redaction (P0):** the §8.3 secret redactor runs **at the ExecEvent normaliser** (§2.1/§2.7: "normalized ExecEvent → same audit kinds"), not at a single SDK call site, so all four executors pass through it — `_handle_message` (`session.py:1138-1170`), `codex_cli.py`'s JSONL mapping (`agent_message`/`command_execution` items), `completions.py` responses and `script.py` stdout/stderr — over `tool_result` previews and `text` before the JSONL/`jobs:stream` write, with `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, every vendor-key name, the literal path `~/.config/ai-server/publish-key` and the PEM shape `-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----` in its pattern set, so a `printenv` or a `cat` of a sealed key in any session never lands a credential value in the per-job JSONL, the stream, `ai-mcp` reads or the learning extractor's input. An opt-in raw recorder (`SDK_RECORD=1`, P0) dumps every SDK message (dataclass → JSON, secrets redacted) to **`volumes/sdk_recordings/<id>.json`** — deliberately *outside* `volumes/audit_log/`, because three consumers glob that directory with `*.jsonl` (`audit_index.py:167`, `retrospective.py:214`, `:364`) and would index full-fidelity untruncated `tool_use.input`/`tool_result`/`SystemMessage` as if they were audit entries (wrong shape, double-counted events, untruncated proprietary text flowing into an Anthropic retro prompt). The directory joins the §4.1 sensitivity-gated read path and `backup.sh`'s seal list; **recordings are deleted once P3's replay fixtures are frozen, and `SDK_RECORD` is off in prod outside a recording window (P0 exit test asserts the flag is unset after the ≥20 jobs are captured)** so the P3 replay gate has real fixtures — the per-job JSONL is a lossy derived view (`tool_use.input` truncated at 2000 chars, `tool_result` 500-char preview, no `SystemMessage`/`StreamEvent`, `session.py:1138-1190`) and cannot exercise `_run_in_process`'s isinstance dispatch; ≥20 jobs across skill classes are recorded before P3.
- `codex_cli.py` (P4): `codex exec --json --ephemeral --sandbox read-only -m <pinned> --cd <copy> [--output-schema s.json -o out/out.json]`, invoked as `sudo -n -u _aicodex /usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec exec <job-copy-dir> …` — the root-owned fixed-argv wrapper of §2.6 "User separation mechanism" is the *only* way the runner reaches the dedicated unprivileged user `_aicodex`; any sudo prompt (`-n`) fails the job closed with `terminal_reason=api_error{sudo}`. `HOME` and `TMPDIR` are the stream home, never the owner's; per-job settings are passed as `codex exec -c key=value` overrides — `features.goals=false`, `otel.metrics_exporter="none"`, `analytics.enabled=false`, `web_search="cached"`, `approval_policy=never`, `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false`, **`cli_auth_credentials_store="file"`** (the reference documents `file|keyring|auto|ephemeral` and **does not state the default**, `chatgpt-openai.md:366-378`; `_aicodex` is a non-GUI service account with no unlocked login keychain, so an `auto`/`keyring` default would either fail the device-auth login or put the credential where neither the `codex:stream:<n>` lease nor the "no `auth.json` outside the stream home" assertion can see it). **These six overrides are containment, not convenience** — `features.goals=false` is the rule-(a) acceptability condition (§3, `chatgpt-openai.md §3`), `otel.metrics_exporter="none"` stops the `statsig` default (matrix §5 item 3), `network_access=false` is pinned because the research marks the default unverified — so **§14 Q11 is a P4 *entry* criterion with a behavioural assertion, not a flag-presence one**: a fixture run must prove each override *took effect* (a prompt that would trigger automatic continuation produces exactly one `turn.completed`; no outbound connection to OpenAI telemetry hosts during an exec; an outbound `curl` inside the copy fails; `auth.json` lands in the stream home). **If `-c` is not honoured, the owner-written per-stream `config.toml` is mandatory, not a fallback** (§12a row 16b), and `codex_cli.py` refuses to dispatch when the stream home has no config file whose parsed contents match the pinned set. `CODEX_HOME=/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-<n>/` (owned `_aicodex`, 0700 — **outside the checkout**: `~/Library` and `Application Support` are `drwx------` on this box, so a home under `volumes/` would be untraversable for `_aicodex` and would contradict the "`volumes/` mode-restricted" rule; `backup.sh` learns the new path, `auth.json` excluded) is **persistent per stream** (n = the lane's fan-out budget, initially 1), created once by the owner running `sudo -u _aicodex /usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec login stream-<n>` (device-auth, so `auth.json` is *born* owned by `_aicodex` 0600 and the CLI can rotate it in place) and never copied, seeded or re-seeded by the runner: Codex rotates the refresh token in place on every refresh and treats reuse of a rotated-out token as the permanent `refresh_token_reused`/`refresh_token_invalidated` error (`chatgpt-openai.md §3` lines 377-388), so two copies of one `auth.json` racing each other lock the whole lane out and repeated reuse on one Free account is the credential-sharing signature the ChatGPT Terms prohibit (`runtimes-aggregators.md §3(a)` line 148: "never re-seed an existing file"; `crosscut-tos §3.2`). **Every process that opens the stream home holds the Redis lease `codex:stream:<n>`** — `codex exec`, the app-server `account/rateLimits/read` (the pre-dispatch read is taken inside the job's lease; the post-job read completes before the lease is released; the runner-start read takes the lease itself) and the daily canary (which *skips*, not queues, when the lease is held) — because the app-server read authenticates from the same file and concurrent refreshes rotate tokens against each other (`chatgpt-openai.md §3`; `runtimes-aggregators.md §3(b)`); the P4 fixture test asserts "no two processes hold `stream-<n>` concurrently (an RPC read during a running exec is refused)". `refresh_token_reused|expired|invalidated` → `terminal_reason=auth_expired`, parks the *stream* (not the lane) and DMs the D3 re-login card; a model-not-available error (the Free plan may not serve the pinned id — `chatgpt-openai.md §1`: Free/Go get Luna in the desktop app only) → `terminal_reason=model_unavailable` and `verdicts.skip_reason=model_unavailable`, **excluded** from the `skip_reason=quota` rate that decides D5; the P4 fixture test asserts no `auth.json` is ever created outside `/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-*/` and that the three permanent errors map to `auth_expired` + stream park + owner card; SIGTERM→SIGKILL on cancel/timeout. The job copy (`/Users/Shared/ai-codex/<job8>/`, setgid `ai-codex`, 2770, with the owner in the group) is materialised **by the runner, as the owner** — `git -C <clone> archive HEAD | tar -x -C /Users/Shared/ai-codex/<job8>` followed by `chgrp -R ai-codex`, `chmod -R g+rwX` and setgid on the directories (round 3: round 2's "`git clone --shared` run *as `_aicodex`* through the wrapper's `materialise` verb" **cannot execute** — the source clone is `<server_root>/volumes/workspaces/<job>` under `~/Library/Application Support`, which is `drwx------`, so `_aicodex` cannot open `<clone>/.git`; and `--shared` would write `.git/objects/info/alternates` pointing back into that unreadable tree, so every later git read inside the copy would depend on a path the containment forbids). `git archive` gives tracked files only — a gitignored `.env` never rides along, and there is no `.git` dir inside the copy at all, so nothing leaks through alternates; `provision_env_files` is executor-aware and runs after provider resolution (§2.6). The **archive is also the diff baseline** for the `code-project` canary (or a runner-side shadow `git init` over it), because git does not work inside the copy. Outputs land in `<copy>/out/` (`umask 007` under setgid) so the runner reads `out.json`/the diff back without `chown`; `finalize_workspace` **always** removes the copy (success, quota requeue, timeout or cancel — it runs from a `finally`, §2.7), preserving only `out.json`/diff under `volumes/workspaces/<job>/` for the failure case. `ask codex` (P4) uses the same path with an empty copy dir. JSONL mapping *[graft: incremental §1h]*: `thread.started→job_started`, `agent_message→text`, `reasoning→thinking`, `command_execution→tool_use{tool_name:"Bash"}+tool_result`, `file_change→tool_use{tool_name:"Edit"}+tool_result`, `web_search→tool_use{WebSearch}`, `turn.completed.usage→job_completed.usage`, `turn.failed/error→job_failed` (`429 slow_down→rate_limited`, `503→api_error`, model-not-found→`model_unavailable`). Contract-tested on pinned-CLI fixtures.
- `completions.py`: `utility_call(task_class, system, user, schema, sensitivity) → dict|None` replacing the four copy-pasted `query()` loops (`llm_router.py:156-168`, `learning.py:266-278`, `review.py:258-278`, `evals/run.py:74-100`; the reviewer keeps its 6-turn agent form, pinned). Runs **in-process over HTTP** (Ollama native `/api/chat` with `format`, Gemini `generateContent`, Anthropic utility via SDK `query()` as the terminal link) so routing/learning stop spawning two ~148 MB `claude` subprocesses per job *[graft: kernel §4]*. Schema-validate-and-retry once; fail-open only where the caller is fail-open today. Every prompt bound for a non-Anthropic HTTP lane passes the §8.3 secret/identifier redactor first and audits `utility_call{provider, purpose, redacted: true, patterns_hit: n}`; the P3 "zero owner-private prompts on free lanes" gate asserts it. Transcript-derived text (the learning extractor's input: final summary, Bash command text, assistant text, thinking excerpts — `learning.py:138-170`, routinely carrying `curl -H 'Authorization: …'` lines) is class `utility-learning` and never leaves `ollama-local`/`anthropic-utility` (§2.5).
- `script.py`: implements `no_llm: true` + `command:`. It is not weaker than a Claude session: every command is pre-checked with `guards.bash_violation()` and executed under `sandbox-exec` with a workspace-write / **no-network profile, without exception** — Seatbelt allows `network-outbound` by address/port, not hostname, so a "host-allowlisted" exception cannot be expressed in the profile; the `url_200|healthcheck_green` checks are therefore performed **in the runner process** (`httpx` against a hardcoded host allowlist in `src/contracts/`), never by a `ScriptExecutor` command; **the profile is read-confining, not write-confining only** (round 3: `ScriptExecutor` runs as the owner with no user separation, so the spec's own Seatbelt insight — `read-only`/`workspace-write` confines writes and network, *not reads* — applies here exactly as it does to Codex): `scripts/sandbox/script.sb` opens with `(deny file-read*)` and allows reads only under the clone, the project venv, `/usr/lib`, `/System` and the interpreter's own prefix, and the committed test asserts that **reads** of `$HOME/.claude`, `$HOME/.ssh`, `~/.config/ai-server/publish-key`, the server `.env` and `projects/atlas/.env` are **denied**, alongside the existing write-outside-the-clone and all-network cases. (If read confinement cannot be expressed for the project venv on this box, the Script lane is rated **C on reads** until it also runs as a dedicated unprivileged user, and the `no_llm` set is gated accordingly — stated rather than assumed.) The lint rule (protected) requires a `no_llm` command to resolve to a tracked file under `scripts/` or `evals/` with no shell metacharacters, and a new `no_llm` skill joins the frozen unisolated-writer allowlist only by owner PR — **but the lint rule bounds the command *string*, and the code it resolves to is INV-4-editable (`scripts/**` and `evals/**` are not protected paths), so containment here is the sandbox profile, not the lint rule.** Round 1's claim that "an autonomous server-patch cannot add scheduled arbitrary command execution at the runner's privilege" was therefore false as written and is retracted. Used for evidence checks (filesystem and transcript kinds), credential canaries and adherence — **not for anything that touches Postgres or Redis**: both are localhost *TCP* (`POSTGRES_DSN=…@localhost:5432`, `REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/0`) and a Seatbelt profile with network denied blocks loopback too, so `db_row`, the retro SQL pre-pass and the deploy autopilot's `enqueue_job` run **in the runner process** (a read-only DSN held by the runner; `gateway.jobs.enqueue_job` for the autopilot) — the same precedent as `url_200` *[graft: kernel §1.2/§5.4]*. `tests_green`/`lint_green` over a clone written by a non-Anthropic executor never run here (attacker-influenced `conftest.py`/`package.json` scripts would escape the writer's Seatbelt): they run inside that executor's own sandbox (`codex exec --sandbox workspace-write` with the test command) or degrade to `tests_ran` (§5.1).

### 2.5 Registry and routing policy (P3) *[plan §6a/§6d]*

`providers.yml` (anthropic `sdk-anthropic` trust anchor, `cost_class: included`, `tier: max-5x` (MISSION §K), `max_sessions: {opus: 2, sonnet: 2, all: 2}` (per family — the Opus bar is the one that hits first) **plus `gate_sessions: 1`, a separate budget for post-step Anthropic sessions (belt-3b merge gate, post-review, `anthropic-utility` terminal link) so a gate never contends with job sessions and a cap-starved gate is a retryable skip, not a fail-closed failure — §5.3 belt 3b**, `overflow_credits: disabled` (D3 confirms usage credits $0 / auto-reload off / spend limit $0); **anthropic-utility `cost_class: included`** (it is the terminal link of four chains and was missing from this list, which lint requires every chain member to appear in); codex `cli-agent` probation, **`cost_class: free-tier`** under D0-A (`plan-flat` under D5(b) Plus, `metered` under D5(c)), `data: {trains: true, max_sensitivity: public}` (raised to `owner-private` by `policy.select()` only while an unexpired `approvals(kind=attestation, subject=codex)` row exists — lint-hardcoded floor), `overflow_credits: disabled` (no Codex credits purchased; the RPC's `credits`/`spendControlReached` are read on every call and a non-zero balance refuses dispatch unless `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK` lists `codex`), `models: {grader: <exact id verified on Free in stream-0 — NOT `gpt-6-luna` unless the P4 entry check shows that id in `turn.completed`>}`; gemini-free `openai-compat` `data:{trains:true, max_sensitivity:public}` `caps:{rpd:250}`; cerebras-free `openai-compat` `data:{trains:false, retains:false, max_sensitivity:owner-private}` `caps:{tokens_per_day:1000000, rpm:5, json_schema:true}` `cost_class: free-tier` **or `trial` with `expires_at` per D18**; groq-free `openai-compat` `cost_class: free-tier` `data:{trains:false, max_sensitivity:owner-private}` `caps:{tokens_per_day_per_model:200000, tpm:8000, json_schema:true}`; gemini-free `cost_class: free-tier`; ollama-local `cost_class: included` `guard:{min_free_gb:3}`; openrouter-free `cost_class: free-tier`, `trust: probation`, no chain; deepinfra `cost_class: metered`, declined and lint/runtime-refused; **`cost_class ∈ {included, free-tier, trial, plan-flat, credit-bundled, metered}` is mandatory on every entry** (plan §6a) — round 3 added `plan-flat` (a second flat-rate subscription such as ChatGPT Plus: not included in an existing seat, not free, not a trial, not per-token) and `credit-bundled` (a flat subscription carrying a bundled credit pool — Ollama Cloud Pro, Mistral Pro; treated like `metered` for the chain-termination rule but eligible for `owner_approved_metered` **without** a metered-spend exception, since the research classes it as a subscription that fits owner policy, `crosscut-tos §3.7` lines 129/134). `plan-flat` and `credit-bundled` entries still need an `approvals(kind=provider_promote, decided_via=telegram)` id and a `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK` plist row before an executor will dispatch to them) feeds `grammar.py`, the PWA picker, `VALID_MODELS` (`tests/test_skill_contracts.py:24`), `_MODEL_BUDGETS` (`session.py:466-471`) and escalation targets (`main.py:759-760`). Bare ids default to `anthropic/` (C17; 72 skills unchanged).

```yaml
# routing-policy.yml (initial). This file is INV-4-lane data. Pins, the pairing rule, grader fallback,
# the data-terms floors, the metered ban and the publish path allowlist are RESTRAINTS whose source of
# truth is the protected scripts/lint_docs.py (hardcoded set, PR #1a at P3 entry) — two belts: the
# protected executor skills run lint_docs.py as a named gate step, and src/runner/restraints.py (protected,
# D2) refuses to start the runner when this file disagrees (§2.5 "Restraints are not data").
classes:
  utility-classify: {chain: [ollama-local, cerebras-free, groq-free, anthropic-utility], fail_open: true, max_sensitivity: owner-private}   # owner-authored text only; never gemini-free/openrouter; cerebras/groq pending D18; proprietary text skips them
  utility-learning: {chain: [ollama-local, anthropic-utility], fail_open: true, max_sensitivity: proprietary}   # transcript-derived text (learning extractor); never a C-rated HTTP host
  utility-public:   {chain: [ollama-local, gemini-free, anthropic-utility], fail_open: true, max_sensitivity: public}  # pre-fetched public docs only
  chat:             {chain: [anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6@low], pinned: true}          # Sonnet 5 low after D13 (matrix §1 item 8)
  # A canary is an ATTRIBUTE, never a chain position (round 3): the hardcoded rule "the last element of every chain
  # and escalation list is cost_class: included or ollama-local" would otherwise fail the moment P4 adds `codex`,
  # and restraints.py would refuse to start the runner. canary/grader/critic lists are exempt from the terminator rule.
  research-read:    {chain: [anthropic], canary: {provider: codex, share: 0.10, from: P4}, grader: [codex#redacted, gemini-free#public, ollama-local#mechanical]}   # D0 as corrected: Codex canary WRITER on Free plan auth from P4 (owner-only memos, belt 3b + publish.py); graders inert until D21
  code-project:     {chain: [anthropic], canary: {provider: codex, share: 0.10, from: P5-exit}, grader: none, gate: anthropic}   # grader: [codex] only after D21 AND 30 shadow grades; writer canary at P5 exit (D15)
  code-server:      {chain: [anthropic], pinned: true, grader: none}                  # plan §7 verbatim: "GPT reviewing anything is not [in-plan]"
  review-gate:      {chain: [anthropic], pinned: true}
  _evaluate:        {chain: [anthropic], pinned: true}
  trading-research: {chain: [anthropic], pinned: true, critic: [codex#redacted, ollama-local#mechanical], default_sensitivity: proprietary}   # critic after D21 + D4 attestation
pairing_rule: grader.provider != writer.provider        # enforced in grading.py, not in a prompt
grader_fallback: skipped                                # never anthropic (quota asymmetry)
grader_sampling: {trading-research: all, research-read: 0.10, code-project: 0.10, per_5h_cap: 6}   # §5.3 belt 4; over OUR cap → skipped(sampling_cap), vendor throttle → skipped(quota), unsampled → skipped(sampled).
                                                                                                 # The three are distinct because D5 (spend money?) is decided on skip_reason=quota alone.
owner_approved_metered: []                              # an id here needs an approvals(kind=provider_promote, decided_via=telegram) reference
publish_allowlist:   # hardcoded in lint_docs.py and enforced in publish.py for every non-Anthropic writer (§2.6; the INV-22 belt for non-Anthropic writers, §8.2)
  # MATCHING RULE (round 3 — it decided whether the belt holds at all, and was unstated): paths are repo-root-relative
  # POSIX globs; `**` crosses separators; DENY IS EVALUATED FIRST AND WINS over any allow; a diff is refused unless
  # EVERY changed path matches an allow pattern AND no deny pattern; a rename counts as both paths.
  # The verticals' own research dirs are named explicitly, so `swing/research/**` is NOT writable by an any-depth read.
  atlas: {allow: ["research/**", "docs/**", "alpha-lab/research/**", "quant/research/**", "*/reports/**"], deny: ["swing/**", "tradingcore/**", "**/risk*", "*/limits.yaml", "*/budget.yaml", ".github/**", ".claude/**", "integrations/ai-server/**"]}
lanes:   # weekly budgets in cost_usd_list (list-equivalent $) over rolling 7-d provider_ledger sums, per Anthropic model family — raw tokens
         # cannot budget a window that weights Opus cache-writes 33× a Sonnet cache-read. seed = trailing-7-d usage × 1.2.
         # WHERE P2 READS THEM (round 3 — this file is a P3 deliverable and P2 enforces the budget): in P2 the per-lane,
         # per-family budgets are read from `Settings` (`LANE_WEEKLY_BUDGET_JSON`), seeded by hand from the P0 reconcile
         # script's printed table — exactly the pattern round 2 chose for ANTHROPIC_MAX_SESSIONS; `routing-policy.yml:
         # lanes.<lane>.weekly_budget` takes over in P3, and automatic weekly re-seeding begins then, not in P0.
         # Never seeded from the trailing-30-d mean: the alpha flywheel (2026-09-23) tripled daily load after it.
  owner:      {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}, reserved: true}   # the owner lane is never held for budget
  kernel:     {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}}
  atlas:      {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}, governed: true}   # ≈60 % of fleet on the 30-d mean, ≈75 % since the flywheel; a hold is a LOOP.md §7 proposal, never automatic
  alpha:      {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}, governed: true}   # round 3: the flywheel is ≈58 % of week 39 and had NO lane — alpha-governor matched "event-trigger → kernel" and alpha-research (dispatch-mcp) matched nothing, so the dominant load was invisible to the budget and an alpha exhaustion would have held the kernel ops lane
  build:      {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}}                   # round 3: phase PRs (purpose=build) so the §9 cap can actually bind — the owner lane it used to land in is exempt from every hold
  utility:    {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}}
  background: {weekly_budget: {opus: seed, sonnet: seed}}
presets:
  programmatic_credit_mode: {pool_usd_list: 100, funded_first: [owner, kernel], atlas: proposal}   # flipped by ANTHROPIC_CREDIT_MODE=1 if the paused Agent-SDK credit split returns (§11)
infer:
  - {when: {subagents: nonempty}, require_caps: [subagents]}
  - {when: {tags: [needs-dispatch-mcp, needs-projects-mcp]}, require_caps: [mcp]}
  - {when: {isolation: none}, require_caps: [guard-hooks]}   # 48 unisolated skills stay on Anthropic
sensitivity_default: {telegram: owner-private, transcript: owner-private, scheduled: skill.sensitivity, atlas: proprietary, alpha: proprietary}
# ^ THIS MAP IS A RESTRAINT, NOT DATA (round 3): every per-provider data floor is enforced against a *label*, so a yml
# patch that relabelled `telegram: public` and added gemini-free to utility-classify would send owner text to a
# trains+human-review lane with every hardcoded floor still holding. The map and every class's `max_sensitivity:`
# are therefore in restraints.py's fixed set (§2.5 "Restraints are not data").
# effective sensitivity = max(source job's class, text-class default): an atlas job's transcript is proprietary, not owner-private;
# transcript-derived text also carries purpose=learning, which maps to utility-learning above.
```

Skill frontmatter extension (backward-compatible; `SkillConfig.model` stays a raw string with derived `provider`/`model_id` so `test_agents.py:33-56` and `test_registry_failclosed.py:58-61` keep passing; 72 skills need no edit):

```yaml
# additive fields on skills/<name>/SKILL.md (all optional)
model: anthropic/claude-opus-5          # bare id → anthropic/; unknown provider fails the job (C12)
task_class: research-read               # inferred from isolation + tags when absent (plan §6e)
providers: {allow: [anthropic], critic: [codex, ollama-local]}   # writer chain and grader/critic lanes; deny: [...] also accepted
sensitivity: proprietary                # public | owner-private | proprietary; atlas/alpha default proprietary
done:                                   # §5.1; absent → the default contract runs: pass ⇒ done_verdict=unverified, fail ⇒ failed_checks; verified needs a declared contract
  deliverables: [{file: "projects/research/*-{{date}}.md"}, {commit: true}]
  checks: [{name: citations_min, min: 3}, {name: sections_present, sections: [Thesis, Evidence, Risks]}, {name: git_pushed}]
  on_fail: flag                         # flag (default) | fail (deploy-verify only) | fix-round
no_llm: true                            # with command: → ScriptExecutor
command: "python -m evals.run --provider {{provider}}"
escalation:
  on_failure: [{provider: anthropic, model: claude-opus-4-7, effort: high}]   # list form; the bare-map form of 31 skills still accepted
```

**Restraints are not data** (§M: "the system must not be able to relax its own restraints"). `routing-policy.yml`, `providers.yml`, skill frontmatter, env switches **and the repo's own project-scope settings files `.claude/settings.json` / `.claude/settings.local.json`** (tracked, loaded by `setting_sources=["project"]` for every clone and for the ~48 `isolation: none` skills whose cwd is the server root — §2.4) are all editable on the INV-4 lane (agent LGTM, no owner), so none of them is the source of truth for a restraint. The protected `scripts/lint_docs.py` — **owner PR #1a, approved at P3 *entry***, not P4 (D2 is split: round 1's "lint at P4" left the whole of P3 — the phase that ships the yml, the pinned classes and `policy.select()` — with only `tests/test_registry.py` as enforcement, a file under `tests/` an INV-4 patch may edit alongside the yml) — hardcodes and enforces: **the pinned class set `{code-server, review-gate, _evaluate, chat, trading-research}` ⇒ `anthropic/` only** (five, written identically here, in §1, §2.2 and §6 — round 3: §6 already listed `trading-research` while this set and the parse-time refusal listed four, so a yml patch could have dropped `pinned: true` from the class that produces trading memos and added `codex#canary` to it with nothing protected disagreeing), **and the rule that a `critic:`/`grader:` list on a pinned class may contain only providers with `data.trains=false` — or `data.trains=true` plus an unexpired `approvals(kind=attestation, subject=<provider>)` row (the `codex#redacted` case on `trading-research`, D4) — plus `ollama-local`**; `pairing_rule: grader.provider != writer.provider`; `grader_fallback: skipped`; **`sensitivity_default` (`telegram: owner-private`, `transcript: owner-private`, `atlas: proprietary`, `alpha: proprietary`) and every class's `max_sensitivity:` as fixed values** (`utility-classify ≤ owner-private`, `utility-learning ≤ proprietary` with the chain restricted to `{ollama-local, anthropic-utility}`, `utility-public ≤ public`) — the per-provider floors are enforced against a label, so the label map has to be a restraint or the floors are theatre (§11's claim that thesis/owner-text routing is "refused, not merely discouraged" depends on this);
