**the rule that every render of `verdicts.findings[].text`, `grade_done`, critic text and `ask` answers into an Anthropic prompt goes through `untrusted_envelope()` — which lives in the protected `src/runner/restraints.py`, not in `src/runner/grading.py`** (round 3: an INV-4-editable helper asserted only by a test under `tests/` is the exact combination round 1 finding #1 rejected for the pins); the per-provider data-terms floor (`gemini-free: trains=true, max_sensitivity=public`; `codex: trains=true, max_sensitivity=public` unless an unexpired attestation row exists; `openrouter-free` in no chain; `cerebras-free`/`groq-free` ≤ `owner-private`; `utility-learning` ⊆ {ollama-local, anthropic-utility}); `cost_class` mandatory and no `cost_class: metered` provider in any `chain`/`grader`/`critic`/`escalation` list or `enabled: true` unless listed under `owner_approved_metered:` with an `approvals(kind=provider_promote, decided_via=telegram)` id; **the last element of every `chain` and `escalation` list is `cost_class: included` or `ollama-local`** (plan §10.3: every chain terminates in an included-cost provider; **`canary:`, `grader:` and `critic:` are exempt from the terminator rule** — `grader`/`critic` lists may end in `skipped`, and a canary is an attribute rather than a chain position precisely so that adding the P4 Codex writer cannot make the runner refuse to boot); the `publish_allowlist` per target (above); `division: atlas|alpha` skills may not declare `sensitivity` below `proprietary`; non-Anthropic provider ⇒ `isolation: workspace`; provider and every check name must exist in the registries; `sensitivity: proprietary` ⇒ critic lanes with `data.trains=false` and an unexpired attestation only; a `governed` schedule row's kind must be an atlas-charter skill; `no_llm` commands resolve to a tracked file under `scripts/` or `evals/` with no shell metacharacters; the sudoers wrapper path `/usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec` is in `guards.py`'s `_HARD_DENY_PATTERNS` (PR #1b). **Two belts, because lint alone was not one:** `skills/server-deploy/SKILL.md` gates on `pipenv run pytest -q` (`:40, :133`) and never invokes `lint_docs.py`, and `server-patch/SKILL.md` names it as a file (`:68`) but not as a gate step — the lint checks reach pytest only through `tests/test_doc_lint.py`, which an INV-4 patch may skip or delete. So (belt 1) PR #1a also adds `pipenv run python scripts/lint_docs.py` as a **named gate step** in both executor skills (protected path #8, same owner PR), and (belt 2) `src/runner/restraints.py` — a new file D2 adds to the MISSION §M protected list, **together with `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`** (§2.4) — holds the same hardcoded set, is imported by `policy.py` and by `lint_docs.py`, and **refuses to start the runner** when `routing-policy.yml`/`providers.yml` disagree with it (runtime fail-closed, `test_restraints_startup`).

**What those two belts do and do not cover — stated honestly (round 3 critical).** A patch that edits **only the yml** is caught by the lint gate step and by `restraints.py` at startup: it cannot un-pin a class, flip `gemini-free` or `codex` to `trains: false`, enable DeepInfra, relabel `sensitivity_default`, widen the publish allowlist or change the fallback. A patch that edits **`restraints.py` or `lint_docs.py` themselves alongside the yml** passes both belts and boots — because protected-path status has **no mechanical enforcement in the dev repo where INV-4 patches are born**: the only check today is the patching agent comparing its own diff against a list printed in its own SKILL.md (`skills/server-patch/SKILL.md:57-73, 152-154`), plus the owner notification after the merge; the dev `.git/hooks/pre-commit` enforces CHANGELOG only, and `scripts/install-prod-hooks.sh` blocks commits on the *production* checkout, not dev pushes (state map C6 rates this "convention + partial code"). **So PR #1a also makes the gate mechanical, and wires the `approvals(kind=protected_path)` row migration 008 already defines and nothing else ever produced or consumed:** a dev-side `scripts/install-dev-hooks.sh` pre-commit guard (mirroring `install-prod-hooks.sh`) refuses any commit whose staged diff touches a path on the §M list unless the commit message carries `Approved-Protected-Path: ap-<id>` resolving to an approved `approvals(kind=protected_path, decided_via=telegram)` row with its bot-only artefact (§0a row 10). The installer **and its path list** join the protected set, the hook is re-armed on every deploy exactly as the prod hooks are (`server-deploy/SKILL.md`, the same owner PR), and **`test_protected_paths_hook` is a P0 test gate** — the script and the test are ordinary unprotected files, so they ship in P0; PR #1a at P3 entry is what adds them to the MISSION §M list and to the deploy re-arm. Runtime belts that are **not env-readable**: `if provider != "anthropic": pre_publish_gate = True` unconditionally; metered providers are refused unless `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK=<id,…>` is present in the launchd env (auth config, owner-only, C2 — the plist edit is a §12a owner row). Capability matching keeps the 20 subagent skills and 16 dispatch-MCP skills on Anthropic with no special-case code.

**`policy.select()` is the only provider chooser — one override rule, stated once.** Precedence: `payload.provider/model` > `policy.select()` > `skill.model`, where `payload.provider/model` is honoured under exactly two regimes: **(i) owner override** — an owner-originated launch (`origin_channel ∈ {telegram, pwa, cli}`) may name any *registered, non-metered* provider that has an executor for the class's capability set, on a non-pinned class, **irrespective of chain membership or ladder state** (so `--model=codex/<id>` on `research-read` is honoured from P4 and on `code-project` from P5 exit; refused `no_executor` before the executor ships, `pinned` on the **five** pinned classes, `metered` unless under `owner_approved_metered`); sensitivity, attestation, `max_sessions`, the path allowlist and the belt-3b gate still apply; audited `provider_selected{reason: owner_override}`; bypasses the `canary_share` cap but never the pairing rule; does not count toward the ladder. **Until D10 lands, an owner override is honoured only for `origin_channel == telegram` or a request carrying a recorded Cloudflare Access `aud` claim** — the same rule §4.5 applies to *decisions*, because this regime **is** an authority widening (it honours any registered non-metered provider irrespective of chain membership or ladder state and bypasses `canary_share`), and pre-D10 the PWA/CLI bearer is one static token on a public hostname; a `pwa`/`cli` launch naming a non-default provider is refused `needs_cf_access` and falls back to `policy.select()` (`test_intents` case). This is why §4.6's "launch/cancel/retry/lane-hold cannot widen authority" is stated with `--model=`/`--second-opinion=` carved out; **(ii) chain-only** — `payload.model` on a scheduled row and dispatch-MCP children may pick a model only from the resolved class chain, and a provider outside the chain fails at spec build with `provider_refused{reason: not_in_chain}`. Any non-Anthropic provider on a pinned class fails for every origin with `provider_refused{reason: pinned}`; unknown ids with `no_executor`; metered with `metered` (fail-closed, C12; today `session.py:699-700` applies `payload["model"]` unchecked). `test_registry`: "owner override to codex on `research-read` yields `provider_selected{reason: owner_override}`, not `provider_refused`; the same on a scheduled row yields `not_in_chain` until the lane is in the chain". `mcp_dispatch._sanitize_payload` strips `provider`/`model`/`effort` unless inside the child's chain and always strips `approval_id` (today `mcp_dispatch.py:38` strips only `isolation`/`permission_mode`/`permission`). Both rules are in `test_registry` and the P3 exit criteria. Unknown provider/model fails the job at spec build (C12), never falls back. `routing_decision` confidence is thresholded (<0.6 → generic workspace task; today discarded at `session.py:616`); `--kind` corrections are recorded as `routing_correction{from, to}` events and appended to `evals/cases/_router.yml` (the labelled router dataset, §9 P1). Escalation becomes a chain of `{provider, model, effort}` with error-class gating: `rate_limited`/`auth_expired`/`api_error` never spawn `self-diagnose` (21 % of all jobs today, `main.py:704-853`).

### 2.6 Containment equivalents (INV-17/20/21/22) *[kernel §1.6 matrix; multi-bot §1.6 text-only]*

| Executor | Tier | Filesystem | Network | Secrets | Tool veto | Review of diffs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude SDK | none (allowlist), workspace, host (god) | clone or shared | via SDK | the subprocess inherits the runner's `os.environ` (SDK 0.1.81 `env=` is an overlay, §2.4), so the belt is a **clean runner environment** — fail-closed startup assertion that no vendor key and none of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`/`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`/`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is exported (named, not a blanket `ANTHROPIC_*` prefix — §2.4); Keychain credential; `env=` overlay carries only `DISABLE_ERROR_REPORTING=1`/`DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` (+ the sealed token for owner-resumed fallback jobs only); `<cwd>/.claude/settings*.json` **settings-override** check (`hooks`/`permissions`/`mcpServers`/`apiKeyHelper`/`env`/`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, hash-pinned in `restraints.py` — §2.4); `env_files` per manifest, scoped per vertical (P5); audit/stream redactor at the ExecEvent normaliser | PreToolUse hooks unchanged (`guards.py:381-490`, attached `session.py:753/755`) | in-session `code-review` (merge gate) + post-review (post-push, flag-only — D8) |
| Codex CLI | **workspace only**; `read-only` sandbox for grader/critic and for the `research-read` canary (the artefact is the `--output-schema` text; `publish.py` writes it); `workspace-write` only for the `code-project` canary (P5 exit). Rated **A− (writes/network) / C (reads)** until the dedicated-user separation below ships — a P4 exit criterion, not prose — because Seatbelt `read-only` confines writes and network, **not filesystem reads** (`chatgpt-openai.md §3`: "no network access and write permissions limited to the active workspace"; `crosscut-tos §3.8a`: "read files, edit within the workspace"); `protected_roots()` is a runner-side predicate only the Claude hook consults | a **tracked-files-only materialisation** of the per-job clone, **run by the runner as the owner**: `git -C <clone> archive HEAD \| tar -x -C /Users/Shared/ai-codex/<job8>`, then `chgrp -R ai-codex` + `chmod -R g+rwX` + setgid on the directories (round 3: the wrapper's `materialise` verb is **deleted** — a `git clone --shared` run *as `_aicodex`* cannot open `<clone>/.git`, since the clone sits under `~/Library/Application Support`, which is `drwx------`, i.e. the same unreadability this cell asserts two clauses later; and `--shared` would leave `.git/objects/info/alternates` pointing into that forbidden tree). `git archive` copies tracked files only and no `.git` at all: a gitignored `.env` that `provision_env_files` put in the source clone is absent, nothing leaks through alternates, and `provision_env_files` itself becomes executor-aware and runs after provider resolution so it never writes into a clone bound for a non-Anthropic executor. Plus the redacted projection + `AGENTS.md`; the copy is setgid `ai-codex` 2770 (owner in the group) so the runner reads `out/` back; the archive (or a runner-side shadow `git init` over it) is the diff baseline, since git does not work inside the copy; **removed by `finalize_workspace` on every outcome, from a `finally`** (§2.7); the server root, every `.env` (incl. `projects/atlas/.env`, where `TRADIER_SANDBOX_TOKEN` lives per MISSION §M), `projects/`, `volumes/`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, `~/.cloudflared`, `~/.ssh` are unreadable to that user because `~/Library` and the checkout's parents are already `drwx------` (verified on this box) and the checkout itself gets `o-rwx`; `HOME`/`TMPDIR` = the stream home `/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-<n>/` (owned `_aicodex`, 0700, **outside the checkout** — round 1's `volumes/codex/` location was untraversable for `_aicodex` and contradicted this very cell) | off (`sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false` pinned per job — the research marks the default unverified; `crosscut-tos §3.8a`) | `EnvScrubber` default-deny: PATH/HOME/CODEX_HOME/LANG/TMPDIR only; no `env_files`, no git credentials (the runner's `publish.py` pushes after the gate); `auth.json` exists only in `/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-*/`, born owned by `_aicodex` from a device-auth login run through the wrapper | OS sandbox + user separation (sudoers wrapper, below); no MCP; capability matching refuses `delegate/mcp/ask` | merge gate = the `code-review` skill protocol run in-process over the clone diff **before publish**, fail-closed (§5.3 belt 3b) — `pre_publish_gate` is unconditional in code for non-Anthropic lanes; then the hardcoded `publish_allowlist` (§2.5); `stale_head → flagged` |
| Completions (HTTP) | n/a (in-process) | none | one vendor HTTPS | one key | **no tools by construction** ("nothing to sandbox") | n/a |
| Script | workspace | clone — **reads confined by the profile and tested** (`(deny file-read*)` + an explicit allow list; the committed test asserts reads of `$HOME/.claude`, `$HOME/.ssh`, `~/.config/ai-server/publish-key`, the server `.env` and `projects/atlas/.env` are denied — round 3: without read confinement this lane is rated **C on reads**, the same rating the spec refuses to accept for Codex, and it runs as the owner with no user separation) | **off, always, including loopback** (`url_200`/`healthcheck_green` **and everything touching Postgres/Redis** — `db_row`, the retro SQL pre-pass, the autopilot enqueue — run in the runner process; Seatbelt cannot express a hostname allowlist and denies localhost TCP, §2.4) | scrubbed; **stdout/stderr pass through the §8.3 redactor at the ExecEvent normaliser** (§2.4 — the redactor is not an SDK-path-only belt) | `guards.bash_violation()` pre-check (a command-string pre-check only) + `sandbox-exec` read-confining/workspace-write/no-network profile (`scripts/sandbox/script.sb`, tested); declared, lint-checked command **string** — the code it resolves to is INV-4-editable, so the profile is the containment, not the lint rule | n/a |

**User separation mechanism (the part round 1 hand-waved).** The runner is a per-user LaunchAgent running as the owner (`scripts/install-launchd.sh:27-45,80-100`; live label `gui/<uid>/com.assistant.runner`); an unprivileged process cannot change uid, `sudo -n true` on this box answers "a password is required", and a non-root process cannot `chown` a copy to another user. The cited `openai/codex-action` `unprivileged-user` strategy (`chatgpt-openai.md:559-561`) is a Linux GitHub-Actions strategy that "requires an API key" and describes no macOS mechanism. The mechanism is therefore: `/etc/sudoers.d/ai-codex` grants the runner user exactly `<owner> ALL=(_aicodex) NOPASSWD: /usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec` — a **root-owned, fixed-argv wrapper** (0755, not writable by the owner) whose verbs are `exec <job-copy-dir>` (execs `codex exec` with the pinned flags and the copy dir as its sole variable argument), `rpc <stream>` (`account/rateLimits/read`), `canary <stream>` and `login <stream>` (device-auth; owner-run once per stream) — never a shell, never `cat`, never an arbitrary path — so no skill Bash can read the stream home through it. **`materialise` is gone** (round 3): the job copy is produced runner-side with `git archive` because the source clone is unreadable to `_aicodex` (§2.4, Codex row above). **The wrapper constrains its own arguments, not just its verbs:** `exec` refuses any path that does not `realpath` to `^/Users/Shared/ai-codex/[0-9a-f]{8}$`, contains a symlink component, or is world-writable, and `rpc`/`canary`/`login` take a stream index, never a path. The executor always invokes it as `sudo -n -u _aicodex …` and fails closed on any prompt. The sudoers drop-in + wrapper are **host auth config (protected path #2)**, installed by the owner (§12a row 15b) and counted in §11. **Residual, stated at its real size (round 3 — round 2's "quota spend only" understated it):** `guards.py` hard-denies `sudo` only where hooks attach (`guards.py:138`), so any of the 48 unhooked `isolation: none` skills could invoke `sudo -u _aicodex /usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec exec /Users/Shared/ai-codex/<8 hex>` — and because the runner and every session run **as the same user**, no mechanism inside the wrapper can distinguish a directory the runner staged from one an unhooked session staged there. So the residual is not only ChatGPT quota spend: such a session could put text of its choosing in front of a plan-auth, trains-until-deleted vendor lane with no sensitivity check, no §8.3 redaction, no D4 attestation and no ledger row. It still cannot read `auth.json` (the wrapper has no read verb) and the text it can send is text it already holds. Mitigations: the path-prefix/symlink/mode checks above (so `/tmp/<planted-dir>` is refused and audited — a §8.2(b) tripwire case), the wrapper path in `_HARD_DENY_PATTERNS` (PR #1b), the §8.2(b) assertion that `sudo -u _aicodex cat <canary>` fails for each planted path, and **D16 (isolation flip + allowlist shrink) as the only real fix** — this residual is one of D16's named reasons. `sudo pmset autorestart 1` (§12a row 5) is unrelated and one-off.

`ExecSpec` → executor adapter contract *[graft: kernel §1.8]*:

| `ExecSpec` field | `claude_sdk.py` | `codex_cli.py` | `completions.py` | `script.py` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cwd` | `cwd=` (delivery-resolved, `session.py:819-858`) | `--cd <clone>` (workspace only) | n/a | subprocess cwd = clone |
| `system_prompt` (directive + job id + markers + transcript + skill body + context_files) | `system_prompt=` as today (`session.py:664-692`) | written to `AGENTS.md` in the clone (32 KiB cap, `chatgpt-openai.md §2`) + prepended to the prompt | `system` message | ignored |
| `capabilities` | `allowed_tools` via a `Capability → tool name` table; the 9-tool default (`session.py:706-709`) minus `AskUserQuestion` becomes the default set | `--sandbox read-only` unless `write/edit/shell` requested (`code-project` canary, P5 exit only); refuse `delegate/mcp/ask` | refuse anything but `structured_output` | refuse anything but `shell` |
| `guard_profile` | `hooks=` via today's `make_guard_hooks`/`make_readonly_guard_hooks` (`guards.py:381, 419`) | OS sandbox + `EnvScrubber` | n/a | `EnvScrubber` |
| `provider`, `model`, `effort` | `model=`, `effort=` passthrough (`agents.py:38-54`) | `-m <id>` pinned (never the picker default; the rate-limit prompt silently retargets Luna), `model_reasoning_effort` | Ollama tag by digest / Gemini `thinking_level` via `effort_map` | n/a |
| `max_turns`, `timeout_s` | `max_turns=`; `wait_for` → `interrupt()` then `kill()` | wall clock from the `--json` stream; SIGTERM→SIGKILL | HTTP timeout | wall clock |
| `mcp_servers`, `subagents` | in-process (`session.py:773`, `:733`) | none | none | none |
| `output_schema` | `output_format={"type":"json_schema",…}` | `--output-schema s.json -o out.json` | `format=` / `response_schema` | n/a |
| `sensitivity` | any | `proprietary` only after D4 (toggle off) and only on the redacted projection | `proprietary` ⇒ local only | any |

`EnvScrubber` contract: child env = `{PATH, HOME, LANG, TMPDIR}` (HOME/TMPDIR = the stream home, never the owner's) + `CODEX_HOME=/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-<n>` (the stream home; the job may write there only through the CLI's own token refresh) + nothing else, passed through `sudo --preserve-env=PATH,HOME,LANG,TMPDIR,CODEX_HOME` (sudoers `env_reset` strips the rest); `delivery.env_files` (`workspaces.py:192-244`) is skipped for every non-Anthropic executor **and** the source clone's `.env` is absent from the Codex copy (tracked-files-only materialisation — `test_codex_events`: "a `.env` present in the source clone is absent from the Codex copy"); `executor_started.env_keys` lists what was passed and a test asserts none of `ALPACA_*|TRADIER_*|ANTHROPIC_*|CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN|GEMINI_*|CEREBRAS_*|GROQ_*|TELEGRAM_*|DATABASE_URL` nor the runner's own deploy key (`publish.py`) appears; for the Claude subprocess the assertion runs against the **actual child env** — a `printenv` canary job whose `tool_result` is asserted (the dict the executor constructs is an overlay and proves nothing, §2.4) — for `GEMINI_*|CEREBRAS_*|GROQ_*|CODEX_*|OPENROUTER_*|*_API_KEY|CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`.

`guards.py` edits (protected; owner PR #1b in P4, D2): (i) add `GEMINI_API_KEY|OPENROUTER_API_KEY|OPENAI_API_KEY|CODEX_API_KEY|XAI_API_KEY|CEREBRAS_API_KEY|GROQ_API_KEY|CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` to the assignment/export deny beside `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`guards.py:148-149, 261-262` guard the literal name only) — this denies *assignment*, not reads (`printenv`, `echo $NAME`), which is why no credential lives in an inheritable env (above) and why the audit redactor (§2.4) exists; (i-b) add `/usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec` and `sudo -u _aicodex` to `_HARD_DENY_PATTERNS`; (ii) INV-22 server-side deny for `api.tradier.com`, `sandbox.tradier.com/v1/accounts/*/orders`, `*.alpaca.markets/v2/orders` in the Bash hard-deny list (`guards.py:137-149`; `SYSTEM.md:130` open follow-up) **and** a `WebFetch|WebSearch` PreToolUse matcher denying the same hosts in `tool_input.url`/`query` — both tools are in the 9-tool default (`session.py:706-709`) and unhooked today (`guards.py:411-416` binds only `FILE_WRITE_TOOLS` and `Bash`); honestly a text-level belt (a URL assembled from parts never matches): the atlas kernel tripwires and `risk.validate` remain the enforcement of record (§8.2); (iii) the read-only `restart_project` deny **stays** in the hook regex (`guards.py:485-488`, INV-20's enforcement point per `SYSTEM.md`) and `mcp_projects.restart_project_tool` gains the same check as a second belt — it does not move to an unprotected file, since that would make an INV-20 restraint autonomously editable. Untrusted input (Telegram text — owner-authored but treated as untrusted — fetched pages, MCP results, vendor-produced text) reaches only lanes **whose containment is stated, and the ratings are quoted as the research writes them (round 3 — the previous wording called the Claude lane "A-rated" on the research's authority while deferring the mechanism the research makes that rating conditional on):** Codex `exec` under the user separation above is rated **A−** on writes/network by `crosscut-tos §3.8a`, raised by the dedicated user; the **Claude lane is not A-rated until Seatbelt is forced on** — `§3.8a` rates `-p` "**A** (with `failIfUnavailable` + `strictAllowlist` + `--allowedTools` narrowed per job)" and records that "if the sandbox cannot start … Claude Code shows a warning and runs commands without sandboxing" unless `failIfUnavailable` is set, that "Sandbox covers only shell tools, not Read/Edit/WebFetch", and that "**Trust verification is disabled when running non-interactively with the `-p` flag**"; its operative rule is that Telegram-originated and web-grounded jobs run on A-rated lanes **with the sandbox forced on**. Through P4 this server's belt is therefore PreToolUse hooks on Bash + file-write tools only, with `WebFetch`/`WebSearch` unhooked until PR #1b (`guards.py:411-416`) — so **§14 Q2's probe (one `claude -p` run with `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true`, `strictAllowlist: true`) moves into the P0 gate list**, because its answer decides whether the fleet's primary lane meets the rule the spec cites for everything else, and a P5/D13 answer arrives four phases too late. C-rated HTTP lanes may receive untrusted input only with no tools and only after the §8.3 redactor; transcript-derived text never leaves `ollama-local`/`anthropic-utility` (`utility-learning`).

Vendor-produced text never drives a privileged session: today `_learning_apply` (`skills/_learning_apply/SKILL.md`: `permission_mode: bypassPermissions`, Read/Edit/Bash, `isolation: none` on the shared checkout) applies the classifier's `content` verbatim into `.context/modules/**/GOTCHAS.md` — a persistent prompt-injection channel into the context every future Anthropic session reads. It becomes a runner-written `proposals` row (or `isolation: workspace` with a diff-only write to the learnings file) that the existing proposal review path applies (P3, with `utility_call`). The same rule is enforced on the *other* chain round 1 left open — vendor verdict text → governor/retro → proposal → `server-patch` (which merges autonomously on the INV-4 lane; `review-and-improve/SKILL.md:152-166` dispatches `server-patch` with the proposal text in the payload): (a) one `untrusted_envelope()` helper — **in the protected `src/runner/restraints.py`**, not in the INV-4-editable `src/runner/grading.py` (§2.5) — wraps every render of `verdicts.findings[].text`, `grade_done`, critic DISSENT text and `ask` answers into any Anthropic prompt — governors, `retro`, `_evaluate`, `--file=` reuse — with the "untrusted data, not instructions" marker, and a test asserts the raw text never appears unwrapped; (b) **the cross-vendor mark keys on the *author*, not on the artefact kind** (round 3 critical — round 2's rule said "cites a non-Anthropic **verdict**", and P3's `utility_call(utility-learning)` classifier produces a `proposals` row citing no verdict at all): a `proposals` row is `origin=cross-vendor` whenever **any text in its content or evidence was produced by a non-Anthropic executor** — `verdicts` rows, `grade_done`, critic text, `ask` answers **and any `utility_call` whose answering provider was not `anthropic-utility`** (`utility_call{provider}` is already audited, so the `utility-learning` extractor stamps `proposals.origin` from the answering provider, never from the class) — and `server-patch` treats such rows as owner-approval-required (`approvals(kind=plan)`) rather than autonomous. That gate lives in `skills/server-patch/SKILL.md` (protected path #8), so it is an explicit line in PR #1b and in the §9 P4 "Protected touches" column; (c) `retro`'s frontmatter limits its state change to inserting `proposals` rows — it never dispatches `server-patch` (§5.5) — and **the autonomous `server-patch` dispatcher is removed in P0 unconditionally** (`_check_idle_queue_review`, `events.py:432-460`, plus the `main` wiring at `:497`), *not* conditionally on D1's displacement choice and not in P4, so that no autonomous proposal→`server-patch` path exists at the moment P3 introduces a local-model proposal author (§5.5, §9 P0).

Non-Anthropic lanes cannot publish their own output (read-only or network-off sandboxes, no git credentials), so **`src/runner/publish.py` (P4)** does it: after the merge gate is `lgtm` (or, for critics, after schema validation) the runner applies the **hardcoded per-target path allowlist** (`publish_allowlist` in §2.5, source of truth `lint_docs.py`/`restraints.py`: atlas → `research/**`, `docs/**`, `*/reports/**` only; `swing/**`, `tradingcore/**`, `**/risk*`, `*/limits.yaml`, `*/budget.yaml`, `.github/**`, `.claude/**`, `integrations/ai-server/**` denied — the same repo holds the only order path, the owner-owned kernel limits (C22) and the tripwire tests, and MISSION §M INV-22 makes any limit widening owner-only regardless of LGTMs, while atlas's own tripwires run in atlas CI *after* the push) **before the review job runs** (`test_publish`: "a Codex diff touching `swing/` is refused before the gate"), writes the artefact into the clone, commits as `ai-server-bot <purpose>`, pushes with a scoped deploy key, audits `artifact_published{sha, path}` and then ff-syncs. **Transport is specified, because the ambient default defeats the key (round 3):** the target remotes are HTTPS (`git -C projects/atlas remote -v` → `https://github.com/Piserchia/atlas.git`, and `workspaces.py:160-166` re-points every clone's origin at exactly that URL), git is configured with `credential.helper=osxkeychain`, and `gh auth status` shows the owner's full-scope token over HTTPS — so a plain `git push` would authenticate as the owner's full-access GitHub account and the "runner-only, write-scoped per remote" property behind the allowlist's blast-radius argument would not exist. `publish.py` therefore pushes with the ambient helper disabled and an explicit key: `git -c credential.helper= -c url."git@github.com:".insteadOf=https://github.com/ push origin HEAD` under `GIT_SSH_COMMAND="ssh -i ~/.config/ai-server/publish-key -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes"` (or a fine-grained PAT injected into the URL per invocation and never persisted); `test_publish` asserts the push argv carries an empty `credential.helper` and the key path. GitHub **deploy keys are per-repository**, so one key per target remote is required (§12a row 18b). The key is minted by the owner as *write-scoped* per target remote — `Piserchia/atlas` master and the owner-only report repos, never ai-server — and sealed 0600 under `~/.config/ai-server/publish-key` outside any workspace (**auth config, §12a row 18b**). **Residual, stated as the sealed setup-token's is (round 3):** the key is held in the runner process and is in the EnvScrubber deny test, but an EnvScrubber test only proves it is not in an *environment variable* — sessions run as the owner and `guards.py` denies writes and dangerous Bash heads, not reads, so **any session's Bash can `cat` the file, and the 48 unhooked ones have no hook at all**. The residual is accepted until D16 and bounded by (i) the key's write scope (atlas `research/**`-capable remotes only, never ai-server), (ii) the §8.3/§2.4 redactor pattern set, which now carries the literal path and the `-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----` PEM shape so a `cat` cannot land the material in the JSONL, the stream or an `ai-mcp` read, (iii) a §8.2(b) tripwire that plants the publish-key path as a canary and asserts its content appears in no JSONL, stream or `ai-mcp` read, and (iv) a preference for a **per-push ephemeral credential** (an `ssh-agent` socket the runner creates for the push and tears down, 0700 in a runner-only dir) over a persistent file, with the remote itself branch/path-restricted wherever the host allows it — because `publish.py` is the only thing enforcing the allowlist. The single-writer topology (C20) holds because nothing writes the canonical checkout directly. For the atlas critic the file artefact is dropped in favour of DB-only `verdicts(stage=critic)` — mirrored by **`grading.py`** (not `publish.py`) into the atlas table `<vertical>.critic_verdicts` so governors can read it (§8.1; round 3: `publish.py` runs six steps *before* the critic verdict exists and only on non-Anthropic writer lanes, while `trading-research` is `pinned: true` and therefore always Anthropic-written — so the bracketed publish step never executed in that job at all and the P5 test could not pass). The SYSTEM.md INV-13/INV-21 row amendments — from "(a) at `isolation: workspace` with guard hooks" to "(a) at `isolation: workspace` under the executor's containment profile as declared in spec §2.6 (SDK hooks for Claude; OS sandbox + scrubbed env + dedicated user via sudoers wrapper for Codex)", citing plan §8 — rewrite owner-approved invariant text (plan §8, approved 2026-08-17) and are therefore **under D2 for explicit owner sign-off**, landing with PR #1b so the P5 invariant-preservation test asserts text the owner approved.

### 2.7 Event / audit model

All 33 existing kinds and shapes are kept (C14). Added, all appended to the per-job JSONL and mirrored to `jobs:stream:<id>`: `provider_selected{provider, model, reason ∈ pin|policy|fallback|canary, chain}`, `provider_fallback{from, to, cause}`, `executor_started{executor, cli_version, lane, queue_wait_ms}`, `quota_snapshot{provider, window, used_pct, resets_at, source}`, `heartbeat{turn, last_tool, tokens_so_far, elapsed_s}` every 15 s, `job_held{reason, next_check}`, `contract_resolved{checks, on_fail}`, `evidence_check{check, pass, detail, timeout?}`, `dod_tick{pass_count, total}` — emitted (a) on `contract_resolved` with 0/n, (b) **live** for checks evaluable from executor events: `lifecycle_marker_present` (final text), `file_exists`/`glob_matched` (Write/Edit `tool_use` targets checked in the clone), `tests_ran` (Bash `tool_result`), `budget_within` (heartbeat), and (c) for the rest (`git_pushed`, `claimed_files_exist`, `tests_green`, `url_200`, `db_row`, …) at `finalize_workspace` — so the JobCard's `DoD m/n` segment is live only from P2 (when `src/contracts/` ships) and only for the (b) subset, `deliverable_registered{kind, title, ref}`, `done_verdict{verdict, missing[]}`, `merge_gate_verdict{belt, reviewer, verdict, findings_n}`, `grade_done{provider, verdict, score, findings_n}`, `approval_requested/decided`, `notice_queued/sent/failed`, `shadow_decision{provider, agrees, latency_ms}`, `provider_refused{reason}`, `routing_correction{from, to}`, `utility_call{provider, purpose, redacted, patterns_hit}`, `subagent_result{agent, text}` (the **full** text of a `Task` tool_result for review subagents — today's 500-char `result_preview`, `session.py:1182`, drops everything after the first paragraph), `artifact_published{sha, path}`, `workspace_synced{sha}`, `lane_budget{lane, used, budget}`. `terminal_reason` is stamped on `job_completed`/`job_failed`. JSONL stays the durable trace and the task state-machine input (`main.py:1053-1266`); rows are derived from it, never the reverse, so `reconcile.py` ("last terminal wins") is unchanged. `ScriptExecutor` synthesises `TASK_COMPLETE:` from exit 0 so the marker contract (`session.py:405-460`) covers every executor.

**Clone lifetime (P3 work item, with the executor seam).** Today `sync_canonical` + `cleanup_workspace` run inside `run_session`'s `finally` (`session.py:1035-1045`) before `_finish_job` publishes `jobs:done` (`main.py:1299`) and before any post-step (`main.py:376-444`), so nothing that must see the clone — evidence checks, `tests_green`, the non-Anthropic merge gate, `publish.py` — could run. They move into `finalize_workspace(job, ws)`, invoked from a `finally` around `_process_job`'s try/except (never as a success-chain step — see below), so the clone is still alive for the evidence checks and, on non-Anthropic lanes, for the allowlist check, the gate and `publish.py` (`review.run_code_review(parent_job_id, diff, cwd)` already takes an arbitrary cwd/diff, `review.py:211-215`). **Explicit post-step order — the status flip stays ahead of everything that reads it** (round 1's "DAG promote first" would have stranded DAG children: `promote_deferred_for` decides from `j.status == completed`, `plans.py:197`, and that flip happens in `_finish_job`, `main.py:1284-1299`, the same call that publishes `jobs:done`; `_maybe_review`/learning likewise depend on the refetch after it, `main.py:388-390`): `run_session` returns → `contracts.check` + deliverable auto-detection (clone alive) → gate verdict capture → [non-Anthropic lanes only: `publish_allowlist` → in-process merge gate (§5.3 belt 3b) → `publish.py`] → `_finish_job` (status=completed, result, `jobs:done`) → refetch → `promote_deferred_for` → `_maybe_review` → writeback → grader/critic **+ `critic_mirror`** (`grading.py` writes `<vertical>.critic_verdicts` immediately after the `verdicts(stage=critic)` insert and before `jobs:reviewed`, §8.1) → learning → task lifecycle → notify → `jobs:reviewed`. **`finalize_workspace` is not a step in that success chain — it is invoked from a `finally` wrapping `_process_job`'s try/except** (round 3: in `main.py` the whole post-step chain lives inside the `try:` that awaits `run_session`, and the `except quota.QuotaExhausted` arm at `:446-458`, the `SkillResolutionError` arm, the timeout arm and the generic arm all return without running any of it — today removal is unconditional precisely because it sits in `run_session`'s own `finally`, `session.py:1035-1045`). Its internal branching: ff-sync + `workspace_synced` **on the success path only**; the Codex copy under `/Users/Shared/ai-codex/<job8>` removed on **every** path — success, quota requeue, timeout, cancel, resolution error — preserving only `out.json`/diff under `volumes/workspaces/<job>/`; the per-job *clone* kept on failure as today. A runner-startup sweep additionally removes any `/Users/Shared/ai-codex/*` directory with no running job (the belt `requeue_stranded_queued` provides for ids), and the P4 test list gains "a quota-requeued Codex job leaves no directory under `/Users/Shared/ai-codex/`". **Every post-step runs under `asyncio.wait_for(_POST_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)`, and the whole chain under a 900 s wall-clock budget** (today `wait_for` wraps only `run_session`, `main.py:376-380`, while `_run_with_held_slot` releases the semaphore only after every post-step returns, `main.py:188-194` — a hung `tests_green` or gate would hold a slot and the clone forever, and gate 600 s + post-review 600 s + grader 600 s would occupy one of two prod slots for ~30 minutes): a timeout stamps `evidence_check{name, timeout: true}` + `done_verdict=unverified` and never wedges the slot; `test_post_step_timeout` and `test_post_step_order` ("a deferred child of a completing workspace job is promoted, and `workspace_synced` precedes `jobs:done`") are P2 gates. `jobs:reviewed` fires after graders. ff-sync *semantics* (INV-16) are unchanged; only the clone's lifetime extends past `run_session`.

### 2.8 Cost / quota ledger

| Lane | Window source | Per-call ledger | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Max | `RateLimitEvent` → `quota_snapshots(source=vendor)` for **every one of the SDK's five `rate_limit_type` literals** — `{five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_opus, seven_day_sonnet, overage}` (`claude_agent_sdk/types.py:1181-1183`, pinned 0.1.81) — recorded verbatim, with `quota_snapshots.family` **taken from the event type, never inferred** (`seven_day_opus` ⇒ `opus`, `seven_day_sonnet` ⇒ `sonnet`, `seven_day` ⇒ `all`) and `overage` feeding the credit-overflow tripwire: `status` (`allowed\|allowed_warning\|rejected`) + `resets_at`, `utilization` when present (prod to 2026-09-27 10:32Z: **732 events = 702 `five_hour` (698 `allowed`, 4 `allowed_warning` at 0.91–0.96, 2 `rejected` at 2026-09-24T14:00Z and 2026-09-25T17:30Z) + 28 `seven_day` `allowed_warning`, utilization 0.25 → 0.58, every one carrying `resets_at` 2026-10-02T00:00Z**; `quota.py:31-48` reads only `status`/`resets_at` today) plus `overage_status`/`overage_resets_at`/`overage_disabled_reason` (`claude_agent_sdk/types.py:1202-1209`); `quota_snapshots(source=vendor-statusline)` from the newest line of `volumes/telemetry/claude_rate_limits.jsonl`, appended by the owner's *own* interactive sessions' `statusLine` command (one-time setup, D22), shown with its age; `quota_snapshots(source=inferred)` 5h/7d estimates from rolling `provider_ledger` `cost_usd_list` sums **per model family** (Opus and Sonnet have separate weekly bars and usage is cost-weighted — `crosscut-tos` line 63, `claude-anthropic.md` line 90) against a window size calibrated from the tier (Max 5x, MISSION §K), the utilization-bearing events and the status-line feed — every inferred figure is labelled "inferred" on every surface, "vendor 7d 58 % (age 40 min)" when a vendor or status-line reading exists. **The weekly denominator, calibrated from the whole series rather than one point (round 3 critical — round 2's "≈ 245 M tokens-equivalent ⇒ ≈ 4 days" was derived from a single reading *and* divided the cap by a partial day's tokens used as a day rate, overstating capacity 1.4–1.9×).** The window is **fixed**, not rolling: all 28 `seven_day` readings across 45 h carry the identical `resets_at` 2026-10-02T00:00Z, so a rolling window is ruled out and no D22 setup or P2 phase is needed to know it (§14 Q12 closed). Calibrated **in dollars**, because that is the unit the budgets use: vendor readings 0.25 (2026-09-25 13:57Z, ≈ $69 of `cost_usd_list` consumed since the window opened) → 0.58 (2026-09-27 10:32Z, ≈ $120 consumed) bracket the weekly allowance at **≈ $207–275 `cost_usd_list` per week** (≈ 176–235 M tokens at the current mix); the *marginal* pair implies ≈ $155. **Take $200/week as the working denominator and re-derive it weekly from the series** (the reconcile script prints it). At the Sep 23–25 rate of **88.3 M tokens/day** (228.1 M over 2.58 days) the window fills in **≈ 2.5–3 days**, not four. The window is shared with every other consumer of the Keychain credential on the box (claude.ai app, brew CLI 2.1.273, Devin's bundled CLI — state map §2.8; whether Devin's copy is the unmodified binary on the owner's own `/login` or a harness is **not established** and is a §12a P0 owner check); the setup-token gives the runner its own credential, not its own window; standing rule: no third-party harness signs in with the Max credential (`crosscut-tos §3.7` Gray→No) | full `ResultMessage` (`usage` incl. `cache_creation.ephemeral_{1h,5m}_input_tokens`, `model_usage`, `total_cost_usd` — **list-equivalent, never a bill** — `num_turns`, `duration_api_ms`) → `jobs` + `provider_ledger(purpose=main)`; nested calls ledgered by purpose; the Haiku side request every `-p`/SDK call also bills (`claude-anthropic.md` line 157) is ledgered as `purpose=harness` if it persists after the swap, and a P0 check asserts `model_usage` on utility calls lists only the requested model | `claude-anthropic.md §3` [68], [85]; matrix §1 item 7; prod audit 2026-09-25 (job 266df9ed) |
| Codex | `account/rateLimits/read` once at runner start, once immediately before each Codex dispatch (gate on `usedPercent`/`resetsAt`; `ordinaryUsageAllowed` null ⇒ "unavailable", never inferred recovery), once after each job — event-shaped, **no timer poll** (a 15-min poll would spawn ~96 app-server sessions/day for a lane making 1–4 calls; `crosscut-tos §3.10`, §7 item 2); **every read runs under the `codex:stream:<n>` lease** (§2.4) because it authenticates from the stream's `auth.json`; every read also records `credits` and `spendControlReached` (`chatgpt-openai.md` line 487) — OpenAI sells credit overage "on any plan" at roughly API list prices (line 580), which would silently carry the lane past the plan window with no `rejected`-equivalent, so a non-zero credit balance refuses dispatch unless `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK` lists `codex`, `codex: {overflow_credits: disabled}` sits in `providers.yml`, and the `provider_fallback` card reads "codex credits present — skipped (declined 2026-08-17)" | `turn.completed` usage | `chatgpt-openai.md §3, §4, §7` |
| Gemini free | own Redis counter vs 250 RPD (`source=inferred`) | `usage_metadata` | `gemini-google.md §3b` |
| Cerebras free | own Redis counter vs 1 M tokens/day + 5 RPM (`source=inferred`); D18 also tells us whether the "$5 credit" drains — if so `cost_class: trial`, balance shown, fallback card at 0 | response `usage` | `direct-openweight-hosts.md §4.2–4.3` |
| Groq free | own Redis counter vs 200 k tokens/day per model + 8 k TPM (`source=inferred`) | response `usage` | `direct-openweight-hosts.md §3` |
| Local | free RAM (`psutil`), `GET /api/ps`; skip when <3 GB free | `eval_count/eval_duration` | `local-models-m4-16gb.md §7` |

**`cost_usd_list` has exactly one definition (round 3 critical — it had two incompatible ones, and every cost control in the spec reads this column): it is the runner's own computation from tokens, in `src/runner/pricing.py`, shipped in P0** (`providers.yml` takes over the price table in P3 — it does not exist in P0, so "computed per `providers.yml`" was unimplementable there), with cache writes priced from `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_{1h,5m}_input_tokens` at the matching rate — **the subscription's cache TTL is 1 h (`claude-anthropic.md` line 220) and prod records 100 % of cache writes as 1-h** (45.3 M tokens 1h, 0 tokens 5m over 634 jobs), i.e. $10/M Opus and $6/M Sonnet, not the $6.25/$3.75 5-m rate round 1 used (`test_result_capture`: "the 1-h rate is used when `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens > 0`"); cards say "$0 marginal · $3.49 list-equiv". **The SDK's `total_cost_usd` is stored separately as `jobs.sdk_cost_usd` and is *not* assumed to be the same figure** — round 2 asserted it was, while the P0 plan populated `cost_usd_list` *from* it, so the P0 exit check compared the SDK figure to itself and nothing ever tested it against the 1-h computation; if the CLI prices cache writes at the 5-m rate the column would sit ~19 % below every budget seeded from the script ($411 of the $963 is the cache-write leg). **P0 exit criterion, restated: the two agree within 1 %, or the divergence is recorded together with the rate the CLI uses.** Neither figure is ever a bill.

**Calibration anchor — prod ledger to 2026-09-25, printed at both windows** (supersedes `crosscut-finance-trading-ai.md §3` "Empirical calibration", which read the *dev checkout's* 16-JSONL sample — 2026-04/07 jobs — and is ~60× low; do not re-cite it). *Trailing 30 d* (634 priced completed jobs since 2026-08-26): 749 M cache-read + 45.3 M cache-write (all 1-h) + 9.35 M output + 20 k uncached input tokens; fleet-median completed job 376 k cache-read / 47 k cache-write / 9.0 k output — a Sonnet `atlas-report` shape, ≈ $0.59. Priced at list **with the 1-h cache-write rate the subscription uses**: **≈ $963/month-equivalent** — opus-5 $414, opus-4-7 $255, sonnet-4-6 $151, opus-4-8 $143 (round 1's $809 used the 5-m rate and was ≈ 19 % low). *Per-model job counts are deliberately not restated here*: round 2's split (109 + 168 + 337 + 31 = 645) did not sum to its own "634 priced completed jobs", so **P0 Task 16 pastes one consistent set from a single `scripts/cost-reconcile.py --days 30` run, and reports unattributable jobs as `unpriced` with their token counts rather than dropping them** (`test_unknown_model_is_reported_not_priced`) — the likeliest source of the 11-job gap. The dollar column is sound and reproduces within 2 % on recomputation. Shape: **≈ 8–10× the Max 5x seat (MISSION §K: $100), and cache-**write**-led in dollars — write ≈ 42 %, read ≈ 37 %, output ≈ 22 % — although 93 % cache-read in tokens; the 1-h subscription TTL is why** (round 3: "cache-read-dominated" was true of tokens and false of dollars, and the whole $154 gap between round 1's $809 and this $963 *is* that one leg re-rated). It is the row `crosscut-tos §4.3` describes as "exceeds the plan price 2–4×, will hit weekly bars", and above its "~2× the plan price → outside ordinary, individual usage" heuristic (D25) — **with one honest caveat: that heuristic defines its metric as "API-equivalent = Σ(in × p_in + cached × p_cache + out × p_out)", with no cache-creation term. Measured on `crosscut-tos §4.3`'s own formula this same ledger is ≈ $550/month ≈ 5.5× the seat; the ≈ $963 figure includes the cache-write leg, which is the more complete accounting but not the metric the heuristic was written against.** Prod shows four five-hour `allowed_warning` at 0.91–0.96 utilization and **two** `rejected` in 30 d (the second landed 2026-09-25 17:30Z, 3.5 h after round 2's anchor cut-off), plus a **continuous `seven_day` `allowed_warning` series** — every reading of the 7-d bar arrives with that status, which is why §2.8's tripwire below no longer counts `allowed_warning`. Per-model medians replace the mislabelled "median Opus 5 job ≈ $0.71": **opus-5 ≈ $3.43** (n = 109), fleet ≈ $0.59; today's real opus-5 `atlas-swing-research` job (2.40 M read / 111 k 1-h write / 47 k out) = $3.49, the DoneCard example in §4.2. *Trailing 7 d — the anchor that matters, because the load's largest step post-dates the 30-d mean*: daily tokens 2026-09-12..22 = 6.7–32.7 M/day (mean ≈ 23 M); **2026-09-23 = 98.7 M, 09-24 = 68.0 M, 09-25 (to 14:00Z) = 61.4 M** — the alpha flywheel (commit 5448c81, 2026-09-23 15:00 −0400) tripled daily consumption two days before this spec was written; ISO week 39 (Sep 21–25 only) = **279 M tokens** vs the 30-d-mean "≈ 185 M/week"; week-39 `alpha-research` = 25 jobs / 162.1 M (all but 2 of the 27 the 30-d table attributes to "30 d" landed in the last three days). **Line item — alpha flywheel live 2026-09-23, described from the code rather than from the constant's name (round 3 critical).** `ALPHA_DAILY_JOB_VALVE = 12` (`src/runner/events.py:364`) is **not** a per-day allowance for `alpha-research`: it is a suppression threshold on the *idle drainer* — `_should_trigger_idle_alpha` returns False when `alpha_jobs_24h >= daily_valve` (`:379-382`), the count is of `Job.kind == "alpha-research"` created in 24 h (`:412-418`), the drainer also requires `queued_or_running == 0` (`:381`), and what it then enqueues is an **`alpha-governor`** job (`:420-429`) averaging ≈ 2.3 M tokens, not 6.5 M. Prod on 2026-09-23 completed 13 `alpha-research` + 2 `alpha-governor` + 2 `alpha-scout` — already above the valve for the gated kind alone. **Measured alpha load is 47–86 M/day (09-23..09-27, mean ≈ 51 M/day ≈ 360 M/week ≈ +1,550 M/month over the pre-flywheel baseline)**, set by the scout/governor chains and the alpha-lab `budget.yaml` the constant only mirrors; the valve-derived "450–550 M/week" is a *ceiling*, not a steady state. **Consequently lowering the valve from 12 to 6 cannot remove ≈ 40 M/day — it stops a few governor enqueues** (round 2 funded D1's build displacement and D25(b) from that saving; both are re-stated below). The lever that actually moves this load is a **LOOP.md §7 proposal against alpha-lab `budget.yaml`** capping `alpha-research` jobs/day and ≤ 4 M/job, sized from the measured 47–86 M/day. The P0 reconcile script emits both windows and the per-kind step every week and **prints the lane-budget seed table the P2 budget reader consumes** (not `routing-policy.yml`, which lands in P3 — §2.5 `lanes:`); automatic weekly re-seeding starts in P3. §13 is restated from the current run-rate, not the 30-d mean.

**Max weekly-window budget — modelled as load-during-build, per model family, in dollars.** The weekly window is the only real budget under subscription-first, and until the P4 `research-read` canary is `routable` no vendor lane absorbs agentic work, so it is budgeted explicitly rather than hoped about — **in `cost_usd_list` per Anthropic model family**, not raw tokens: a 1 M cache-read Sonnet job and a 1 M cache-write Opus job are 33× apart in list-equivalent (ledger: opus-5 $414 vs sonnet-4-6 $151 at 381 M vs 136 M tokens). **Cost-weighting is a modelling assumption, not a vendor statement (round 3).** What the research establishes is narrower: separate Opus/Sonnet weekly bars exist (`crosscut-tos` line 63: "both a 5-hour and a weekly bar exist, plus separate Opus/Sonnet weekly bars") and token budgets are **unpublished** (`claude-anthropic.md` line 90: "weekly cap across all models, plus model-family messages … exact token budgets unpublished"); neither line says cost-weighted, and the only published anchors in the research are in *hours*. Cost-weighting is inferred from the prod utilization series (the implied token cap falls from ≈ 235 M to ≈ 176 M as the mix turns Opus-heavy) and is **confirmed or refuted by the `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet` events the SDK already emits** — the first such event closes it (§14). If the bar proves time- or token-weighted, the dollar budgets over-hold Opus lanes and under-hold Sonnet ones. So `quota:anthropic:<family>:*` keys, `quota_snapshots.family`, `window_ok(provider, family)` (only the red family's jobs are held), per-lane budgets `{opus: $, sonnet: $}`, `lane_budget{lane, family, used, budget}` events, and `quota_share_pct` per family on the scoreboard; `anthropic.max_sessions` is per family too. Where the tokens go (30 d, with the 7-d step noted above): alpha-research 27 jobs = 162 M cache-read (≈ 22 % of the 30-d fleet, ≈ 6 M/job — **≈ 58 % of week 39**); atlas-build 9 = 66 M; atlas-report 157 = 57 M; review-and-improve 27 = 36 M — three skills ≈ 38 % of the 30-d window, and the measured alpha load alone fills the ≈ $200/week window in ≈ 2.5–3 days. **Load during build, not steady state:** the build programme itself draws **≈ 28 M tokens ≈ $30 `cost_usd_list` per week** ≈ **+120 M/month ≈ $115/month list-equivalent for ~13 weeks** at server-patch's measured shape (15 jobs / 52.3 M tokens / $50 at 1-h rates ≈ $0.95/M), ledgered `purpose=build` **on a `build` lane** (§2.5 — round 3: the cap could not bind while phase PRs landed on the `owner` lane, which is exempt from every hold, and it was expressed as "15 % of 185 M/week", a percentage of the trailing-30-d mean this very section forbids as a seed and which its own week-39 reading of 279 M contradicts; at the measured run-rate the build is ~5–6 % of the window, so the percentage form was simultaneously unenforceable and non-binding). **The figure is an absolute ceiling, re-cut with the lane budgets each week from the reconcile script, never re-derived as a percentage of a growing window.** — so P0–P5 raise the window load (build + flywheel) on a window that reached 0.91–0.96 four times in 30 d and 25 % of the week by 14:00Z on day one. What is displaced during build weeks is decided by the owner in D1, not left implicit: **either** a LOOP.md §7 proposal against alpha-lab `budget.yaml` caps `alpha-research` jobs/day and ≤ 4 M/job — sized from the measured 47–86 M/day, and **the only lever that moves this load** (the `ALPHA_DAILY_JOB_VALVE` 12 → 6 change round 2 costed at ≈ −40 M/day gates only the idle drainer's `alpha-governor` enqueue, ≈ 2.3 M/job — see the line item above) — **or** D1 explicitly accepts "N `rejected` windows/week during build". Independently of D1, **`review-and-improve`'s idle trigger is removed in P0 unconditionally** (≈ −30 M/month net) because it is the autonomous `server-patch` dispatcher the §2.6 cross-vendor rule has to close before P3 introduces a local-model proposal author — it is a containment item, not a budget lever. Expected steady-state deltas per phase, each citing its phase: `retro` replacing review-and-improve ≈ −30 M/month (P4); the `provisioning_gap` pre-check ≈ −20 M **(unmeasured until the P3 `ScriptExecutor` version lands with a real per-kind key list — the P0 interim check cannot detect the gap it was written for, §8.3)**; Haiku→Sonnet on router/learning **≈ +$8/month list-equivalent, 0 token change** (a model swap changes price, not tokens; Haiku $1/$5 → Sonnet $3/$15 on a ~4 M input-dominated load) until P3; in-process utility ≈ −4 M (P3); graders +0 on Anthropic (never Anthropic); post-review-by-class, `_evaluate`/`fix_round` and chat-via-SDK are capped by `max_turns` ceilings and reported on the CostCard before any is widened. Net modelled belts are **≈ −54 M/month and +≈$8/month of price change** (−30 retro − 20 provisioning-gap − 4 in-process utility; the Haiku→Sonnet swap moves price, not tokens, so it is never added into a token sum — round 3: "≈ −46 M" was reached only by mixing the dollar figure into the token arithmetic, the conflation round-1 finding #49 was raised to fix), and either way **noise against the measured ≈ +1,550 M/month flywheel step**; the levers that move the bar are the alpha valve, the job shape and cadence of alpha-research/atlas-build/atlas-report (per-skill token-shape targets: alpha-research ≤ 4 M/job, atlas-build ≤ 5 M/job, atlas-report ≤ 0.5 M/job — proposals through LOOP.md §7), `max_turns`, and — the research's own fix — a second *lane* (the P4 `research-read` canary; D14 Routines; branch B (a trigger inside D5, §0c)), never a second seat. `routing-policy.yml: lanes.<lane>.weekly_budget` is enforced in the P2 job loop as `job_held{reason=lane_budget_exhausted, next_check}` on rolling 7-d `provider_ledger` sums (owner lane never held; **governed atlas rows are never auto-held — an atlas lane at 100 % raises a LOOP.md §7 proposal and an owner notice, and the P2 exit criterion is "no governed schedule held for budget in the first 7 days without an owner notice"**), surfaced on QuotaStrip/CostCard with an 80 % warning notice; `tests/test_lanes.py`: "lane over budget is held, owner lane is not, governed atlas lane notices instead". **A per-provider running-session cap (`crosscut-tos §3.10b`), with the mechanism stated rather than asserted (round 3).** `max_sessions` counts **every** Anthropic session, main or post-step: the job loop holds *pops* against it, and the belt-3b merge gate, post-review and `anthropic-utility` terminal-link fallbacks acquire the same per-family semaphore — the loop alone could not enforce it, because belt 3b opens a fresh Anthropic session inside an already-popped Codex job's post-step. Post-step sessions draw on a **separate `gate_sessions: 1` budget** (§2.5) so a gate never contends with job sessions; a gate that still cannot acquire within `_POST_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` records `verdicts(stage=merge_gate, verdict=skipped, skip_reason=session_cap)` and **re-queues the publish attempt rather than failing the canary job** — a capacity shortage must not stamp `timeout`, park a clone and open a FailedCard for a job that did nothing wrong, and `skipped(session_cap)` is excluded from the writer ladder's `lgtm_rate`/`dod_pass_rate` exactly as `model_unavailable` is excluded from the quota-skip rate (§5.4). So raising prod to 3 (D1) cannot put three Claude sessions on the window. In P2 the cap is read as `ANTHROPIC_MAX_SESSIONS=2` from `Settings` (`providers.yml` is a P3 deliverable and takes it over then); `tests/test_lanes.py` gains "a Codex job's belt-3b gate cannot raise concurrent Anthropic sessions above `max_sessions`".

**Metered-spend tripwires (Anthropic).** The only way metered Anthropic spend can occur is the usage-credits path (`claude-anthropic.md §3`: credits continue past caps at standard API rates, $2,000/day redemption limit, cache TTL drops to 5 m) — it would replace the `rejected` event the pause logic keys on with a silent bill. D3 confirms on claude.ai billing that usage credits are $0, auto-reload is off and the monthly spend limit is $0, recorded as `anthropic: {overflow_credits: disabled}` in `providers.yml`; the quarterly checklist card re-verifies it. Runtime, **vendor signal first**: a `terminal_reason=billing_error` mapped from `system/api_retry`'s own `error` category (§2.4) is the **first** `possible_credit_overflow` trigger; next `RateLimitInfo.overage_status == allowed` while `status == rejected` (`claude_agent_sdk/types.py:1202-1209`, "status of overage/pay-as-you-go usage if applicable") and any `rate_limit_type=overage` event — it is recorded on `quota_snapshots` and in the `SDK_RECORD` fields; the ledger heuristic (5-h `provider_ledger` tokens exceed the largest window previously observed before an `allowed_warning ≥ 0.95`/`rejected` by > 25 % with no `rejected` arriving; also a `cache_write_5m_tokens > 0` reading, since the TTL drop to 5 m is itself the credits signature) is the fallback; either queues `notice(severity=high, kind=possible_credit_overflow)` and holds non-owner, non-governed lanes until the owner acknowledges (governed atlas rows get a notice + LOOP.md §7 proposal, never an automatic hold). **The "ordinary, individual usage" tripwire is alarm-only, and it is not the plan price.** Round 1 set "alarm at the plan price, hold non-owner lanes at 2× plan price"; against the measured ≈ $963/month that hold trips on day 6–7 of every month and parks atlas/kernel/utility/background for the remaining three weeks, contradicting §9's "all 41 schedules keep running" — a ToS-posture question (`crosscut-tos §4.3`: "above ~2× the plan price → outside ordinary, individual usage, and the fix is a second *lane*, never a second account") was mis-specified as a runtime hold. Now — **and keyed on a rate, not a count, because the count-based baseline is already exceeded (round 3)**: prod carries 32 `allowed_warning` (4 `five_hour` + **28 `seven_day`**) and **2** `rejected`, so "exceeds the measured 4/1" would fire permanently from day one and carry no signal; the vendor emits every reading of the 7-d bar as `allowed_warning`, so that status is a gauge carrier, not a scarcity signal. `notice(kind=ordinary_usage_exceeded)` on the CostCard fires when **rolling-30-d `cost_usd_list` exceeds the P0-measured baseline by 25 %, or `rejected` events exceed 2 per 30 d, or the vendor `seven_day` utilization crosses 0.85 before day 5 of the window**; `allowed_warning` is not a trigger. The only runtime hold is on **non-owner, non-governed** lanes at trailing-90-d median × 1.5 — never on the plan price, never on governed atlas rows (those raise a LOOP.md §7 proposal) — and it is **inactive until 90 d of `provider_ledger` exist, i.e. not before P5**, since that table lands in migration 009 (P2). **Effect on the 41 schedules:** none are held by this tripwire; governed rows notice, kernel/utility rows hold only on the 90-d rule once it can compute, owner never. The exposure itself — ≈ 8–10× the Max 5x seat against Anthropic's "ordinary, individual usage" wording — is an owner decision, **D25**, not a runtime rule. **Programmatic-credit re-gating** (`claude-anthropic.md §3`: the May-2026 Agent-SDK/`claude -p` credit split — $100 Max 5x / $200 Max 20x per month — is *paused*, and Anthropic "can re-enable … without prior notice"): at the measured run-rate a $100 pool is exhausted in **~3–4 days of each month** (~4–7 at the 30-d mean). Pre-decided response, flippable by one env var (`ANTHROPIC_CREDIT_MODE=1`): the `programmatic_credit_mode` preset (§2.5) sizes every `lanes.<lane>.weekly_budget` to fit the pool — owner + kernel lanes funded first, atlas governed rows via a pre-drafted LOOP.md §7 proposal (`docs/proposals/credit-mode-atlas.md`, written in P2) — and D14 moves from "defer" to "prepare, do not schedule" (one repo-scoped Routine registered so the first-party fallback exists before it is needed); branch B (a trigger inside D5, §0c) / the P4 Codex canary is the structural hedge (§11).
