## 4) Interfaces and visibility

### 4.1 Grammar (identical strings on Telegram `/verb`, `ai verb`, and `ai_verb` tools)

```
task <text> [--kind=<skill>] [--model=<provider/model|alias>] [--effort=low|medium|high|xhigh|max] [--project=<slug>] [--timeout=<s>] [--watch] [--quiet] [--second-opinion=<provider>]
watch <job|task>     jobs [--running|--queued|--held|--blocked|--failed|--lane=<lane>|--provider=<id>]     job <id> [--log|--files|--cost|--verdicts]     queue
tasks [--blocked|--open]     help [<verb>]
cancel <id>          retry <job> [--model=][--effort=][--second-opinion=]     reopen <task|job>     mute <job>|today|--lane=<lane>
approvals [--open|--all]     approve <approval-id> [note]     reject <approval-id> [note]     reply <task|job|approval-id> <text>
ask <provider> "<text>" [--file=<deliverable-id>]     alerts [--since=<dur>]     digest [--since=<dur>]     scoreboard [--class=<task_class>]
schedule list [--lane=][--due=today][--flagged]|show <name>|run <name>|pause <name>|resume <name>|edit <name> --cron="<preset|cron>" [--notify=…]|add "<preset or cron>" --kind=<skill> [--project=…]
providers [status|pause <id>|resume <id>|ladder]     quota     cost [--week|--month] [--by=provider|skill|vertical|lane]
lane hold <lane> [--until=<preset|time>] [--reason=<text>]     lane release <lane>     (Telegram `/lane`, CLI `ai lane`, PWA Now-panel toggle)
deliverables [<task|job>]     open <deliverable-id>     send <deliverable-id>     atlas [status|<vertical>]     proposals [list|show|approve <id>]     project <slug> status|deploy|logs
```

Verb semantics the cards depend on: `approvals` lists open ids + kind + age (the discovery path for `approve`, which the CLI/PWA otherwise cannot find); `jobs --blocked` = jobs waiting on the owner (the week-one question "what is waiting on me?") and is distinct from `--held` (window/budget hold); `jobs --provider=<id>` and `ai_jobs(provider=)` answer "what is Codex doing right now"; `retry` re-runs the same spec by default (escalation hop not inherited; `--model/--effort` under the §2.5 rules); `reopen` re-opens a completed task/job for a follow-up turn; `mute <job>` stops JobCard edits, `mute today` silences non-failure cards until local midnight, `mute --lane=` silences a lane's non-failure cards; `[Ask]` on an ApprovalCard = `reply <approval-id> <text>` routed to the requesting job's task turn; `ask <provider>` (**D23**) is a bounded one-shot with no tools at `sensitivity=owner-private` by default (`--file=` raises it to the deliverable's class), stored as `deliverables(kind=report)` and rendered as a DeliverableCard with a cost strip — **P3 for HTTP/local lanes only** (`utility_call`: `ollama-local`, `anthropic-utility`; `cerebras-free`/`groq-free` once D18 is approved; `gemini-free` refused at owner-private), **P4 for `ask codex`**, which runs through `codex_cli.py` — as `_aicodex` via the wrapper, in an empty copy dir, under the stream lease, EnvScrubber, at the `public` ceiling until the D4 attestation row exists (§2.5 floor) — and never a special-cased exec; until P4 `ask codex` answers `provider_refused{reason: no_executor}`, and from P4 `provider_refused{reason: blocked}` until the §8.2(b) tripwire is green (`test_grammar`); **`lane hold <lane>`** parks a lane by owner decision (§6: durable `quota:lane:<lane>:hold_until`; already-popped jobs finish; newly due rows enqueue but park as `job_held{reason=owner_hold}`; `/queue` and the QuotaStrip show `lane atlas HELD by owner until 08:00`; a hold is not a schedule edit, so `governed=true` rows need no LOOP.md §7 proposal) and `lane release` re-promotes at the lane head — the week-one "hold atlas until tomorrow, I need the window" lever that `schedule pause` (per-row), `providers pause anthropic` (stops the owner too), `mute --lane` (cards only) and `/clear` (destructive) did not give; `job <id> --verdicts` renders the VerdictCard (§4.2) — the surface an owner needs in order to *read* a dissent before adjudicating it, which round 2's `[Uphold] [Dismiss]` path assumed and never provided; `tasks [--blocked|--open]` is the grammar verb behind the PWA's Tasks view and the existing `/api/tasks` routes (`web.py:526,547`), which had no verb at all; `help [<verb>]` is rendered from the verb/flag table, not hand-written (§2.2); `send` = Telegram document; `digest` = the DailyDigest on demand; `alerts` = outbox history; `schedule list` paginates in the renderer (41 rows × next fire + last outcome + adherence exceed 4096 chars; today `telegram_bot.py:547-552` prints every row in one message). The same names appear in the §2.2 intents list and the §4.2 surface table.

Telegram: bare text = `task`; `/status`, `/tasks`, `/chat`, `/god`, `/resume`, `/projects` unchanged; `/clear` requires a confirm button; the four ghost commands (`/cancel <prefix>`, `/status <prefix>`, `/proposals`) become real and `/rate` leaves the help text (endpoint kept; 👍/👎 only on DeliverableCards). `ai-mcp` (stdio, `mcp<2`) exposes **read-only** tools only: `ai_jobs, ai_job, ai_job_stream, ai_queue, ai_quota, ai_cost, ai_deliverables, ai_schedule_list, ai_atlas`. No `ai_run/ai_approve/ai_cancel` inside a model context (judge 3 fatal flaw); mutating verbs require Telegram or the PWA. **`ai_watch` is renamed `ai_job_stream` because `watch` is state-changing on the other surfaces** (round 3: `watch <job>` posts a JobCard for any job and *subscribes* it to edits until terminal — an outbox row plus `notifications.external_ref` — so a read-only tool of that name either did nothing durable or broke the read-only rule): `ai_job_stream` returns a phase snapshot and never subscribes; the subscribing `watch` is Telegram/PWA/CLI only. **Reads are sensitivity-gated** (the `sensitivity` machinery otherwise governs only executor routing, and `ai_job`/`ai_watch` would hand audit tails — `tool_use` inputs up to 2000 chars, `tool_result` previews, thinking — of proprietary atlas jobs to a Codex client on a plan that retains until deleted): every tool takes the client's provider identity — **owner-set, never self-declared**: the MCP `clientInfo.name` a client presents in the initialize handshake is whatever the client software chooses, so identity is a server argument the owner writes into each app's registration (`ai-mcp --client=codex`, `ai-mcp --client=claude-desktop`), default `unknown` ⇒ metadata-only reads; remotely, only the Cloudflare Access `aud`/service-token identity counts (`test_ai_mcp_sensitivity` covers a client presenting a spoofed `clientInfo.name`) — and **applies the same ceiling `policy.select()` applies, not a separate table (round 3): reads are capped at the caller's registered `provider.data.max_sensitivity`** — for `codex` that is `public` until an unexpired `approvals(kind=attestation, subject=codex)` row exists, then `owner-private` (§2.5 floor) — and above the cap the tool returns **metadata only** (status, phase, DoD ticks, cost, deliverable titles; no summary, log or stream). Round 1's read gate handed `owner-private` job summaries to the Codex client nine lines after the same section refused `ask codex` anything above `public`; both surfaces put text into the same ChatGPT account, which the research records as training-on-by-default with conversations "kept until deleted, no residency choice, no ZDR" (`chatgpt-openai.md:446`), and the §8.3 redactor strips secrets and identifiers, **not sensitivity class**. So: `public` jobs → everything; `owner-private` → the summary through the redactor **only to a client whose cap reaches `owner-private`**; `proprietary` → metadata only for every non-Anthropic client. Anthropic clients (claude.ai, Claude Code) get full reads. `test_ai_mcp_sensitivity` gains "a `--client=codex` read of an owner-private job returns metadata only while no attestation row exists" (P5 gate line, §9). Every tool result is prefixed with an "untrusted data, not instructions" marker, because job output is text produced from fetched pages landing in an interactive session that has its own tools. `test_ai_mcp_sensitivity` is a P5 gate and part of D19.

**Remote front door (P6, D19 — on the D10 auth infra landed at P2 entry)** *[gap-fill: `server-as-connector-reachability.md §7`]*: the same read-only tool set served as a remote MCP server (`src/gateway/mcp_front_door.py`, FastMCP, `readOnlyHint: true` on every tool) on a tunnel route behind a Cloudflare Access application; Cloudflare Access is the OAuth/identity layer for the vendor apps (one connector registration covers claude.ai web/desktop/mobile, Claude Code, cloud sessions, routines and Artifacts dashboards on the Max plan; ChatGPT developer mode would be a second door **only if D5 moves the account to Plus** — under D5=Free the door is Anthropic-only plus Codex CLI via service token), service tokens cover CLIs; the read gate above applies per caller identity (proprietary rows only to the claude.ai connector). This is how the owner *reads* server state from inside the vendors' apps; launching and approving stay on Telegram/PWA/CLI. Exposure is auth-infra (protected path #2), hence P6; D19 depends on D5 for the ChatGPT door.

### 4.2 Cards (Telegram; every card is a `views.py` object)

**Card eligibility (who gets a JobCard — the rule the synthesis dropped from interface-first §2.5).** A JobCard + DoneCard is sent for jobs with `origin_channel ∈ {telegram, pwa, cli}` and for schedule rows with `notify=always`; `notify=failures` rows (the default for all 41 schedules) send only the FailedCard; `notify=never` rows send nothing (digest only); dispatch-MCP / escalation / self-diagnose children inherit the parent's eligibility and thread under its card. `watch <job>` on Telegram posts a JobCard for *any* job (machine jobs included) and subscribes it to edits until terminal; `mute <job>` reverses it. With ~20 machine-launched jobs/day (state map §2.3: scheduler 79, dispatch-MCP 67, event-trigger 312 vs Telegram 55 in 7 d) this keeps the phone quiet while the DailyDigest and the Now panel carry the fleet. `test_views`: "a scheduler job with `notify=failures` produces no JobCard row in the outbox; `watch` on it does".

- **JobCard** (one message per job, edited on phase change and otherwise coalesced to ≤1 edit / 10 s *[graft: multi-bot §2.1]*): one sequence, defined once in `views.py`: `queued (lane owner · pos 1 (P2+) · est 0-2 min (P2+))` → `held` → `starting (anthropic/claude-opus-5 · workspace clone 3 s)` → `working (elapsed 3m40s / 30m · last: Bash pytest -q · files 3 · turn 12 (P2+) · 41k tok (P2+) · DoD 2/5 (P2+))` → `blocked` → `done` (at `jobs:done`: deliverables linked, verdict lines show "pending") → `reviewed` (at `jobs:reviewed`: verdict lines filled) | `failed`. **Every segment is marked with the phase that can produce it (round 3 — round 2 marked only the DoD segment, leaving the rest of the same two lines reading as P1 while the machinery behind them is P2):** `turn`/`tokens_so_far` come from `heartbeat`, `lane position` from `LLEN jobs:queue:<lane>`, the window strip from `quota_snapshots` — heartbeat, lane queues and migration 009 are all **P2** (§9). Either the `heartbeat{turn, last_tool, tokens_so_far, elapsed_s}` emitter moves into P1 (it is a 15 s task inside `_run_in_process` and the cheapest thing in the phase — **taken: it is in the P1 scope cell**, because with no heartbeat and edits only on phase change a 30-minute P1 job shows a card that does not move, the opposite of P1's promise) or the segments read `n/a` in P1. The `DoD m/n` segment stays **P2+** and shows only the live-evaluable subset defined in §2.7 (`lifecycle_marker_present`, `file_exists`/`glob_matched`, `tests_ran`, `budget_within`); the post-session checks (`git_pushed`, `claimed_files_exist`, `tests_green`, `url_200`, `db_row`) tick at `finalize_workspace` — the card never shows "0/n for the whole run, n/n at done" as if that were live. There is no separate "reviewing" phase that withholds "done": an Anthropic-lane commit is already ff-synced at `done`, and post-review (600 s) + grader (600 s) would otherwise show a pushed deliverable as "not done" for 20 minutes. **Notification semantics:** JobCard edits are silent (Telegram message edits do not push-notify); DoneCard, FailedCard and ApprovalCard are *new* messages sent as replies to the JobCard (they notify); the JobCard's final edit reads "done → see card below"; `notify=failures` schedules get only the FailedCard. Buttons **in P1 `[Cancel] [Details] [Mute]`; callback `j:<8-char>:c|d|m`** (≤64 bytes) — `[Mute]` is in the button row and the callback vocabulary rather than described in prose the callbacks could not address, and **`[Open]` is P2+**, because the PWA it targets ships in P2 (round 3). Message id persisted in `notifications.external_ref` so a bot restart loses nothing.
- **DoneCard**: verdict lines (gate, post-review, critic CONCUR|DISSENT — "pending" at `jobs:done`, filled on `jobs:reviewed`; the card says "done · verdicts pending", never "completed & verified", before the belts finish), `done_verdict` with DoD ticks, deliverables with deep links, cost strip *[graft: multi-bot §2.4]*, `[Reopen] [👍] [👎] [Send file] [Open]`.
- **FailedCard**: error class, `terminal_reason`, escalation hop, `[Retry] [Details] [Mute today]`; fires for scheduled jobs too (silent today, state map §5.7).
- **ApprovalCard**: from an `approvals` row (deploy parks here instead of failing), `[Approve] [Reject] [Ask]`. **Dissent variant:** for `approvals(kind=question, subject=verdict)` — the row a high-severity DISSENT on a governor-feeding memo opens (§5.3) — the buttons are `[Uphold] [Dismiss] [Ask]`, writing `verdicts.adjudication` + `adjudicated_by=owner`; callback `v:<verdict8>:u|d`. Round 2 routed the owner to `[Uphold] [Dismiss]` while this catalogue defined only `[Approve] [Reject] [Ask]`, so the adjudication path that drives the whole grader ladder had no renderer.
- **VerdictCard** (new in round 3; `job <id> --verdicts`, `GET /api/jobs/{id}/verdicts`, P4): per stage — grader provider/model, verdict, score with its scale, each finding's severity/location/text **through `untrusted_envelope()`**, adjudication state, `[Uphold] [Dismiss]`. Without it the owner was asked to adjudicate a dissent (upheld/dismissed drives `dissent_precision` and the ladder, §5.4) with no place to read the finding text: the only rendering was the DoneCard one-liner "critic (codex): DISSENT (2 findings, 1 high)" and an aggregate on the scoreboard.
- **DeliverableCard**: title, kind, 500-char preview, `[Send file]` (Telegram document ≤50 MB) is the **only** file action in P1; `[Open]` → PWA `/app/d/<id>` appears in **P2** (the phase that ships the PWA) and stays secondary even then, because a Telegram deep link opens in Telegram's in-app browser, which carries neither the paste-bootstrapped bearer nor a Cloudflare Access cookie — so it hits `web.py:_check_auth`'s Basic prompt (`web.py:49-63`, 401 + `WWW-Authenticate: Basic`) and, after D10, an Access login as well. **The PWA is a laptop/home-screen surface; `[Send file]` stays the primary file path indefinitely** (round 3 — "frictionless after D10" was not true for the in-app browser).
- **ScheduleCard**: next fire, last outcome, adherence flag (DARK/NEVER_RAN/STUCK/FAILURE_STREAK/LATE/MISSING), `[Run now] [Pause] [Edit]`.
- **QuotaStrip / ProviderPanel**: `anthropic 5h ok · resets 14:10 · 7d 58 % (vendor, 40 min) · codex 5h 12 % · credits $0 · gemini 88/250 · local ok 5.1 GB free · lane atlas opus 62 % / sonnet 31 % of week · lane atlas HELD by owner until 08:00` — the "(inferred)"/"est." marker is mandatory whenever `source=inferred`; a used-% for Claude appears with its source and age when a vendor `seven_day`/`seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet` event or the status-line file carried it (`vendor`, `statusline`), "inferred" when the newest such reading is older than 24 h, "uncalibrated" only when older than 7 d. **ProviderPanel is split by phase (round 3 — a P2 exit criterion cannot depend on P3/P4 components): the P2 subset** is one line per provider — `running n (job ids) · queued n · held n · today: jobs/grades/utility calls · last error <terminal_reason> <age>` (from `provider_selected`/`executor_started` + `jobs.resolved_provider`) plus the QuotaStrip, and that is what the P2 exit reconciles against `jobs` rows; **pause/resume arrives with the P3 registry and breaker state + ladder with migration 010 in P4** (`provider_qualifications`).
- **DailyDigest 07:15** replaces the bash `schedule-monitor.sh` DM via `python -m src.notify digest` on the same launchd timer — the only phone surface for 41 schedules under the `notify=failures` default, so it is defined, not named. Fields: header (`date · 5h ok · 7d 58 % (vendor, 40 min)`); **Ran since last digest: n ok / n failed / n held / n skipped(misfire)** with the failed rows named (`terminal_reason`, escalation hop); **Adherence** findings (DARK/NEVER_RAN/STUCK/FAILURE_STREAK/LATE/MISSING — a superset of today's script); **Deliverables produced** (title → deep link / `send` id); **Open approvals / blocked jobs** (age); **Next 6 fires**; lane holds in force. **Quiet rule (preserves `schedule-monitor.sh:7-9, :17, :47-58` semantics):** send when there is at least one failure, finding, deliverable or open approval since the last digest; otherwise silent, except the Sunday all-clear; never more than one digest per 12 h unless a finding is new. Example: `📋 Fri 26 Sep · 5h ok · 7d 58 % (vendor, 40 min)` / `ran: 9 ok · 1 failed (atlas-daily-brief: api_error 529 → L1 queued) · 0 held` / `adherence: LATE alpha-scout (+42 min)` / `deliverables: swing-2026-09-25.md (d-3f9c)` / `open: ap-3f9c deploy (2 h)` / `next: 08:00 atlas-report · 09:00 alpha-scout · …`. `test_views`: "no findings, no failures, weekday ⇒ no digest row; Sunday ⇒ all-clear row".
- **CostCard** (Monday 08:00 + `/cost`; also the monthly card on the first Sunday): by provider × lane × skill top-5 (`cost_usd_list`, week-over-week delta), per-family budget bar (Opus / Sonnet: used vs `weekly_budget`, 80 % lane warnings), `purpose=build` tokens this week, the ordinary-usage notice state (§2.8), Codex `credits` balance and `spendControlReached`, R2 bucket size, Cerebras trial balance if `cost_class: trial`.
- **AtlasCard** (`/atlas`): one row per vertical — `last run · status (ok/stale/blocked) · last decision · gate status · provisioning gaps (env_required keys missing)` from `resolved_provider/model`, `schedule_rollup` and the read-only `*.runs` role.
- **AlertHistory** (`/alerts`): `kind · severity · subject · sent_at · delivery status (sent/failed/attempts)` from the `notifications` outbox, newest first, paginated.

```
▶ RUNNING  c6cfbf6b · atlas-swing-research · lane atlas (watched)
   anthropic/claude-opus-5 · high · workspace 7f2a…
   3m40s / 30m · last: Bash pytest -q · files 3 · turn 12 · 41k tok       ← turn/tokens from heartbeat (P1 scope cell)
   DoD 2/4  ✅ marker  ✅ files_exist  ⬜ git_pushed  ⬜ citations_min        ← P2+: live subset; the two ⬜ tick at finalize_workspace
   window 5h ok · resets 14:10 · 7d 58 % (vendor, 40 min) · lane atlas opus 62 % of week   ← window strip P2+ (5-h status/resets from quota.py in P1)
   [Cancel] [Details] [Mute]                                              ← [Open] is P2+ (the PWA ships in P2)

✅ DONE  c6cfbf6b · atlas-swing-research · 6m12s · $0 marginal ($3.49 list-equiv · 2.40M cache-read · 111k cache-write(1h) · 47k out)

   done: verified (4/4 checks · 2 deliverables)

⚠️ FAILED  1a9e42c0 · atlas-daily-brief · scheduler · 2m03s
```

Phase model (single source in `views.py`; same strings on every surface) *[interface-first §2.5]*:

| Phase | Trigger | What the owner sees | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `queued` | `enqueue_job` | lane, position, estimated wait from p50 of running jobs | `LLEN jobs:queue:<lane>`, `jobs.started_at` |
| `held` | provider-window gate | reason + next check | `job_held` |
| `starting` | `job_started` | provider/model resolved, clone status, queue wait | `provider_selected`, `executor_started`, `workspace_created` |
| `working` | first executor event, then `heartbeat` | turn, last tool, files, tokens, elapsed/timeout, DoD ticks, window % | `heartbeat`, `tool_use`, `dod_tick`, `quota_snapshot` |
| `blocked` | `approval_requested` / `task_question` | what is asked + buttons; `awaiting_since` age | `approvals` row |
| `done` | `jobs:done` (after `workspace_synced` on success) | `done_verdict` + DoD ticks, deliverables, cost strip; verdict lines "pending" — the card says "done · verdicts pending", never "completed & verified" | columns, `done_verdict` |
| `reviewed` | `jobs:reviewed` (after post-review + graders) | verdict lines filled (gate, post-review, critic) | `merge_gate_verdict`, `grade_done`, `verdicts` |
| `failed` | `job_failed` | `terminal_reason`, escalation hop, `[Retry]` | columns |

Surface coverage (which view renders which object):

| View object | Telegram | PWA | `ai` CLI | `ai-mcp` (read-only) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| JobCard / DoneCard / FailedCard | edited message | Jobs list + detail | `--watch` lines | `ai_job_stream` (snapshot only — never subscribes, §4.1) |
| ApprovalCard / approvals list | buttons; `/approvals` | Approvals inbox | `ai approvals`, `ai approve` | — (deliberately absent) |
| VerdictCard | ApprovalCard dissent variant `[Uphold] [Dismiss]` | Job detail → Verdicts | `ai job <id> --verdicts` | — |
| Tasks list | `/tasks` | Tasks view | `ai tasks` | `ai_jobs` (job-shaped only) |
| help | `/help` | in-app help sheet | `ai help [<verb>]` | — |
| DeliverableCard | preview + Open/Send file | Deliverables browser | `ai open` prints path/URL | `ai_deliverables` |
| ScheduleCard / board | list + per-row buttons | week board | table | `ai_schedule_list` |
| QuotaStrip / ProviderPanel / held queue | one-line strip; `/queue` | Now + Providers | `ai quota`, `ai queue` | `ai_quota`, `ai_queue` |
| CostCard / Scoreboard | weekly card; `/scoreboard` | Cost + Scoreboard | `ai cost`, `ai scoreboard` | `ai_cost` |
| AtlasCard | card | Atlas view | `ai atlas` | `ai_atlas` |
| AlertHistory / DailyDigest | `/alerts`, `/digest` | Alerts view | `ai alerts`, `ai digest` | — |
| Ask answer (D23) | DeliverableCard | Deliverables browser | `ai ask` | — |

### 4.3 PWA `/app` (static SPA under `static/app/`, no build step, installable)

Views: **Now** (running/queued/held by lane, QuotaStrip, next 6 fires, open approvals), **Jobs** (filterable; detail = live transcript, tool timeline, guard denials, tokens/cost, provider served vs requested, cancel/approve/reply), **Tasks** (turns, plan DAG from `payload.depends_on`, evaluator rounds, `awaiting_since` age), **Schedules** (week board, presets like `weekdays 17:30 America/New_York`, run-now/pause/edit, adherence), **Providers** (windows, breakers, ladder), **Cost**, **Deliverables**, **Approvals**, **Atlas** ("is any of this working"), **Alerts** (outbox history), **Proposals**, **Scoreboard**. **Origin, named (round 3 — it was unstated, and the default is worse than the intent):** the PWA is served by FastAPI at **`dashboard.chrispiserchia.com/app`**, behind the vhost D10's Access application covers, which means `web.py` gains a `StaticFiles` mount for `static/app/` (it has none today). Without that mount `static/app/` would be reachable only through Caddy's **apex** `file_server` (`Caddyfile:18-26`, `root * …/static`) — a hostname with no Access — and that same apex block proxies `handle /api/* → localhost:8080`, i.e. **a second copy of every §4.6 route behind the one static bearer**. Auth: the existing shared token stays; the PWA bootstraps it once per device (paste → `localStorage`, `ai login`-style) and the live stream uses `fetch()` + `ReadableStream` with the bearer header so the SSE token-in-URL (`web.py:600-640`) is retired. Telegram deep links (`[Open]`) never carry that bearer — the in-app browser shares no storage with the installed PWA — so `[Send file]` is the primary file action indefinitely, not only in P1 (§4.2). **D10 is a security prerequisite, not a deep-link convenience** (§12 D10): the dashboard vhost is proxied on the tunnel with no Cloudflare Access (`Caddyfile:34-36`), `_check_auth` is one static shared token (`web.py:49-63`, Bearer or Basic; **unset ⇒ open**), and the spec routes deploy approvals, `providers pause|resume`, `provider_promote` and training attestations through it. **D10's contents therefore include a reader for the identity §4.5 verifies:** `_check_auth` gains a verified `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` path (JWKS fetch + `aud`/identity check) and the decide routes persist the `aud` + subject on the `approvals` row as the `decided_via='pwa'|'cli'` evidence the deploy gate checks — without it the `aud` clause in §4.5 has no reader at all, `decided_via ∈ {pwa, cli}` can never become acceptable, and `403 needs_cf_access` is a permanent state that someone eventually loosens (round 3: D10's stated contents were only "CF Access on the dashboard vhost, SSE cookie, VAPID keys", while §4.6 still said "every route takes the bearer token through the existing `_check_auth`" and promised `web.py:49-63` would not be touched — the two cannot both hold). **D10 is a hard *P2-entry* gate, not a P1-exit gate** (round 3: P1 ships neither the PWA nor the CLI — both are in P2 — so there is no PWA/CLI decision for it to gate in P1, and the exposure is already closed for P1 by the rule P1 itself ships: decision routes accept `decided_via=telegram` only). CF Access, an SSE cookie and Web Push VAPID keys are one owner-approved auth PR (D10) plus the CF Access application + policy the owner creates by hand **over every hostname that proxies `/api/*`, the apex included** (§12a row 7b). The old inline dashboard (`web.py:646-847`) stays until P5.

### 4.4 `ai` CLI

`src/cli/__main__.py` (stdlib `argparse`): REST client over the §4.6 routes (it runs on the owner's laptop, not only on the Mini); `ai login` stores the `WEB_AUTH_TOKEN` in the Keychain, and **`ai login --service-token` stores `CF-Access-Client-Id`/`CF-Access-Client-Secret` beside it so every `ai` request sends both headers** — without it the CLI is locked out from the moment Access lands: a browser gets a redirect, a bearer-only CLI gets that redirect instead of a 401 it can answer, and round 2 scheduled the service token at §12a row 20 (P5/P6) while the CLI ships in P2, darking "launch from the laptop" for three phases (round 3: the token moves into §12a row 7b, with the Access application, and the Access policy must include a service-token rule for `/api/*`). Verb coverage: `ai jobs|job|tasks|queue|quota|cost|providers|deliverables|schedule|atlas|approvals|approve|reject|reply|alerts|scoreboard|digest|ask|watch|mute|reopen|retry|cancel|send|open|lane|project|help`; `ai task "…" --watch` prints the JobCard phase lines and tails `GET /api/jobs/{id}/log`; `ai job <id> --log` consumes the same streamed route (never a local file tail); `ai jobs|queue|quota|cost|providers|deliverables|schedule|atlas|approvals|alerts|scoreboard|digest|ask` render `views.py` tables. Owner terminal work stops appearing as ad-hoc `created_by` strings (`owner-terminal`, `owner-dispatch`, …): `origin_channel=cli`.

### 4.5 Notifications and approvals

`src/notify/` outbox with renderers; retries with backoff; `notice_sent/failed` recorded; per-schedule `notify ∈ {never, failures, always}` keeps machines quiet (default `failures`). **Notice kinds, enumerated (round 3): `possible_credit_overflow`, `ordinary_usage_exceeded`, the 80 % lane-budget warning, `provider_enforcement` (§2.4) and — new — `lane_held` and `window_red`, severity `high`, debounced one per lane per reason per window and cleared on `promote_held`.** The last two exist because "the fleet DMs the hold instead of going silent" — P5's headline drill and the direct answer to the 2026-08-17 governor-dark pattern — had **no mechanism**: a hold produced an audit event (`job_held{reason, next_check}`, audited once per reason) and a JobCard phase, but JobCard edits are silent and machine rows default to `notify=failures` and get no JobCard at all, so for the 41 scheduled rows a red window or an exhausted lane was invisible until the next DailyDigest (up to 24 h, and suppressed unless something else qualified). They ship with holds in **P2**, join the AlertHistory kinds, and the P2 exit adds "the first hold in a window DMs within 60 s"; `_error_safe`'s self-diagnose auto-dispatch is capped by the breaker (C26). `DeployNeedsApproval` (`main.py:492`) creates `approvals(kind=deploy)` + ApprovalCard with deploy-director's what's-deploying summary. The approve path couples at the trigger, not the payload (a payload key would be forgeable — `approval_id` is not in `mcp_dispatch._STRIPPED_PAYLOAD_KEYS`, `mcp_dispatch.py:38`, and a dispatching session's payload reaches `enqueue_job` verbatim): `approve` enqueues the deploy with `created_by="approval:<id>:<channel>"`; `run_session`'s deploy gate (`session.py:872-881`) resolves the approvals row (status=approved, subject kind/project match, **`decided_via == 'telegram'` or a recorded Cloudflare Access `aud` claim** — `pwa`/`cli` decisions count only after D10, because until then the PWA/CLI bearer is one static token on a public hostname, §4.3) and verifies the row's bot-only artefact — **either a Keychain-held bot key that signs on request over `events:control`, or a runner-side re-verification of the recorded `update_id`/`callback_query.id` against the Telegram Bot API; never an HMAC under a secret in `.env`**, which any unhooked session with `DATABASE_URL` can also read (§0a row 10) — and only then calls `delivery.deploy_permitted(deliv, trigger="human")` — `deploy_permitted`'s signature (`delivery.py:75-101`) is untouched. The same `decided_via` rule applies to `providers pause|resume`, `provider_promote` and `attestation` rows. The same P1 PR adds `dispatch-mcp`, `deploy-autopilot`, `bot-error-handler` to `_AUTONOMOUS_PREFIXES` (`delivery.py:42-46` — today those producers classify as `human`) and `approval_id` to the dispatch strip list; `test_intents`: "a dispatch-mcp job with `approval_id` in its payload is still autonomous". `TASK_QUESTION`/choices become `approvals(kind=question|choice)` so `awaiting_since` is set and stale asks age visibly. `AskUserQuestion` leaves the default tool list (`session.py:706-709`; documented to hang jobs).

### 4.6 REST surface (the PWA and `ai` CLI are clients of these; `web.py:49-63` stays untouched)

| Route | Method | Intent | View object | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/api/jobs` | **POST** | `task` — **the owner's primary verb, and it had no row** (round 3; the route already exists at `web.py:377`) | JobCard | P1 |
| `/api/jobs?running\|queued\|held\|blocked\|failed\|lane=\|provider=` | GET | `jobs` | JobCard rows | P1 |
| `/api/tasks` · `/api/tasks/{id}` | GET | `tasks` — the source the P2 PWA's **Tasks** view is declared to read (routes already exist, `web.py:526,547`) | Tasks list | P2 |
| `/api/jobs/{id}` · `/api/jobs/{id}/log` (streamed, bearer header; supersedes token-in-URL SSE) · `/api/jobs/{id}/cancel` · `/api/jobs/{id}/retry` | GET · GET · POST · POST | `job`, `watch`, `cancel`, `retry` | JobCard/DoneCard/FailedCard | P1 |
| `/api/approvals` · `/api/approvals/{id}/decide` · `/api/approvals/{id}/reply` | GET · POST · POST | `approvals`, `approve\|reject`, `[Ask]` | ApprovalCard | P1 |
| `/api/deliverables` · `/api/deliverables/{id}` · `/api/deliverables/{id}/file` | GET | `deliverables`, `open`, `send` | DeliverableCard | P1 |
| `/api/queue` · `/api/quota` · `/api/cost?by=&window=` | GET | `queue`, `quota`, `cost` | held queue, QuotaStrip, CostCard | P2 |
| `/api/schedules` · `/api/schedules/{name}` · `/api/schedules/{name}/{run\|pause\|resume}` | GET · POST (**`schedule add`** — round 3: neither the PWA nor the CLI could add a schedule in any phase) · PATCH · POST | `schedule_*` | ScheduleCard/board | P2 (run/pause/resume P1; **`add` + the preset parser P2**) |
| `/api/proposals/{id}/approve` · `/api/jobs/{id}/reopen` · `/api/jobs/{id}/mute` · `/api/projects/{slug}/{status\|deploy\|logs}` | POST · POST · POST · GET/POST | `proposals approve`, `reopen`, `mute`, `project_*` — grammar verbs that had no route (round 3) | — | P2 |
| `/api/jobs/{id}/verdicts` | GET | `job --verdicts` | VerdictCard | P4 |
| `/api/providers` | GET | `providers status` (counts + QuotaStrip subset) | ProviderPanel (P2 subset) | **P2** |
| `/api/providers/{id}/{pause\|resume}` · `/api/scoreboard` | POST · GET | `providers pause\|resume`, `scoreboard` | ProviderPanel (full), Scoreboard | P3 · P4 |
| `/api/alerts` · `/api/digest` · `/api/atlas` · `/api/proposals` | GET | `alerts`, `digest`, `atlas`, `proposals` | AlertHistory, DailyDigest, AtlasCard | P2/P5 |
| `/api/ask` | POST | `ask` (D23) | DeliverableCard | P3 (HTTP/local lanes) · P4 (`codex`) |
| `/api/lanes/{lane}/{hold\|release}` | POST | `lane hold`, `lane release` | QuotaStrip / held queue | P2 |

Every route takes the bearer token through the existing `_check_auth` (`web.py:49-63`: one token, Bearer or Basic; **unset ⇒ every route above is open**, which is why `WEB_AUTH_TOKEN` is a P0 runbook row, §12a row 6); mutating routes are the same intents Telegram uses, so `test_intents` covers them once. **Until D10 lands, the decision routes — `/api/approvals/{id}/decide`, `/api/providers/{id}/{pause|resume}`, `provider_promote`, attestation — return `403 needs_cf_access` from the PWA/CLI** (`decided_via ∈ {pwa, cli}` is not accepted by the deploy gate, §4.5); launch/cancel/retry/lane-hold stay available on the bearer, **but `--model=`/`--second-opinion=` on those routes are refused `needs_cf_access` pre-D10, because the owner-override regime *does* widen authority** — it honours any registered non-metered provider irrespective of chain membership or ladder state and bypasses the `canary_share` cap (§2.5 (i)); without that carve-out a holder of the static token could put a Codex canary writer on a `research-read` job from a public hostname with no Access, through `task` or `retry`, both of which this paragraph classifies as harmless (`test_intents` case).

## 5) Effectiveness

### 5.1 Definition of done — the Deliverable Contract *[core: outcome-first §3.1]*

Declared in frontmatter `done:`, overridable per job via `payload.contract`, resolved once at session start (`contract_resolved`), and checked by the runner (`ScriptExecutor`) — never by the model.

| Check | Args | Runs | Typical class |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lifecycle_marker_present` | final text ends in one of `TASK_COMPLETE:` \| `TASK_QUESTION:` \| `EVAL_PASS:` \| `EVAL_FAIL:` (task-bearing jobs only; `EVAL_*` satisfy it for class `_evaluate`, `skills/_evaluate/SKILL.md:77,90,96`); `TASK_QUESTION:` ⇒ `done_verdict=n/a`, phase `blocked`, excluded from `dod_pass_rate` — asking the owner a question is correct behaviour, not a DoD failure | summary parse | all (default) |
| `claimed_files_exist` | paths named in final text or Write/Edit `tool_use` targets exist post ff-sync | fs | all (default) |
| `budget_within` | tokens / turns / seconds ceilings | columns | all (default) |
| `file_exists`, `glob_matched`, `sections_present`, `word_range`, `citations_min` | path/glob, sections[], min | fs + regex over the file | research-read, memos |
| `git_pushed`, `changelog_touched` | — | ff-sync SHA (`workspaces.py:247-270`), diff names | code-* |
| `tests_ran` | pattern | transcript: a `Bash` `tool_use` matching `pytest\|npm test` with a non-failing `tool_result` (weaker; honest) | code-* default |
| `tests_green`, `lint_green` | cmd | runner executes cmd in the clone before ff-sync (the clone outlives `run_session` from P3, §2.7; opt-in; needs `command:` and the project venv); over a clone written by a **non-Anthropic** executor the command runs inside that executor's own sandbox (`codex exec --sandbox workspace-write`) or the check degrades to `tests_ran` — never in the runner process | code-server, delivery |
| `url_200`, `healthcheck_green` | url | HTTP **in the runner process** (`httpx`, hardcoded host allowlist in `src/contracts/`) — never a `ScriptExecutor` command, whose Seatbelt profile is network-off without exception (§2.4) | project delivery |
| `db_row` | read-only SQL via a read-only atlas role (P5) | **in the runner process**, on a read-only DSN the runner holds — never a `ScriptExecutor` command (round 3: Postgres is localhost *TCP* and the Script profile denies all network including loopback, §2.4; the same precedent as `url_200`) | trading (`swing.runs` status, `value.theses` count) |
| `json_field` | `{path, field, predicate}` | fs + JSON | trading constitution (`metrics.json.dsr` present and numeric, `worst_fold_sharpe` present, lifetime N stated — alpha-lab PROTOCOL §3) |
| `jsonl_before` | `{file, match, before: {file, marker}}` | fs: the matching JSONL line's `ts` < the marker entry's timestamp | trading constitution (`trials.jsonl` line with the cycle's `params_hash` lands before the LEDGER `VERDICT` — PROTOCOL §4: "I didn't log the rejects" invalidates the cycle) |
| `tools_absent`, `dates_within` | `{names: [WebSearch, WebFetch]}`; `{cutoff}` | transcript: no `tool_use` of the named kinds; dates quoted in the artefact ≤ cutoff | trading evaluation runs, memos |
| `command` (per-vertical validator) | tracked script under the vertical | `ScriptExecutor` in the clone (e.g. `quantlab.trials.distinct_n`, PROTOCOL §4) | trading constitution; lands as `<vertical>/evaluation/dod.yml` through LOOP.md §7 (D11) |
| `schema_valid` | JSON schema over structured output | — | utility, grading |

Default contract for every job without a `done:` block — **the single source for what `done_verdict` means** (§2.5's frontmatter comment and D17 defer to this sentence): the default contract runs; a pass yields `done_verdict=unverified` (the runner observed nothing the skill promised), a fail yields `failed_checks`; `verified` requires a skill-declared or `payload.contract` contract. The default is class-aware so it does not drown the signal in noise (512 `task_complete` events against 646 `job_completed` today — roughly one in five completed jobs has no marker): `chat`/`utility-*` → `budget_within`; every other class → `claimed_files_exist ∧ budget_within`, with `lifecycle_marker_present` added only when the job has a `task_id` (the marker protocol is a task-lifecycle contract, `session.py:405-460`; round 1's `marker_present`/`no_marker` pair would have graded every `_evaluate` run — which ends in `EVAL_PASS:`/`EVAL_FAIL:` and always carries a `task_id`, `main.py:949-975` — and every legitimate `TASK_QUESTION:` as `failed_checks`; `test_contracts`: "an `_evaluate` job ending in EVAL_FAIL and a task job ending in TASK_QUESTION are not `failed_checks`"). Outcome: `done_verdict ∈ {verified, unverified, failed_checks, n/a}`; `jobs.status` is untouched. `on_fail` default is `flag` fleet-wide (card + scoreboard); `fail` only for deploy-verify skills (`deploy-director: verify`) where the check *is* the deliverable; trading-ledger checks flag, never fail, so loops cannot go dark on a flaky probe (judge 1 flaw on outcome-first). `fix_round` reuses the evaluator fix loop (`main.py:949-985`, max 2) when a skill opts in. A `done:` block lands on the 10 highest-volume skills in P1 and on every routable class before its lane leaves shadow.

Example (research-report; the optional deliverables block is the only thing the model writes, everything else is runner-observed):

```
<<<DELIVERABLES
- file: projects/research/spy-liquidity-2026-09-24.md
- url: https://atlas.chrispiserchia.com/reports/spy-liquidity
DELIVERABLES>>>
TASK_COMPLETE: liquidity memo committed (9d3ebcf) with 7 cited sources.
```

```json
// <job>.evidence.json (runner-written)
{"contract": "research-report@done", "checks": [
  {"name": "lifecycle_marker_present", "pass": true, "detail": "TASK_COMPLETE"}, {"name": "claimed_files_exist", "pass": true, "detail": "1/1"},
  {"name": "citations_min", "pass": true, "detail": "7 >= 3"}, {"name": "git_pushed", "pass": true, "detail": "9d3ebcf"},
  {"name": "budget_within", "pass": true, "detail": "92k tok / 200k; 6m12s / 30m"}],
 "deliverables": [{"kind": "file", "path": "projects/research/spy-liquidity-2026-09-24.md", "sha": "…"}, {"kind": "commit", "sha": "9d3ebcf"}, {"kind": "url", "url": "https://…"}],
 "diff": {"files": 1, "insertions": 214, "deletions": 0}, "done_verdict": "verified"}
```

### 5.2 Evidence

`<job>.evidence.json` from (a) check results, (b) auto-detected deliverables (Write/Edit targets under `projects/**`, `docs/**`, `research/**`; ff-sync SHA; PR URLs in final text; `<job>.summary.md`), (c) an *optional* `<<<DELIVERABLES … DELIVERABLES>>>` block (same mechanism as `<<<TASK_PLAN>>>`, `session.py:405-460`; no mandatory `<<<EVIDENCE>>>` ceremony), (d) the workspace diff summary. `deliverables` rows are what cards link; the bundle is what graders read — never the writer's transcript alone.

### 5.3 Review belts — vendor ≠ writer, Anthropic the only LGTM

1. **Evidence checks** (no LLM).
2. **In-session gate**: the `code-review` subagent stays the INV-13 merge gate on Anthropic lanes; the **first line** of its result (`LGTM|CHANGES|BLOCKER` exactly — `skills/code-review/SKILL.md:128-130`; there is no `VERDICT:` line) is parsed from the `Task` tool_result whose matching `tool_use` targeted the `code-review` subagent, into `jobs.merge_gate_verdict` (P1 — the `verdicts` table is migration 009, so P1 fills only the column and the `verdicts(stage=merge_gate)` row is backfilled from it when 009 lands in P2); the 500-char `result_preview` (`session.py:1182`) always contains the first line, and P1 also records the full text as `subagent_result` (§2.7) so the findings are available for the Jaccard computation in item 5. Test: a deploy of a workspace-produced commit without a recorded `lgtm` is refused for non-Anthropic-written commits and warns (card) for Anthropic ones *[graft: hub §8 H2, narrowed]*.
3. **Post-review and the non-Anthropic merge gate** — two mechanisms, stated separately because "pre-push" was not true for either as first drafted:
   - **3a Anthropic lanes**: `review.py:247-256` (Anthropic-pinned, reviewer chosen by registry class not by the skill, C5) stays **post-push, flag-only** — workspace sessions push to origin themselves (`workspaces.py:157-166` re-points the clone's `origin` at the real remote; every atlas research skill ends with "rebase before push") and `sync_canonical` only ff-pulls afterwards (`workspaces.py:247-270`), so a review "before `sync_canonical`" is post-push/pre-ff-sync, not pre-push. The 600 s timeout stamps `review_outcome=timeout` + `post_review_flagged` (closes `main.py:616-626`). This matches the SYSTEM.md INV-13 row as corrected 2026-08-31. Making it pre-push would require withholding push credentials from the session and having the runner push — a separate design; **D8 is rewritten** to defer exactly that; the `REVIEW_PRE_PUSH` switch is deleted from §9 until D8 is un-deferred, because it would toggle nothing that exists.
   - **3b Non-Anthropic writer lanes (P4 `research-read` canary, P5-exit `code-project` canary; INV-21(c))**: the clone cannot push (network off, no credentials), so the gate runs before the runner publishes. It is **not** `review.py`'s 6-turn flag-only prompt but the `code-review` skill protocol — Anthropic, `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` body, 10 turns, read-only hooks — over the clone diff. **Mechanism (stated, because the slot it runs in matters):** it runs **in-process in the parent's post-step** — `review.run_code_review` gains parameters for the skill body, turn count and read-only hooks and runs under `asyncio.wait_for(600)`, stamping `verdicts(stage=merge_gate, grader_job_id=NULL)` — *not* as a second queued job: `_run_with_held_slot` releases the semaphore only after every post-step returns (`main.py:188-194`), so with prod `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS=2` two non-Anthropic writers waiting on queued review jobs would hold both slots while their reviews sat in the queue, a deadlock nothing times out. The gate session counts against `anthropic.max_sessions` and draws on the separate `gate_sessions: 1` budget (§2.5/§2.8), because the job loop gates *pops* and this session opens inside an already-popped job's post-step. **Fail-closed**: any outcome other than `lgtm` (`changes_requested`, `blocker`, `error`, `timeout`, stale head, empty diff) parks the clone (kept, as failures are today), opens a FailedCard and never publishes. **One exception, and it is a capacity case, not a verdict case (round 3):** a gate that cannot acquire a session within `_POST_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` stamps `verdicts(stage=merge_gate, verdict=skipped, skip_reason=session_cap)` and **re-queues the publish attempt** — a retryable skip. Stamping `timeout`, parking the clone and opening a FailedCard for a pure capacity shortage would fail a canary job that did nothing wrong *and* feed capacity noise into the P4 writer-ladder bars, the error class round 2 already excluded for `model_unavailable`; `skipped(session_cap)` is excluded from `lgtm_rate`/`dod_pass_rate` (§5.4). `pre_publish_gate = True` is unconditional in code for `provider != "anthropic"` (not env-readable, §2.5). After `lgtm` the runner applies the `publish_allowlist` (§2.5; a diff outside it is refused *before* the gate runs, so no review session is spent on it) and `publish.py` pushes and ff-syncs (§2.6). P4 entry criterion + test: "a Codex job can never produce a commit on `origin` without a `verdicts(stage=merge_gate, verdict=lgtm)` row, and never outside the allowlist". The INV-13/INV-21 SYSTEM.md rows are amended with this wording under D2 (owner sign-off, PR #1b).
4. **Grader / critic** (`src/runner/grading.py`, new post-step; **active only after D21**): picks `grader.provider != writer.provider` from the class policy; runs read-only on the evidence bundle + class rubric with structured output `{verdict ∈ CONCUR|DISSENT, score 0-10, confidence, findings[{severity, text, location, kind}], dod_items[]}` *[graft: multi-bot §2.3 vote schema]*; Codex `exec --sandbox read-only --output-schema grade.json` (**the exact `providers.yml: codex.models.grader` id verified on the Free account in `stream-0` at P4 entry — never the picker default and never "a Luna-class model", which §3 records as an assumption round 2 retracted**, `max_turns` bounded so one grade stays inside a single 5-h band); **`grading.py` also owns the `critic_mirror` sub-step** — it writes `<vertical>.critic_verdicts` immediately after the `verdicts(stage=critic)` insert and before `jobs:reviewed`, because `publish.py` runs six steps earlier in the chain and only on non-Anthropic writer lanes while `trading-research` is `pinned: true` (§2.6, §8.1); the **gemini-free grader input is defined narrowly** — the public-class memo text plus one fixed generic rubric string, no `evals/cases`, no skill body, no transcript, no DoD evidence beyond file names (the rubric/skill text is owner-authored and therefore `owner-private`, which the unpaid key would train on with human review; a redactor golden test covers this input); local only on the **redacted projection** (§8.3); bounded 600 s; error → `verdict=error` ("ungraded" on the card); quota → `skipped(quota)`, **never Anthropic**. **Grading population, sized from the prod table** (the classes above cover ~440 jobs/month — atlas-report 157, atlas-daily-brief 30, alpha-research 27, atlas-swing-trade 19, alpha-governor 15, atlas-*-research 23, … plus ~15 code-project — not "30-120", and atlas schedules burst at fixed ET slots inside one 5-h window): `grader_sampling` in `routing-policy.yml` grades every governor-feeding memo (`trading-research` critic), a fixed 10 % sample of other `research-read`/`code-project` jobs, capped at 6 per 5-h window via `quota:codex:counters` — **over that cap the reason is `skipped(sampling_cap)`, never `skipped(quota)`** (round 3: the graded population bursts at fixed ET slots inside one 5-h window, so our own cap fires precisely on the days the D5 measurement is taken, and labelling its skips `quota` made a self-imposed limit indistinguishable from a vendor 429 in the one number that decides whether the owner spends money); unsampled jobs are `skipped(sampled)`, so the scoreboard distinguishes "not sampled" from "our cap" from "vendor throttle". Expected volume ≈ 60–90 Codex runs/month, which ChatGPT Free ("explore quick coding tasks") may not carry — §13 presents Plus $20 as the probable D5 *capacity* outcome; the belt is scheduled volume and therefore rule-(b) accepted risk on plan auth regardless of tier (§3 Codex row). Kimi/MiniMax/DeepSeek/Qwen never grade Claude output (`crosscut-tos §5.2a`). A `DISSENT` with `severity=high` on a memo that feeds a governor opens `approvals(kind=question)` → owner card with `[Uphold] [Dismiss]`. **Dissent is adjudicated, not just recorded** (`verdicts.adjudication`, §2.3): (i) the governor's grade block cites `verdict_id` with `upheld|dismissed` (the LOOP.md §7 "cite the DISSENT" amendment, D11); (ii) the owner's DISSENT card buttons; (iii) a later `evidence_check` failure on the same job whose `kind` matches a finding auto-upholds it — so the scoreboard can say whether a second opinion was *right*, not only whether it agreed.
5. **Independence is measured**: monthly pairwise Jaccard on normalised findings per (grader, anchor) pair; a pair >0.7 is "not a second opinion" and is rotated (`runtimes-aggregators.md §3(g)`). **Anchor per class** (κ needs one, and round 1 assumed one that does not exist for the largest population): `code-project`/`code-server` → `verdicts(stage=merge_gate|post_review)`; `trading-research` → a new `<<<VALIDATOR {verdict: PASS|FLAG|FAIL, findings[]} VALIDATOR>>>` block the research skills' in-session adversarial validator must emit (same parse path as `<<<TASK_PLAN>>>`, `session.py:405-460`; today "4. ADVERSARIAL VALIDATOR (clean context)" is a phase inside the same session with no verdict line, `atlas-swing-research/SKILL.md:39`), recorded as `verdicts(stage=grade, provider=anthropic)`; `research-read` → **no anchor** — `atlas-report`/`atlas-daily-brief` declare no `post_review` and deliver via `atlas-dash save-report`/Telegram, so `_maybe_review` skips on an empty diff even if enabled — and its grader ladder promotes on decoy catch-rate + `error` rate + Jaccard, with κ shown as "n/a". Verdict mapping for κ: CONCUR ≡ {lgtm, pass}, DISSENT ≡ {changes_requested, blocker, fail, flag}. P4 exit: "anchor coverage ≥ 80 % of graded `trading-research` jobs".

### 5.4 Scoreboard and qualification ladder

`scoreboard` view keyed **`(provider, model, task_class, role ∈ {grader, writer})`** over `verdicts ⋈ provider_ledger ⋈ jobs` — the same key as the ladders, because once a provider is both grader and writer a single `grade_mean` is ambiguous (grades *given* vs *received*). **Writer rows** (source column in brackets): `n` (`jobs`), `dod_pass_rate` (`jobs.done_verdict`, `n/a` excluded), `unverified_rate` (same), `grade_received_mean/p10` (`verdicts.score` where `job_id`'s writer = this provider, `scale`-normalised), `lgtm_rate` (`verdicts.stage ∈ {merge_gate, post_review}`), `escalation_rate` (`jobs.created_by` escalation prefix), `fail_rate_by_terminal_reason` (`jobs.terminal_reason`), `cost_usd_list_p50`, `latency_p50/p90`, `ttft_p50`, `rate_limited_incidence` (`provider_ledger`), `quota_share_pct` per family (`provider_ledger.cost_usd_list` / family budget), `ladder_state`. **Grader rows**: `n_grades` (`verdicts.grader_provider`), `dissent_rate`, `dissent_precision = upheld / (upheld + dismissed)` and `adjudicated_share` (`verdicts.adjudication`), `kappa_vs_anchor` (per the §5.3 anchor mapping; "n/a" for `research-read`), `independence_jaccard`, `error_rate`, `skip_rate_by_reason` (`quota`/`sampling_cap`/`sampled`/`model_unavailable`/`session_cap` — the five reported separately, since only `quota` feeds D5), `catch_rate` (`verdicts.decoy`), `ladder_state`. Served at `/api/scoreboard`, `/scoreboard` (Telegram, PWA, CLI); replaces the never-called `retrospective.skill_performance` (`retrospective.py:39-96`). **Two ladders**, keyed `(provider, task_class, role ∈ {grader, writer})` in `provider_qualifications` (migration 010 carries `role`), because a grader lane and a writer lane measure different things and Codex enters as a grader and a `research-read` canary writer in the same phase (P4) but on separate ladders. Units: the eval judge emits `SCORE: <1-5>` (`evals/harness.py:104-106`) while graders emit `0-10`; `verdicts.scale` stores the native scale and the view normalises (×2) — no threshold below mixes them.
- **Grader ladder**: `shadow → canary` needs ≥30 shadow grades, grader `error` rate < 5 %, `independence_jaccard` < 0.7, **`dissent_precision ≥ 0.5 on ≥10 adjudicated dissents`** (a grader qualified for *agreeing* with Claude — κ alone — would be the opposite of "additive dissent"; κ ≥ 0.4 vs the class anchor stays only as an anti-noise floor where an anchor exists, §5.3 item 5), and — for trading-research — `critic_catch_rate ≥ 0.8` on the ≥8-decoy calibration set (§8.1); `canary → qualified` after 30 more with the same bars; `qualified → demoted` on `dissent_precision < 0.3` over a rolling 20 adjudicated, Jaccard > 0.7 for a month, catch-rate < 0.5 over the rolling last 5 decoys (calibration set included), `rate_limited` on ≥3 consecutive runs, or two `auth_expired` in a week (breaker). (The earlier "within 1.0 of the anchor's self-grade" bar is dropped: the pairing rule means an Anthropic self-grade of Anthropic output is never produced, so it was uncomputable.)
- **Writer ladder**: `shadow` = eval cases with `--provider` persisted to `verdicts(stage=eval)` (judge score normalised to 0-10); `canary` (≤10 % of low-stakes live jobs, anchor-graded; for the P4 `research-read` canary this is the ≥20-job bar D0 names) needs ≥20 jobs with `dod_pass_rate ≥ 0.9`, `lgtm_rate ≥` the *class's* Anthropic rate over the same window (not the fleet-wide 49/60; `lgtm_rate` here is the belt-3b merge-gate rate for non-Anthropic writers vs the post-review rate for Anthropic ones — the scoreboard names the stage), grade-received mean within 1.0 of the class's Anthropic grade-received mean, `unverified_rate` no worse than the anchor's. **For a class with no anchor and no eligible cross-vendor grader the last two bars are `n/a`, and this ladder then equals §9 P4's operative bar (round 3 — the two were stated incompatibly and the §5.4 version was uncomputable for the one class it gates):** on `research-read` today codex is excluded from grading its own output by `pairing_rule`, Anthropic by `grader_fallback: skipped`, `gemini-free` by its `public` floor (the canary's targets are atlas `research/**`/`docs/**`, `sensitivity_default: {atlas: proprietary}`), and `ollama-local#mechanical` produces a check, not a 0-10 score — so `grade_received_mean` has no left-hand side, and §5.3 item 5 says the class has **no anchor** for `unverified_rate` to be compared against either. Promotion for such a class is therefore: **≥20 canary jobs at `dod_pass_rate ≥ 0.9`, belt-3b `lgtm_rate ≥` the class's Anthropic post-review rate over the same window (or `≥ 0.9` absolute if that rate does not exist), `unverified_rate` no worse than the class's Anthropic `unverified_rate`, zero allowlist refusals, and grade-received reported `n/a`** — excluding `skipped(session_cap)` and `skipped(sampling_cap)` rows from every rate. The comparative bars stand for `code-project`, where `merge_gate`/`post_review` anchors do exist. The same sentence appears in D0(A) so the owner sees what the ≥20-job gate measures; `qualified → demoted` on any rolling-20 window with `dod_pass_rate < 0.7`, grade-received −1.5 vs the class anchor, `rate_limited` on ≥3 consecutive runs, or two `auth_expired` in a week.
- **Who decides**: promotions and `canary_share` changes for any class whose executor can *write* (`research-read` via `publish.py`, `code-project`) are always `approvals(kind=provider_promote)` decided on Telegram — never autonomous, consistent with INV-21/D15; utility and grader classes may promote autonomously once the thresholds hold, as `proposals` of the `default-model` kind; demotion is automatic everywhere; `demoted` re-enters `shadow` only via an owner-approved `default-model` proposal. Judge stays Anthropic (`evals/run.py:48`, INV-21); eval coverage target: one case file per routable skill (7/72 today).

### 5.5 Retrospective

`retro` skill (weekly Sunday; Anthropic read-only, opus-4-7/medium) replaces the idle-triggered `review-and-improve` at opus-4-7/max (28 runs/30 d, ~1.3 M cache-read tokens each, for 11 pending proposals): an in-runner SQL pre-pass (read-only DSN, not `ScriptExecutor` — §2.4) produces the tables (per task class: which vendor delivered, DoD, grade, dissent, dissent precision, cost, latency, quota incidents, ladder moves), the LLM writes the narrative to `docs/retro/<week>.md` and **files `proposals` rows — its frontmatter limits its state change to that insert; it never dispatches `server-patch`** (the path `review-and-improve/SKILL.md:152-166` uses today), every verdict text it reads arrives through `untrusted_envelope()`, and a proposal whose evidence cites a non-Anthropic verdict carries `origin=cross-vendor` and is owner-approval-required (§2.6); review-and-improve's idle trigger `_check_idle_queue_review` (`events.py:432-460`) and its predicate `_should_trigger_idle_review` (`:345-361`) are removed **in P0, unconditionally** — not in P4 and not conditionally on D1's displacement choice (round 3 critical): it is the autonomous `server-patch` dispatcher (`review-and-improve/SKILL.md:152-166` passes proposal text in the payload), and leaving it live through P3 would keep a path open from a fetched page → the P3 local-model learning classifier → a `proposals` row → an autonomous `server-patch` merge of server code. **The consequence is stated rather than discovered: between P0 and P4 there is no retrospective/self-improvement loop at all** (the loop that files `proposals` rows today). The interim is the weekly CostCard plus an owner-run `/task retro` on demand — no schedule row — and `retro` itself lands in P4. A `ScriptExecutor` SQL pre-pass is **not** how `retro`'s tables are produced (Postgres is localhost TCP and that profile denies loopback, §2.4): the pre-pass runs in the runner process on a read-only DSN. **the alpha idle drainer `_check_idle_queue_alpha` (`events.py:392-429`, wired at `:504`) is explicitly kept** — it is a different, owner-approved loop (alpha flywheel, 2026-09-23, commits 5448c81/ef375b7) and a P4 test asserts it still enqueues `alpha-governor` under the same predicate and 4 h cooldown (extending the breaker-gating tests that already mock it); a monthly CostCard DM rides the first Sunday.

## 6) Scheduling

- **One scheduler of record** (`main.py:1314-1362`); vendor-native schedulers (Routines, ChatGPT/Codex tasks, Gemini Scheduled Actions, Grok) are not producers — none enqueues into `jobs:queue`, most are UI-only, all draw the same windows (matrix §2; `crosscut-tos §3.1`).
- **Lanes (P2)**: `jobs.lane` is derived **from the resolved skill first and from `created_by` only as a fallback** (round 3 — a `created_by`-only rule mis-routed the largest consumer and left others unmapped: the alpha flywheel is ≈58 % of week 39, yet `alpha-governor` arrives as `created_by="event-trigger:idle-queue"` (`events.py:427`) and matched "event-trigger → kernel", while its `alpha-research` stage jobs are dispatch-driven children with `created_by="dispatch-mcp"` (`mcp_dispatch.py:120,134`) and matched **no rule at all** — so the dominant load had no lane, no `weekly_budget`, and an alpha exhaustion would have held the `kernel` ops lane carrying deploy-autopilot, self-diagnose and the breaker). Rules, in order: `division: alpha` → **`alpha`**; `division: atlas` → `atlas`; `_*`-internal skills → `utility`; `god`/owner-originated → `owner`; `server-patch`/`deploy-autopilot` under a phase label → **`build`** (§2.5, so the §9 build cap can bind); then `created_by`: `telegram/web/cli → owner`; `deploy-autopilot/event-trigger/self-diagnose → kernel`; `writeback:`/`escalation:`/`escalation-L2:`/`escalation-L3:`/`evaluator:`/`eval-fix:`/`auto-continue:`/`plan:`/`learning:`/`dispatch-mcp` → the **parent job's lane** when there is one, else `utility`; everything else → `background` = the untouched `jobs:queue`. **The P2 PR prints the complete `created_by`-prefix → lane table from `grep -rn 'created_by=' src/` (20 producers today: `main.py:689,767,818,842,978,1031,1236`, `learning.py:365`, `events.py:427`, `mcp_dispatch.py:120,134`, …) with a named default**, and `test_lanes` gains "an `alpha-governor` idle-drainer job lands in the alpha lane" and "every `created_by` prefix in `src/` maps to a declared lane". Job loop:

```
# main.py:113-185, same task, slot-before-BLPOP kept (INV-15); every non-dispatch path releases the slot (as main.py:159-171 does today)
promote_held()                                                   # jobs:held:<lane> members whose next_check has passed → LPUSH back to the lane head
await sem.acquire()
non_owner_running = count(running where lane != owner)
if non_owner_running >= MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS - OWNER_RESERVED_SLOTS and llen(jobs:queue:owner) > 0:
    keys = [jobs:queue:owner]                                    # strict reservation only when an owner job is waiting
else:
    # ALWAYS all lanes minus ones under an in-process backoff. NOTE: owner_hold() is deliberately NOT in this filter
    # (round 3): filtering a lane out here means its jobs are never popped and therefore never reach the per-job
    # owner_hold branch below, so §4.1's "newly due rows enqueue but park as job_held{reason=owner_hold}",
    # `jobs --held`, and §0 b8's "a stalled job is never silently stuck" would all be unsupported for the one
    # hold the owner triggers by hand. Held lanes are still popped; their jobs are parked individually.
    keys = rotate([k for k in ALL_QUEUE_KEYS if lane_backoff_until[k] <= now])   # owner first, kernel second, then ROTATED
popped = await redis.blpop(keys, timeout=2)
if not popped: sem.release(); continue
… resolve skill/provider chain, family (opus|sonnet) …
if not any(window_ok(p, family) for p in chain) or lane_budget_exhausted(lane, family) or provider_sessions(p, family) >= max_sessions(p, family) or owner_hold(lane):   # owner_hold = GET quota:lane:<lane>:hold_until > now
    if first_hold_or_reason_changed(job):
        audit job_held{reason, next_check}                       # audited on the first hold and on a reason change only — not every 30 s (~600 events per 5-h red window otherwise)
        queue notice(kind=lane_held|window_red, severity=high)   # §4.5: debounced one per lane per reason per window — otherwise a machine-lane hold is invisible until the next digest
    ZADD jobs:held:<lane> (resets_at if the reason carries one else now+30) job.id   # parked per lane, not pushed back to the BLPOP list
    lane_backoff_until[lane] = now + 30; sem.release(); continue  # in-process backoff (renamed from lane_hold_until, which collided with the DURABLE quota:lane:<lane>:hold_until of §2.3/§4.1); the slot is freed and the other lanes keep draining
```

Why the hold is a per-lane park and not a push-back: Redis `BLPOP k1 k2 …` returns from the *first* non-empty key in list order, so a held atlas job `LPUSH`ed back to `jobs:queue:atlas` would be re-popped on the next iteration ahead of `jobs:queue:utility` and `jobs:queue` — starving exactly the lanes that are supposed to keep draining — and a `sleep 30; continue` while holding the semaphore slot would halve the runner at prod `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS=2`. INV-12's "requeue at the front" is preserved because the promoter re-inserts at the lane head. The loop **always drains every lane key**; `LANES_ENABLED=0` changes only where `enqueue_job` pushes, so the switch is safe in both directions and never strands ids sitting in `jobs:queue:<lane>`.

**Lane order is priority, and fairness comes from rotation — said plainly because a fixed key order is a strict priority queue, not fairness (round 3).** `BLPOP k1 k2 …` returns from the first non-empty key, so a fixed `owner → kernel → atlas → utility → background` list means any standing atlas backlog blocks the utility and background lanes indefinitely — and at prod `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS=2` the atlas lane is self-feeding (each `alpha-research` job dispatches the next stage after its push) and is ≈60–75 % of the fleet, while those lower lanes carry the task-lifecycle children (`writeback`, `evaluator`, `eval-fix`, `auto-continue`) that advance owner tasks. Today's single FIFO cannot starve them: a child enqueued now waits only behind work enqueued earlier. So `owner` stays first and `kernel` second, and **the remaining keys rotate each iteration** (or a deficit round-robin over per-lane pop counts). `test_lanes` gains "with a standing atlas backlog, a background-lane job is popped within N iterations", and the P2 exit carries the same statement beside the owner-reservation line — round 2's exit criterion tested only the owner lane, so this regression would have shipped unnoticed.

**Sites that address `jobs:queue` by name (all move to `db.queue_key(lane)` / `ALL_QUEUE_KEYS` in P2, listed in the P2 scope and `tests/test_lanes.py`):** `reconcile.py:88-101` (`lrange QUEUE_JOBS` — otherwise every lane-queued row looks stranded at startup and is re-`RPUSH`ed to `jobs:queue` on every restart, duplicating ids; **`requeue_stranded_queued` also treats `ZRANGE jobs:held:<lane>` members as present**, otherwise a held job — popped from its lane list, parked in the ZSET, status still `queued` — is re-`RPUSH`ed on every restart with a spurious `job_requeued_stranded` audit; harmless only because `_process_job` returns on `status != queued`, `main.py:331-333`), `telegram_bot.py:512` (`/clear` deletes only `jobs:queue` → **must delete every `jobs:queue:<lane>` *and* every `jobs:held:<lane>` ZSET**, flipping those rows to failed as the DB half already does — otherwise a `/clear` during a red window refills the queue from the ZSETs, because held rows keep `status=queued` and `promote_held()` re-`LPUSH`es them at the next check), `web.py:335` (`/health` queue depth → sum over lanes, **plus a separate `held` count under a new stable JSON key** per C23 — a lane-list sum alone reports 0 while an arbitrary number of jobs sit held, and that number is what the heartbeat Worker and `healthcheck-all.sh` consume), `main.py:347` (tail requeue) and `main.py:449` (INV-12 `LPUSH` → lane head), `plans.py:181/233/311` (owner `plan` DAG children `RPUSH` to `jobs:queue` — they derive the lane from the parent task's `origin_channel`, otherwise the most common owner flow lands in the background lane), `healthcheck-all.sh:244`, `db.py:11` docstring.

`OWNER_RESERVED_SLOTS=1`; at prod `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS=2` the reservation is soft (owner job is *next*, never behind a second atlas job) and becomes strict once in-process utility calls (P3) free the RAM to raise prod to 3 (D1). `tests/test_lanes.py`: owner-next ordering; legacy `jobs:queue` still drained; quota requeue lands at the lane head; "atlas lane red, utility and legacy lanes drain; slot count unchanged during a hold"; "lane over budget is held, owner lane is not, governed atlas lane notices instead"; "owner hold on atlas: utility/kernel/owner drain; atlas parks as `owner_hold`; release re-promotes at lane head"; "a held job is not re-queued on restart"; "`job_held` is audited once per reason"; "Opus bar red, Sonnet job on the same lane runs"; `requeue_stranded_queued` sees lane keys and the held ZSETs; `/clear` empties all lanes **and all held sets**; `/health` depth = sum **and `held` reported separately**; plan children land in the parent's lane; "an `alpha-governor` idle-drainer job lands in the alpha lane"; "every `created_by` prefix in `src/` maps to a declared lane"; "with a standing atlas backlog, a background-lane job is popped within N iterations"; "a Codex job's belt-3b gate cannot raise concurrent Anthropic sessions above `max_sessions`"; breaker not bypassable by lane (C26).
- **Provider-window gate**: a job waits only when every provider in its class chain is red for its model family (plan §6f); pinned lanes pause on an Anthropic red window exactly as today. **Honest consequence under D0 as corrected:** through P3 every writer chain is Anthropic, so an Anthropic red window still pauses every agentic job and what keeps draining is the utility lane (`utility-classify`/`utility-learning`/`utility-public`), the Codex critic/grader post-steps and `ScriptExecutor` checks; **from P4 the `research-read` canary lane also drains** — a `research-read` row with `provider_policy: routable` resolves to Codex when Anthropic is red and the canary is green (≤ `canary_share`, ≥20 canary jobs first) — and from P5 exit the `code-project` canary likewise; the pinned lanes (`code-server`, `review-gate`, `_evaluate`, `chat`, `trading-research`) pause on every Anthropic red window by design. The P5 drill is named accordingly: "utility, critic AND the `research-read` canary lane drain". `window_ok(anthropic, family)` keys on the 5-h/7-d `status`/`resets_at` transitions the SDK actually emits (§2.8) plus the status-line feed, not on an inferred gauge; only the red family's jobs are held. Fan-out per lane is a cost-weighted window budget, not a slot count: start 2 / 2 / 1 / 1 (`crosscut-tos §3.10b`), a per-provider, per-family `max_sessions` cap (anthropic 2; `ANTHROPIC_MAX_SESSIONS` env in P2, registry from P3) enforced independently of `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS`, a per-lane `weekly_budget` in `cost_usd_list` (§2.8) that holds a non-governed lane at 100 % (owner lane exempt; governed atlas rows notice + LOOP.md §7 proposal), the owner's durable `lane hold`, Codex serialized per stream lease, local RAM-guarded.
- **Scheduler tick** (every 30 s): due unpaused rows → if `last_run_job_id` running and `misfire_policy=skip` → `schedule_skipped`; chain = `policy.chain(kind, provider_policy)` (`[anthropic]` for the default `pinned`); no healthy provider → `schedule_deferred{reason=window}` + failures-notify; else `Job(kind, description, payload, lane, schedule_id, provider_hint)`, **commit before RPUSH** (closes the race the retry ladder absorbs, `main.py:196-230`), `next_run_at = croniter.next(slot)` (slot-based), `last_run_job_id` stamped; `kind` validated at enqueue against `list_all()` **minus** `control/policy.py`'s forbidden set (`god`, `_*`-internal, `isolation: host` — C21: god is never scheduled; today `mcp_dispatch.py:32` `_FORBIDDEN_KINDS` exists only for dispatch), the same set `schedule add|edit` uses (`test_intents` case); retires `JobKind`.
- **Adherence**: `schedule_adherence.py` runs in-process every 30 min in addition to the 07:15 launchd belt; `expects: {artifact, within}` per row flips `LATE`/`MISSING` and DMs *[graft: hub §3-4]*; findings appear on ScheduleCards and the Schedules view.
- **Ownership**: `scripts/seed-schedules.sh` stays the sole writer for non-owner-edited rows through P4 — with one P1 change, because `server-deploy` runs it on **every** deploy and its `ON CONFLICT (name) DO UPDATE SET cron_expression = …` (`seed-schedules.sh:40-46`; `server-deploy/SKILL.md:149`) would silently revert every phone edit on the next deploy: the update clause protects `cron_expression`/`job_payload` per column (name/desc may still update) — `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE` is all-or-nothing for the whole `SET`, so the SQL is `cron_expression = CASE WHEN schedules.owner_edited THEN schedules.cron_expression ELSE EXCLUDED.cron_expression END` (and the same for `job_payload`), with a shell test fixture; `owner_edited=true` rows keep cron/payload across deploys (name/desc still overwritten) **only when `governed=false`** — atlas loop rows (`division: atlas` skills) are `governed=true`, and a phone edit on them files a proposal for the LOOP.md §7 front door instead of writing the row (judge 1 must-fix). P5 replaces the 41× `pipenv run python | psql` loop with `schedules.yml` + a Python seeder that validates cron collisions, DST-anchored ET pairs and provider capability — **as a drop-in behind the same path**: `scripts/seed-schedules.sh` becomes a two-line shim that execs `python -m src.schedules.seed`, so `server-deploy/SKILL.md:149` (protected path #8) needs no edit and the old shell seeder cannot keep running against rows the Python seeder owns.
```yaml
# schedules.yml row shape (P5; seeder validates and is the sole writer for governed rows)
- name: atlas-daily-brief
  cron: "0 7 * * 1-5"            # tz America/New_York; DST pairs generated by the seeder, not hand-commented
  kind: atlas-daily-brief
  payload: {project_slug: atlas, session_timeout_seconds: 3600}
  lane: atlas
  provider_policy: pinned        # pinned (default) | routable | <named chain>
  misfire_policy: skip
  notify: failures
  governed: true                 # LOOP.md §7 front door; phone edits → proposal
  expects: {artifact: file, within: 90m}
```

- **Phone add**: `/schedule add "weekdays 17:30 America/New_York" --kind=atlas-daily-brief --project=atlas` → in **P2 a deterministic preset parser** (`weekdays HH:MM [TZ]`, `daily HH:MM`, `every <dow> HH:MM`, `hourly`, `every N hours`, raw 5-field cron — the same presets the PWA lists in §4.3; an unparsed phrase answers "use a preset or cron"), shipped with `schedule edit` and `POST /api/schedules`; **through P1 `schedule add` accepts a raw 5-field cron only** (round 3: §0 b9 and this bullet said P1 while §9's P1 cell listed "preset→cron → P2" under "Moved out", so an implementer reading §6 built it in P1 and one reading §9 never built it; P2 is chosen because it matches the P1 re-cut, P1's own "what the owner feels" line and the fact that no create route existed in any phase) → a confirm button showing the cron and the next three fires → row with payload derived from the kind's project *[graft: multi-bot §2.1]*. Free-text phrases fall through to `utility_call(utility-classify)` (schema `{cron, tz, confidence}`) only **from P3**, when the in-process Ollama lane exists — in P1 the only LLM transport is a ~148 MB `claude` subprocess drawing the Max window, which is neither ≤2 s nor free.
- **Credential canaries as schedules** *[graft: incremental §4]*: daily `claude -p ping` with the `model_usage` assertion, run on the runner's own path — **and with settings scope pinned, because the canary's whole job is to prove *which* credential the fleet runs on**: it builds its options with `setting_sources=["project"]` exactly as the runner does and runs the §2.4 settings-override refusal over its own cwd **and** over `~/.claude/settings.json`/`settings.local.json` before pinging; a settings file carrying any auth or `hooks` key is itself a canary **FAIL** with `settings_override`. Without that, a settings-level `apiKeyHelper` or `env.ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` would let the canary report a green Keychain login while the traffic was API-billed or redirected — and D22 asks the owner to start putting a `statusLine` command into that same file — **in P0 as `scripts/credential-canary.sh` on a launchd timer, invoking the SDK-bundled CLI from the runner venv** (the executor seam is P3, so "via `ClaudeSdkExecutor`" was a P0 dependency on a P3 component; the script migrates onto `ClaudeSdkExecutor` in P3; never the brew binary — a brew-CLI pass says nothing about the runner's path); daily `codex exec --ephemeral "ok"` **inside each stream home** (`/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-<n>/`, via the wrapper's `canary` verb, **under the `codex:stream:<n>` lease — skipping, not queueing, when a job holds it**; the use-tied refresh must happen there — refresh fires only during use and an idle lane lapses after ~8 days, `runtimes-aggregators.md §3`), Gemini key 200, Ollama `/api/ps`; `setup-token` T−30 d alarm from a recorded mint date; a Claude auth failure **holds every lane except `owner`** and DMs an ApprovalCard — it never switches the fleet to the sealed token (§3); a Codex auth failure parks that stream as `auth_expired` before the first job of the day. **Status-line feed (D22, one-time setup, not a chore)**: the owner adds a `statusLine` command to `~/.claude/settings.json` on the Mini (and optionally the laptop) that appends `{ts, rate_limits}` to `volumes/telemetry/claude_rate_limits.jsonl` (5-min de-dup) — the owner already runs interactive `claude` sessions on the Mini continuously (state map §2.8: four `claude` TUIs resident), the window is shared across every Keychain consumer, so the owner's session gauge *is* the runner's gauge; the runner's quota reader takes the newest line as `quota_snapshots(source=vendor-statusline)`, shows its age on the QuotaStrip, labels "inferred" only when the newest line is older than 24 h and "uncalibrated" only when older than 7 d, and uses the series to settle whether the vendor `seven_day` window is fixed or rolling. No runner-driven probe: these are the owner's own sessions.

## 7) Project delivery

1. **Contract**: `projects/<slug>/.context/CONTEXT.md` gains `deliverables:` (build green, healthcheck 200, URL 200, tests, CHANGELOG) → the job's DoD; the delivery contract (`delivery.py:130-147` cwd, `:75-101` authority) and workspace clone + ff-sync semantics (INV-16) are unchanged; the clone's *lifetime* extends past `run_session` from P3 (§2.7) so steps 4–7 can see it.
2. **Plan** (Anthropic `plan` → DAG via `plans.py`) with per-subtask checks.
3. **Build**: Anthropic by default — **including for the public vhosts (pickem, bingo, content-forge), which is permitted**: the owner *generating* a page batch-wise and publishing it is "not addressed either way" by the terms, whereas a surface whose viewer requests trigger Max calls is the pattern they forbid (§1 non-goal, §3 Anthropic row, `claude-anthropic.md` line 87). The Codex rescope in D15 is about **who may write into a public-facing repo on plan auth**, not about whether Anthropic may build those sites; Codex `research-read` canary from P4 (`--sandbox read-only` + `--output-schema`; the artefact is text the runner materialises) and `code-project` canary from P5 exit once `canary` on its writer ladder — `workspace-write`, network off, no MCP, dedicated user, fail-closed Anthropic gate + `publish_allowlist` before publish (closes the ff-sync-before-review hole judge 2 found in interface-first/incremental). **Targets (D15 rescoped; now P5, not P6)**: on ChatGPT plan auth only owner-only artefacts — owner-read report/doc repos and atlas research memos already covered by the redacted-critic lane, and inside the allowlist (`research/**`, `docs/**`, `*/reports/**`; never `swing/**`, `tradingcore/**`, kernel limits, CI or `.claude/`) — because the spec's own ToS source puts "anything a second person consumes (pickem league dashboard, shared project sites, anything under a project's public URL)" on `CODEX_API_KEY` (`chatgpt-openai.md §3` rule (b); `crosscut-tos §3.9`); pickem/bingo/content-forge (public vhosts) join the canary only on a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` project key — priced at the **`code-project` class's measured shape, not the fleet median**: atlas-build jobs are ≈ 7.3 M tokens/job (9 jobs / 65.9 M cache-read + 2.4 M write + 0.44 M out), ≈ $1.5–2/job on Sol (cached input $0.20/M), so the "$1–3/month" total survives only because a 10 % canary of ~15 code-project jobs/month is ~1.5 jobs; on a metered key the per-job figure, not the count, is what a runaway canary would bill, hence the `max_turns` bound — which is a D6-class metered decision and is shown in §13 — never ai-server, never atlas kernels.
4. **Evidence checks** in the clone before anything leaves it (the clone outlives `run_session`, §2.7).
