5. **Gate**: `merge_gate_verdict` recorded; on a non-Anthropic lane the gate is the in-process `code-review` protocol of §5.3 belt 3b (`wait_for(600)`, in the parent's own slot, after the `publish_allowlist` check) — anything but `lgtm` (including `error`/`timeout`/`skipped`/empty diff/outside-allowlist) parks the clone (kept, as failures are today; the Codex copy is removed), opens a FailedCard and a fix round, and nothing is published.
6. **Grader** (cross-vendor, advisory; after D21) → `verdicts`.
7. **Publish → parked approval → gated deploy**: on Anthropic lanes the session pushed during step 3; on non-Anthropic lanes the runner's `publish.py` commits and pushes with its scoped deploy key after step 5 (§2.6) and audits `artifact_published`; then `finalize_workspace` ff-syncs; `deploy_permitted` "needs approval" → `approvals(kind=deploy)` + ApprovalCard; `approve` (via `created_by="approval:<id>:<channel>"`, §4.5) → deploy job (`server-deploy`/`atlas-redeploy`, unchanged executor skills; `deploy-director: verify` writes `done_verdict`) → `deliverable_registered{url, commit, healthcheck}`; a red gate keeps old code serving and produces a FailedCard with `[Retry] [Open logs]`.
8. **Deliverable card** to the origin channel; Deliverables feed on the PWA. `project <slug> status|deploy|logs` wraps `mcp_projects`; the deploy autopilot moves from `healthcheck-all.sh:204-252` raw SQL to `ScriptExecutor` + `enqueue_job`.

Atlas two-repo copies (15/26 drifted, state map §2.6) get a lint diffing `atlas/integrations/ai-server/skills/` against `skills/` (in the P4 lint PR).

## 8) Trading research within INV-22

### 8.1 What runs where

| Stage (existing atlas loop) | Executor | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Research cycles, theses, quant sweeps, alpha scout, CIO memo | Anthropic (Max; "Help improve Claude" **off** → 30-day retention, `claude-anthropic.md §3`); market data through atlas `tradingcore` exactly as today — **no MCP is mounted** (`alpaca-mcp-server` ships order tools, `crosscut-finance-trading-ai.md` line 21, and `allowed_tools` auto-approves rather than restricts, line 248; mounting even a paper-order tool is an LLM-reachable order path = protected-path change under INV-22, so read-only data MCPs are a separate decision, **D24**, default no) | constitution DoD checks expressed with §5.1 primitives that can actually check them: card pre-registered (`file_exists`), `trials.jsonl` line **before** the LEDGER verdict (`jsonl_before`), walk-forward folds and lifetime-N DSR present (`json_field` on `metrics.json`), memo sections (`sections_present`), no web tools in evaluation runs (`tools_absent`), dates ≤ cutoff (`dates_within`), the vertical's own validators (`command`: `quantlab.trials.distinct_n`); they land as `<vertical>/evaluation/dod.yml` through LOOP.md §7 (D11) |
| Adversarial validator | Anthropic clean-context subagent (C22 separated duties) | verdict → `verdicts(stage=grade, provider=anthropic)` |
| **Cross-vendor critic** (new skill `atlas-critic`, staged in `atlas/integrations/ai-server/skills/`; **after D21 + D4**) | Codex `exec --sandbox read-only --output-schema critic.json` as `_aicodex` on the **redacted projection**; local `qwen3.5:9b` for one mechanical check only — "numbers quoted in the memo appear in the cycle's `metrics.json`/`manifest.json`", files the runner passes in (a no-tools HTTP executor cannot check link liveness or tool outputs, and transcript previews are 500 chars); link liveness → `url_200`, dates → `dates_within` (`ScriptExecutor`) | writes `DISSENT/CONCUR` + findings to `verdicts(stage=critic)` **only** — no `critic.jsonl` file (a read-only, network-off critic cannot write into a clone or push, and the runner will not write the canonical checkout); additive after the validator, never replaces it; `DISSENT` severity=high on a governor-feeding memo → owner card |
| **Critic calibration** — (a) a **one-off P5 calibration set** of ≥8 decoy memos, each with one planted PROTOCOL §3 violation drawn from the four kinds (look-ahead window, missing placebo, cost-free backtest, DSR omitted), authored under `atlas/evaluation/decoys/` via LOOP.md §7 and run in one batch before the P5 exit; (b) an ongoing **monthly** single decoy for drift | the same `atlas-critic` path over the decoy memo | run stamped `verdicts.decoy=true`; scoreboard column `critic_catch_rate` per (provider, vertical); a dissent *rate* alone cannot tell a critic that catches violations from one that dissents at random, and atlas's own decoys are "armed-not-exercised" today (`swing/evaluation/PROTOCOL.md:54`: quarterly) — this is the measured version; round 1's "≥ 0.8 on ≥5 decoys" at one decoy/month would have needed five months inside a three-week phase; **P5 exit = catch-rate ≥ 0.8 on the ≥8-decoy calibration set**; < 0.5 over the rolling last 5 decoys (calibration set included) demotes the lane (§5.4) |
| Governors (grade from DB rows only) | Anthropic read-only | read the critic's verdict **from the atlas DB, where governors actually read** — `verdicts` lives in the `assistant` Postgres database (migration 009) and governors pull rows via the clone's `.env` `DATABASE_URL` from the atlas database (`atlas-swing-evaluate/SKILL.md:28`), two separate databases (`backup.sh:22` dumps only `assistant`), so a read-only atlas role could never see `assistant.verdicts`; **`grading.py`'s `critic_mirror` sub-step** therefore mirrors each `verdicts(stage=critic)` row into an append-only atlas table `<vertical>.critic_verdicts(verdict_id, job_id, memo_ref, verdict, findings jsonb, ts)` through the atlas DB write role immediately after the verdict insert and before `jobs:reviewed` fires — **not `publish.py`, which round 2 named** (round 3: `publish.py` runs six steps *earlier* in the post-step order, before the verdict it would mirror exists, and only on non-Anthropic writer lanes, while `trading-research` is `pinned: true` and therefore always Anthropic-written, so that bracketed step never executed in a critic job at all and the P5 test could not pass). `publish.py`'s job is artefact commit/push/ff-sync on non-Anthropic writer lanes, nothing else (P5 scope; test: "a critic verdict is visible in the atlas DB before `jobs:reviewed`"; the table is listed under D11/LOOP.md §7); every finding text reaches the governor prompt through `untrusted_envelope()`; governors must cite a DISSENT by `verdict_id` with `upheld\|dismissed` in their grade (LOOP.md §7 amendment, evidence-gated), which is adjudication source (i) in §5.3 |
| Paper supervision / sandbox swing decisions (inside the kernel's bounded candidates) | Anthropic sonnet | `db_row` checks make "completed ≠ productive" visible (37/37 `provisioning_gap` runs report success today) |
| Sentiment feature (optional) | xAI `x_search` → append-only snapshot table, overlay tag only, forward-paper only (`grok-xai.md §6`) | **D6, metered, default off** |

Critic contract: `isolation: workspace`, `task_class: trading-research`, `providers: {critic: [codex, ollama-local]}`, `required_tools: [Read, Glob, Grep]`, `sensitivity: proprietary`; input = the redacted memo + `metrics.json`/`manifest.json` materialised (tracked-files-only, `git archive` run by the runner, §2.4) into a copy the runner and `_aicodex` share through the setgid `ai-codex` group + the validator's `<<<VALIDATOR>>>` verdict; env = `CODEX_HOME=/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-<n>` (the persistent stream home, §2.4), `HOME`/`TMPDIR` = the stream home, **no atlas `.env`** (`env_files` skipped for non-Anthropic executors and absent from the copy by construction; the owner's home, the server `.env` and `projects/atlas/.env` are unreadable to that user), network off; the copy is removed by `finalize_workspace` on every outcome; cost ≈ one Codex message per memo on Free; Gemini-free only when `memo.sensitivity == public`; the Codex critic receives proprietary text only while the D4 attestation row is unexpired (§2.5 floor).

### 8.2 Never

No lane gets an order tool; the only order path stays `atlas/swing/swing/executor.py --submit` behind `risk.validate` (`:535, :599, :621, :640`); Tradier/IBKR remote MCPs (live order tools in the same server, `crosscut-finance-trading-ai.md §3`) stay off the box; the server-side broker-host deny (§2.6: Bash pattern + `WebFetch|WebSearch` matcher) is a **text-level belt** — a URL assembled from parts never matches — so the atlas kernel tripwires and `risk.validate` remain the enforcement of record; INV-22 stays a documented invariant with a guard belt, not a guard. **For non-Anthropic writers the INV-22 belt is the hardcoded `publish_allowlist`** (§2.5/§2.6): `publish.py` refuses any Codex diff touching `swing/**`, `tradingcore/**`, `**/risk*`, `*/limits.yaml`, `*/budget.yaml`, `.github/**`, `.claude/**` or `integrations/ai-server/**` before the gate runs (`test_publish`), so a non-Anthropic writer can never widen a kernel limit or un-pin the sandbox even with an `lgtm`. **The Max/subscription lane is never the decision step for a live order:** Anthropic Consumer Terms §3 forbids relying on the Services "to buy or sell securities or to provide or receive advice about securities" (`claude-anthropic.md §6(a)`), so if `swing/LADDER.md` ever funds, the decision step must be human — or a Commercial-Terms lane, which owner policy excludes — and live-on-Max is a **No regardless of INV-22**; the owner records this in atlas `swing/GO_LIVE.md` via LOOP.md §7 (§12a). **Tripwire** (scheduled weekly): (a) run the critic against a fixture memo containing `tradier.com/v1/accounts/x/orders` and a fake `TRADIER_SANDBOX_TOKEN=` line; assert no outbound request, no token value in any Codex `agent_message`/`command_execution` item, and `guard_denied` fires on the Anthropic lane for both the Bash and the `WebFetch` form; (b) plant canary files outside the clone copy — a fake `.env`, a `~/.claude/.credentials.json`-shaped file under the owner's home, a fake `projects/atlas/.env` — **and a real `.env` inside the source clone**; assert their content is absent from every Codex transcript item, that the clone's `.env` is absent from the Codex copy, and that `sudo -u _aicodex cat <path>` fails for each planted path (this is the test the dedicated-user separation must pass before Codex leaves `blocked`, §3); **(c) round-3 additions: plant the `~/.config/ai-server/publish-key` path as a canary and assert its content appears in no JSONL, no `jobs:stream`, and no `ai-mcp` read (the redactor now carries the path and the PEM shape, §2.4); and assert `sudo -n -u _aicodex /usr/local/libexec/ai-codex-exec exec /tmp/<planted-dir>` is refused and audited (the wrapper's path-prefix/symlink/mode check, §2.6).**

### 8.3 Data and credential boundaries

- **Redacted projection** *[core: outcome-first §6.3]*: a deterministic, tested redactor strips positions, sizes, ledger rows, account values, owner identifiers and secret-shaped strings (`Authorization: Bearer …`, `KEY=…`, token patterns) before any non-Anthropic lane sees text — critic memos, grader bundles, **and every `utility_call` prompt to a C-rated HTTP host** (§2.4; audited `utility_call{redacted, patterns_hit}`); Anthropic keeps full context. Transcript-derived text is additionally confined to `utility-learning` (local/Anthropic only). Codex on plan auth receives thesis text only after the ChatGPT "Improve the model" toggle is verified off (D4) — otherwise public-class memos only.
- **Credential scoping**: `env_files` today copies the whole atlas `.env` into every atlas workspace (`workspaces.py:192-244`); it becomes per vertical in the atlas manifest (atlas PR via LOOP.md §7): Tradier sandbox token only in swing workspaces, vendor keys never in any atlas workspace, non-Anthropic executors never receive atlas env at all (audit assertion on `executor_started.env_keys`).
- **AUP posture — stated honestly, including the clause that governs the primary lane**: owner-only research memos, human on the order path, nothing served to a second person. That sits inside the finance cross-cut's advice/reliance reading (`crosscut-finance-trading-ai.md §6`), but the Max subscription lane is also bound by **Anthropic's Consumer Terms §3 "Use of our Services"**, which forbids using the Services "to rely upon the Services, the Materials, or the Actions to buy or sell securities or to provide or receive advice about securities…" (`claude-anthropic.md §6(a)`). The research's own reading: a paper-trading assistant that *informs* the owner's decisions is a **grey zone, not "inside"**; an unattended loop where Claude's output *is* the buy/sell trigger on a live account is "rely upon… to buy or sell securities" and is the pattern the clause names; "the subscription lane may research, not decide" (`§8`). So: paper/sandbox swing decisions inside the kernel's bounded candidates (§8.1 row) = grey zone, accepted for paper; live = the named pattern, a No on the Max credential regardless of INV-22 (§8.2). Population-scale 2026 evidence says no vendor has directional edge (`§5, §8`), so no vendor is ever a decision-maker.
- **Blockers — cheap, no vendor work, each with a phase (§9)**: **P0**: provision `TRADIER_SANDBOX_TOKEN` + `FINNHUB_TOKEN` (owner, §12a row 6b, now a P0-exit row); an **interim `provisioning_gap` pre-check that needs no `ScriptExecutor`** — **with two hard rules that round 2's one-line version lacked (round 3 critical)**: (i) **names this server refuses everywhere are exempt from the check** — `atlas/manifest.yml:18-20` declares `env_required: [DATABASE_URL, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY]`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` must never exist anywhere (INV-3/C1; `src/runner/main.py:77-84` exits the runner if it is set), so "manifest `env_required` key absent from `projects/atlas/.env`" is *permanently true for every atlas row*: implemented as round 2 wrote it, this pre-check would have deferred every atlas and alpha schedule on the day P0 ships, reproducing the 2026-08-17 governor-dark class §11 exists to prevent. The check therefore carries a `PROVISIONING_EXEMPT` set (the whole `claude_env` refusal list) and is **fail-open** on a missing/unreadable manifest or `.env`; fixture: `env_required` containing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` must defer nothing. (ii) **the two keys the blocker is actually about — `TRADIER_SANDBOX_TOKEN`, `FINNHUB_TOKEN` — appear in no manifest today, so the check can never fire for them until they are added**: either through an atlas `manifest.yml` amendment via LOOP.md §7 (atlas is GitHub-canonical; this server does not edit it) or through a per-kind `env_required:` list on the schedule row / skill frontmatter. Until one of those lands, the interim check protects nothing the blocker names. It emits `schedule_deferred{reason=provisioning_gap}` and a `notify=failures` DM. **The ≈ −20 M/month delta is marked *unmeasured* in §2.8/§13 until the P3 `ScriptExecutor` version lands with a real key list** — round 2 booked the saving against a check that could not detect the gap; **P1**: `weekly.py:316` `KeyError('screen')` and the Alpaca→yfinance/Tradier data fallback for the paper trader (9/21 `stale_data`) are **atlas-repo patches** — born in the atlas dev clone, dispositioned through LOOP.md §7, executed by the `atlas-build` worker or an owner-dispatched atlas PR, never INV-4 server patches; **P3**: the `ScriptExecutor` version of the pre-check replaces the scheduler shim (~45 sessions/month today).
- **Visibility**: the Atlas view/card reads `resolved_provider/model`, `schedule_rollup` (`web.py:175-260`), adherence and per-vertical `*.runs` (read-only atlas role) — the "is any of this working" page (state map §5.25) *[graft: kernel §2.2]*.

## 9) Migration and rollout

Kill switches (env, one line each): `CONTROL_PLANE_V2=0` (legacy Telegram handlers), `NOTIFY_OUTBOX=0` (legacy `tasks:notify` consumer, `_done_listener` and `_job_to_chat` stay wired through P5 and P0 **dual-writes** — outbox row + legacy publish — with the switch selecting which renderer sends), `LANES_ENABLED=0` (only changes where `enqueue_job` pushes; the loop always drains all lane keys, §6), `CONTRACTS_MODE=observe|enforce`, `GRADING_ENABLED=0`, `EXECUTOR_LEGACY_PATH=1` (verbatim `_run_in_process`), `UTILITY_INPROCESS=0` (legacy `query()` loops), `WORKSPACE_FINALIZE_LEGACY=1` (ff-sync back in `run_session.finally`), **`POST_STEPS_LEGACY=1`** (restores the pre-P2 post-step ordering and drops the per-step timeouts — round 3: P2 re-orders the job lifecycle with two new unit tests as its gate and neither `LANES_ENABLED=0` nor "PWA opt-in" restored the old ordering), `ROUTER_PROVIDERS_ENABLED=anthropic` (plan §2), `ANTHROPIC_CREDIT_MODE=1` (the `programmatic_credit_mode` preset, §2.8), `providers pause <id>`, `lane hold <lane>`. `REVIEW_PRE_PUSH` is **deleted** from this list: D8 defers the Anthropic pre-push gate entirely, so the switch would toggle nothing that exists (it returns, renamed, with whatever design un-defers D8); the non-Anthropic pre-publish gate is unconditional in code and not env-readable (§2.5). Every phase is one migration at most, ships as small server-patch PRs on the INV-4 lane, and is deployed through `server-deploy` gates. What keeps running throughout: all 41 schedules (no runtime tripwire in this spec holds a governed atlas row — §2.8). **The automatic holds, enumerated in full (round 3 — this parenthetical named three of six):** (1) the provider-window gate; (2) the non-governed lane budget; (3) the `possible_credit_overflow` hold on non-owner, non-governed lanes until the owner acknowledges (§2.8); (4) the trailing-90-d-median × 1.5 hold on non-owner, non-governed lanes — **computable only from 90 d of `provider_ledger`, which lands in migration 009 (P2), so it is inactive before P5**; (5) the canary-detected Claude auth-expiry hold, and the new `account_on_hold`/`oauth_revoked` enforcement hold, both of which hold **every lane except `owner`** (§2.4, §3, §6); (6) an owner `lane hold`. No automatic hold ever touches a governed atlas or alpha row — those raise a LOOP.md §7 proposal plus an owner notice. Also running throughout: all 72 skills, the INV-13 gate, the launchd alerters and edge Worker (they gain `notify send`), prod concurrency 2 until D1. **Who executes, on which window:** every phase is built by server-patch sessions on the INV-4 lane drawing the same Max window as the 41 live schedules; each phase's PR states its expected token cost against the standing figure — **≈ 28 M tokens ≈ $30 `cost_usd_list` per week (≈ $115/month list-equivalent for ~13 weeks), `purpose=build`, on the `build` lane** (§2.5, §2.8) — the CostCard reports it, and **the cap is that absolute figure, held like any non-governed lane, not "15 % of the weekly window"** (round 3: the percentage form was computed against the trailing-30-d mean that §2.5's own `lanes` comment forbids as a seed and §13 repudiates; against week 39's 279 M the same 15 % is 42 M/week and against the spec's own 450–550 M/week projection it is 68–82 M/week, i.e. the cap grew the build's absolute budget exactly as the window tightened — and it could not bind at all while phase PRs landed on the `owner` lane, which is exempt from every hold). The figure is re-cut with the lane budgets each week from the reconcile script, never re-derived as a percentage. D1 states what is displaced during build weeks (an alpha-lab `budget.yaml` proposal capping `alpha-research` jobs/day and ≤ 4 M/job — the only lever that moves that load, §2.8 — or N `rejected` windows/week accepted); the idle auditor goes off in P0 regardless, as a containment item. The approved 08-10 plan sat five weeks with zero code; therefore **D1 approves P0–P1 only**, and P2 starts after the owner reads the P1 retro (re-approval gate).

**Shipped ahead of P0, not gated on design sign-off:** the Haiku 4.5 retirement swap (floor 2026-10-15, 20 days out) is a standalone `server-patch` — `llm_router.py:148`, `learning.py:258`, `skills/project-update-poll/SKILL.md:4` (`model: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, missed by the earlier list) and the `haiku` aliases at `telegram_bot.py:132-133` → `claude-sonnet-4-6` `effort=low`; only the registry alias waits for P3.

What the owner feels after each phase. **First, what the approved scope is not: P0–P1 are Anthropic-only hygiene and control-plane work — no other vendor is integrated in the D1 scope.** The first other-vendor lane is P3 (`utility_call`, `ask local|cerebras`), the first non-Anthropic writer P4 (the `research-read` canary), the first cross-vendor second opinion P4 after D21, and the red-window hedge P5. Then: P0 — every human launch DMs, scheduled failures DM, `/cancel` works, backups exist, the real monthly cost is known at both windows, the trading tokens are in; P1 — one-line launch from the phone with a live card, approvals as buttons on Telegram (the PWA and its Access layer arrive in P2), deliverables as Telegram documents, schedules run/pause from a card; P2 — never waits behind atlas, can `lane hold atlas` for the day, sees windows (vendor 7-d with age, inferred labelled) and cost per family, has the PWA and the CLI, schedules editable and they survive deploys; P3 — routing/learning no longer spawn `claude` subprocesses (a RAM/latency win; ≈0.5 % of the window), `ask local "…"`/`ask cerebras "…"` work (**`ask codex` does not yet** — its executor, user and login are P4), and each P3 component has its own kill switch (`EXECUTOR_LEGACY_PATH=1`, `ROUTER_PROVIDERS_ENABLED=anthropic`, `UTILITY_INPROCESS=0`, `WORKSPACE_FINALIZE_LEGACY=1` — "one env var restores today" was untrue once P3 moved `finalize_workspace`, installed `policy.select()` and replaced the `query()` loops); P4 — `ask codex "…"` works once Codex leaves `blocked`, `--model=codex/<id>` on a research task is honoured as an owner override, the first Codex-written research memo lands in atlas through the gate, (after D21) a second opinion on research memos with measured independence and precision, weekly retro, Codex confined to a user that cannot read the owner's home; P5 — an Anthropic red window pauses the pinned and code lanes, but utility, critic and the `research-read` canary keep flowing, the fleet DMs the hold instead of going silent, the trading critic runs with a measured catch-rate, and the "is any of this working" page exists. The availability hedge that motivated the 08-10 plan arrives for `research-read` at P5; for `code-project` it never arrives in either D0 branch (every canary job still consumes an Anthropic gate session, §0c).

Rollback rule per phase: flip the phase's switch, then `git revert` **application code only** — migration files are never reverted (columns are nullable and ignored by older ORM models; reverting `alembic/versions/00N_*.py` leaves prod `alembic_version` pointing at a revision with no script on disk and the next `server-deploy`'s `alembic upgrade head` fails with "Can't locate revision" mid-incident; `tests/test_migrations.py` only asserts one head + a walkable chain today, and its DB layer points at a throwaway database, so the P0 guard is a **runner-startup** check against the live `alembic_version` plus a pure fixture test of the comparison function (§9 P0) — alembic already fails loudly on a missing revision, so **no `server-deploy/SKILL.md` edit** is needed, which round 1 had hidden inside P0 and the P0 plan's Task 18 title still implies); if a migration itself must go, run `alembic downgrade -1` on prod *before* reverting the file. Reconcile, INV-2 and the marker protocol are never altered; `jobs:done` keeps its meaning and moves only later in the post-step order (after `workspace_synced`, §2.7), so a half-migrated runner restarts cleanly. Each phase's PR carries a rollback note naming the switch and the merge SHA.

P5 `scripts/drill-quota-pause.sh` (canary lane drains), INV-22 tripwire (Bash + WebFetch), `test_ai_mcp_sensitivity` (spoofed `clientInfo.name`; **a `--client=codex` read of an owner-private job returns metadata only while no attestation row exists**), critic calibration set catch-rate, critic mirror visible in the atlas DB (written by `grading.py`), seeder shim, invariant-preservation checklist as tests (INV-2/8/9/12/13/15/16/17/18/20/21/22 and C12, against the D2-approved INV-13/INV-21 rows) *[graft: kernel §1.9]*.

| **Programmatic-credit re-gating** — Anthropic paused (2026-06-15) the May-2026 Agent-SDK/`claude -p` credit split ($100 Max 5x / $200 Max 20x per month) and "can re-enable … without prior notice" (`claude-anthropic.md §3, §7`; `crosscut-tos §3.1, §4.3`): **at the measured ≈ $963/month a $100 pool is exhausted in ~3–4 days of each month** (~4–7 at the 30-d mean) | pre-decided, not hollow: `ANTHROPIC_CREDIT_MODE=1` flips the `programmatic_credit_mode` preset (§2.5/§2.8 — every lane budget re-sized to the pool, owner + kernel funded first, atlas governed rows via the pre-drafted LOOP.md §7 proposal written in P2); D14 moves to "prepare, do not schedule" so a first-party fallback exists before it is needed; the P4 Codex `research-read` canary and branch B (a trigger inside D5, §0c) are the structural hedge; the CostCard shows pool burn-down in that mode. "Kill switch restores today" is *not* a mitigation here — today is the same load |
| **Max load ≈ 8–10× the seat is the pattern the research names** — `crosscut-tos §4.3` labels > ~2× plan price "outside ordinary, individual usage", `§3.1` names "continuous background usage" as the reason weekly limits exist; enforcement observed so far is the cap itself (**four five-hour `allowed_warning` at 0.91–0.96 and two `rejected` in 30 d, plus a continuous `seven_day` `allowed_warning` series**; the 7-d bar reached 0.58 by day 2 of its window) | owner decision **D25** (accept the exposure / trim cadence / open a second lane); concrete mitigations with numbers: per-skill token-shape targets via LOOP.md §7 proposals against alpha-lab `budget.yaml` and the atlas charters (alpha-research ≤ 4 M/job from ≈ 6.5 M, atlas-build ≤ 5 M from ≈ 7.3 M, atlas-report ≤ 0.5 M — together ≈ 38 % of the 30-d window and ≈ 60 % of week 39; **that proposal, not `ALPHA_DAILY_JOB_VALVE`, is the lever — the constant gates only the idle drainer's `alpha-governor` enqueue, §2.8**), `retro` replacing the opus/max auditor, and the research's own fix — a second *lane* (P4 Codex canary; D14 Routines; **credit-bundled plans such as Ollama Cloud Pro, which `crosscut-tos §4.3` names in the same breath and round 2 dropped from the list**; branch B (a trigger inside D5, §0c)), never a second seat; the alarm-only `ordinary_usage_exceeded` notice keeps the drift visible (§2.8) |

| **Silent metered Anthropic spend via usage credits** (`claude-anthropic.md §3`: credits continue past caps at API rates, replacing the `rejected` event the pause logic keys on) | D3 confirms credits $0 / auto-reload off / spend limit $0 (`overflow_credits: disabled`), quarterly re-check, runtime `possible_credit_overflow` tripwire keyed first on the SDK's own `overage_status`, then on the ledger/5-m-cache-write heuristic, holding non-owner **non-governed** lanes (§2.8); the "ordinary usage" question is alarm-only + D25, never a plan-price hold that would park the fleet from day 6 |

Everything else on the INV-4 lane; the P0 alembic check and the P5 seeder were re-cut so they need no `server-deploy/SKILL.md` edit of their own |
| **Vendor enforcement action** (account hold, OAuth revocation, ban, or a billing event) — the spec accepts a load the research labels outside "ordinary, individual usage" (D25) and records that "OpenAI may throttle or ban without notice", yet had no detector: the typed terminal set had no class for it and an account hold surfaced as a generic `api_error` | detected from the vendor's **own error category**, not an HTTP status: `terminal_reason ∈ {account_on_hold, oauth_revoked, billing_error}` mapped from `system/api_retry`/`StopFailure` `error` (§2.4; `claude-anthropic.md` line 51, `crosscut-tos` lines 71/213); `account_on_hold`/`oauth_revoked` **hold every lane except `owner`**, queue `notice(kind=provider_enforcement, severity=high)` and DM an ApprovalCard — a **whole-scheduler back-off**, per `crosscut-tos` line 457 item 1(c), not a per-job retry (and `autoContinueAtUsageLimit` is "not offered at all" for `-p` runs); `billing_error` is the first `possible_credit_overflow` trigger; mapping covered by the P0 `test_result_capture` gate |

## 12) Owner decisions required (recommended answer in bold)

**Two sets, because 26 decisions is not a sign-off (round 3).** The **Sign-off set** — the only decisions the approvable unit needs — is **D1** (scope), **D3**'s P0-marked rows, **D4**, **D10** (now a P2-entry gate), **D12**, **D21** (it halves or doubles P4, costs 15 min per §12a row 17, and round 2 scheduled it *at* P4 entry, so the phase's size was undecidable until the phase began) and **D25** (posture). Owner cost for that set, from §12a's own rows 0/1/2/2b/4/4b/5/6/6b/7/7b/8: **≈ 194 min ≈ 3.2 h** — not the footer's 14.7 h, which covers all six phases. Everything else below is the **Deferred register**: it is *not* part of sign-off, and each entry carries the condition that would reopen it rather than a recommendation to act now (D5, D6, D8, D13–D20, D22–D24).

0. **D0 — reserved; resolved 2026-08-17** (plan R3 `:229` + decision 2 `:285-288`). Branch B is a **trigger inside D5**, not a choice the owner makes now; its costed delta is §0c. Kept as a number only so round-1/round-2 cross-references resolve. *(Round 3: D0's own recommendation — "A now; B only if P4 measures `skip_reason=quota` > 30 %" — is a measurement-triggered contingency identical to D5, and the document spent its first page re-litigating a five-week-old decision before stating what the owner gets.)* For reference, the branch as corrected in round 2: The 2026-08-17 decision already approved a Free-plan Codex writer canary on `research-read` (plan R3 `:229` "qualify on `research-read`, then `code-project` canary"; decision 2 `:285-288` "Start Free. $0, probing only. Revisit after R3 canaries show whether Codex actually carries load"), and the research permits owner-only, owner-read output on plan auth (`chatgpt-openai.md §3` rule (a)) while classing a *scheduled* canary as rule-(b) accepted risk. **(A) — 08-17 as approved:** Codex `research-read` canary writer on Free plan auth in P4 (owner-only memos, `--sandbox read-only` + `--output-schema`, `publish_allowlist` + belt-3b `lgtm` + `publish.py`, ≥20 canary jobs before any schedule row is `routable`, `skipped` on quota), `code-project` canary at P5 exit; $0. **(B) — pay:** ChatGPT Plus $20, or a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` for the scheduled belt + canary (rule-(b) clean; a metered exception to 08-17 the owner grants explicitly), costed in §0c; same shape, more capacity, no re-cut. **Recommended: A now; B only if P4 measures `skip_reason=quota` > 30 %, or if the owner wants the rule-(b)-clean lane.** Note honestly: branch A leaves the Max seat at ≈ 8–10× the research's ~2× "ordinary usage" heuristic with `research-read` as the only second lane through P5, and the research's fix for that is a second *lane*, never a second seat (D25).
1. **D1 Scope and phase gates**: **P0–P1 only**, with a re-approval gate on the P1 retro before P2. **Stated plainly, because D1 read in isolation did not say it: P0–P1 are Anthropic-only hygiene and control-plane work — no other vendor is integrated in the approved scope.** The first other-vendor lane is P3 (`utility_call`, `ask local|cerebras`), the first non-Anthropic writer P4 (`research-read` canary), the first cross-vendor second opinion P4 after D21, and the red-window hedge P5; the owner's ask begins ≈6 weeks after sign-off, and what P0–P1 buy is the observability and control needed to run it. What build weeks displace: **a LOOP.md §7 proposal against alpha-lab `budget.yaml` capping `alpha-research` jobs/day and ≤ 4 M/job** (the measured load is 47–86 M/day; `ALPHA_DAILY_JOB_VALVE` 12 → 6 does **not** remove ≈ 40 M/day — it gates only the idle drainer's `alpha-governor` enqueue, §2.8), **or** an explicit acceptance of N `rejected` windows/week; the `review-and-improve` idle trigger goes in P0 either way, as a containment item (§2.6 (c)), which leaves no retrospective loop until P4 — also chosen here, not discovered later. Also: prod `MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS` 2 → 3 after P3's RAM measurement **and** 7 consecutive days with zero `terminal_reason=rate_limited` on the Anthropic lane (`crosscut-tos §3.10b`), with `anthropic.max_sessions: 2` enforced regardless; the strict owner reservation. **Approve P0–P1.**
2. **D2 Protected-path PRs — split in two, because sequencing re-opened round 1's critical #1.** **PR #1a (approve at P3 *entry*)**: `lint_docs.py` as the hardcoded source of truth for the restraints in §2.5 (pinned set, pairing rule, `grader_fallback`, data-terms floors incl. the `codex` attestation floor, `cost_class` mandatory + metered ban via `owner_approved_metered`, chain-terminates-in-included-cost, `publish_allowlist`, atlas/alpha sensitivity floor, `no_llm` command rule) + `pipenv run python scripts/lint_docs.py` as a **named gate step** in `skills/server-deploy/SKILL.md` and `skills/server-patch/SKILL.md` (protected path #8 — today neither runs the linter, §2.5) + `src/runner/restraints.py` **plus `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json` and `scripts/install-dev-hooks.sh` (with its path list)** added to the MISSION §M protected list (the runtime fail-closed belt and the mechanical dev-side protected-path gate, §2.4/§2.5). **PR #1b (approve when P3 exits, lands in P4)**: `guards.py` vendor-key deny names + the sudoers wrapper path in `_HARD_DENY_PATTERNS` + INV-22 broker-host deny (Bash + `WebFetch|WebSearch` matcher) + `restart_project` in-tool second belt (the hook stays) + wiring the four unwired checks + two-repo drift lint + **the SYSTEM.md INV-13/INV-21 row wording** (owner-approved invariant text, plan §8) + **`skills/server-patch/SKILL.md`'s `origin=cross-vendor` → `approvals(kind=plan)` clause** (protected path #8; PR #1a touches the same file at P3 for the lint gate step, so it is listed explicitly here to keep the P4 edit from slipping — if it does, the cross-vendor gate is prose nobody wrote); `UNISOLATED_WRITER_ALLOWLIST` untouched. **Approve #1a at P3 entry, #1b when P3 exits.**
3. **D3 Auth config by hand** — see the §12a runbook for the full list and order. Headlines: Keychain `/login` stays primary; mint `claude setup-token` and seal it (0600, outside any workspace; never in the plist) — **needed before P3, not P0**: nothing can consume it until the executor seam exists, and the Keychain login is the only credential until then; `GEMINI_API_KEY` (unpaid); `codex login --device-auth` on Free run **as `_aicodex` through the wrapper** into `/Users/_aicodex/streams/stream-0/` (one login per stream, never copied); the sudoers drop-in + wrapper; the `publish.py` deploy key; `TRADIER_SANDBOX_TOKEN` + `FINNHUB_TOKEN` in atlas `.env` (P0); R2 credentials for backup; Max tier confirmed as 5x (MISSION §K); **confirm on claude.ai billing that usage credits are $0, auto-reload is off and the monthly spend limit is $0** (recorded as `overflow_credits: disabled`); **residual exposure, stated correctly:** the sealed token is readable by a Bash child of *every job that runs during a fallback window, hooked or not* — which is why the fallback is never fleet-wide (owner-resumed jobs only, §3) and why the audit redactor exists. **Do the P0-marked rows before P0 exit.**
4. **D4 Training toggles + retention**: Claude "Help improve Claude" off; ChatGPT "Improve the model for everyone" off; quarterly checklist card stored as an attestation row. Note: even with the toggle off, plan-auth Codex conversations are **retained until deleted, with no residency or ZDR option** (`chatgpt-openai.md` data matrix); the redacted projection is the only mitigation. **Yes — precondition for any thesis text to Codex, with the retention fact accepted.**
5. **D5 ChatGPT tier — a three-way decision, with the terms question separated from the capacity question.** Free carries the *advisory grader/critic belt* (scheduled, owner-read, `skipped` on quota) and, from P4, the `research-read` canary writer — both scheduled at volume, so both fall under `chatgpt-openai.md §3` **rule (b)** ("anything scheduled at volume, anything unattended for hours, and anything a second person consumes → `CODEX_API_KEY`"; §8 verdict 5: "scheduled volume … runs on `CODEX_API_KEY`") and are an **accepted risk on plan auth at any tier**. Options: **(a) Free with the belt** — rule-(b) risk accepted, capacity probably insufficient (~440 gradeable jobs/month sampled to ≈ 60–90 Codex runs bursting inside fixed ET slots, plus ~20 canary jobs); **(b) Plus $20** — the *same* rule-(b) risk, more capacity (a capacity statement, not a terms statement); registers as **`cost_class: plan-flat`** (a flat-rate subscription that is neither included in an existing seat, free, a trial nor per-token — the enum had no slot for it, §2.5) and still needs an `owner_approved_metered` entry plus a `METERED_PROVIDERS_OK` plist row before an executor will dispatch to it; **(c) a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` project key for the scheduled belt + canary only** — rule-(b) clean, no-training by default, ZDR-eligible (D4's retention note), ≈ $1–2/month at Luna-class or ≈ $27–40 at Sol-class for 60–90 grader runs **+ ≈ $10–16 for the canaries (round 3: the per-job figure was ≈ 2× low — ≈ $0.29/job, not $0.15, because all 380 k input had been priced at the cached rate), i.e. an option-(c) total of ≈ $37–56/month, above Plus's flat $20 — (c) buys rule-(b) cleanliness and ZDR, not a lower bill** (§0c) — a metered exception to 08-17 the owner must grant explicitly (listed in §13 beside D15). Also under D5: **no Codex credits purchased; spend control $0** (the RPC's `credits`/`spendControlReached` are read on every call and refuse dispatch otherwise, §2.8). The Plus/API-key decision is made on the P4 `skip_reason=quota` rate (>30 %) over dispatch *attempts*, **excluding `model_unavailable` and also `sampling_cap` and `session_cap`** — our own 6-per-5-h cap fires precisely on the burst days the measurement is taken, so labelling its skips `quota` would have made a self-imposed limit look like a vendor throttle (round 3) — never by falling back to Anthropic. **If D21 is declined the grader belt does not exist, so this rate is measured on the ~20 canary jobs alone; the smaller sample is stated with the decision rather than silently used.** **Confirm (a) now with rule (b) named as accepted risk; expect (b) on capacity; take (c) if the owner wants the clean lane.**
6. **D6 — split in round 3, because it bucketed two different things.** (a) **Metered side-cars** (xAI `x_search`, billed per item — $5/1k posts, $10/1k profiles; Perplexity `finance_search`): remain declined. **Defer; revisit on critic data.** (b) **Credit-bundled subscriptions — a separate decision, not a metered side-car:** Ollama Cloud Pro ($20/month → $60 of credits, 3 concurrent, `cost_class: credit-bundled`, `data: {trains: false, retains: false, max_sensitivity: proprietary}`, `openweight_lane` HTTP only — the Anthropic-format `/v1/messages` endpoint is unusable here because `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is banned at three enforcement points, §2.4). The research is explicit that this is *not* the same bucket: `crosscut-tos §3.7` line 129 calls it one of "the **two** credit-bundled subscriptions in this set … that fit owner policy (subscription, no standalone API key purchase)", line 134 rates it "**Yes** — credit-bundled metered use; 'Prompt or response data is never logged or trained on'; no automation or resale clause", §3.8 lists it among the only lanes acceptable for proprietary theses, and `§4.3` line 331 names "credit-bundled plans" in the very remedy list — "a second *lane* (Codex, routines, **credit-bundled plans**), never a second account" — that §2.8/§11/D25 quote with that third item dropped. Matrix §5 #25 ranks it "Ollama Cloud Pro → Mistral Pro"; Mistral Pro is excluded on its own basis (§3). **Defer, with the trigger named: revisit if D25 turns on "open a second lane" or if a proprietary-thesis HTTP lane is wanted — it is the only $0-training / no-logging host in the set that could take that text.**
7. **D7 Gemini unpaid key scope**: pre-fetched public documents and public-class memo grading only; never Telegram text, transcripts, theses. **Yes.**
8. **D8 INV-13 pre-push on Anthropic workspace lanes**: as first drafted `REVIEW_PRE_PUSH=anthropic:on` would have been a no-op — workspace sessions push to origin themselves and `sync_canonical` only ff-pulls afterwards (§5.3 belt 3a). Real pre-push on Anthropic lanes requires withholding push credentials from the session and having the runner push (the `publish.py` path built for non-Anthropic lanes) — a separate design. **Defer**; non-Anthropic lanes have the fail-closed pre-publish gate regardless.
9. **D9 Schedule ownership**: phone edits persist on non-governed rows; atlas loop rows file proposals (LOOP.md §7). **Yes.**
10. **D10 Auth PR — a security prerequisite, not a deep-link convenience**: CF Access on the dashboard vhost, SSE cookie, Web Push VAPID keys, **a verified `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` path in `_check_auth` (JWKS + `aud`/identity check) with the decide routes persisting the `aud` + subject on the `approvals` row**, and **a FastAPI `StaticFiles` mount for `static/app/` behind that vhost** — one protected-path #2 PR, plus the CF Access application + policy the owner creates in the Cloudflare dashboard **over every hostname that proxies `/api/*` (the apex `handle /api/*` included, `Caddyfile:18-26`) or the API moves to its own hostname**, with a service-token rule for `/api/*` so the `ai` CLI is not locked out (§12a row 7b). The first two additions are round-3 corrections: without the `aud` reader, §4.5's `aud` clause has no consumer and `decided_via ∈ {pwa, cli}` can never become acceptable; without the static mount, `static/app/` is reachable only through the **apex** `file_server`, a hostname with no Access that also proxies a second copy of every §4.6 route behind the one static bearer — i.e. the exposure D10 exists to close would have survived D10. Today the dashboard vhost is proxied on the tunnel with no Access layer (`Caddyfile:36-38`) and `_check_auth` is one static shared token (`web.py:49-63`; unset ⇒ open), and this spec routes deploy approvals, `providers pause|resume`, `provider_promote` and training attestations — decisions that unlock production deploys and proprietary routing — through that token from a public hostname. So: **hard *P2-entry* gate for any PWA/CLI mutating decision** — P1 ships neither surface (both are in P2), so a P1-exit gate added ceremony the owner could not discharge without a protected-path PR and 50 min of Cloudflare work for surfaces one phase away; until D10 lands, decision routes accept `decided_via=telegram` only and PWA/CLI decide routes return `403 needs_cf_access` (§4.5/§4.6), which is the belt P1 itself ships. `WEB_AUTH_TOKEN` is a P0 runbook row. **Deep links do not become frictionless even after D10** — a Telegram in-app browser carries neither the paste-bootstrapped bearer nor an Access cookie, so the PWA is a laptop/home-screen surface and `[Send file]` stays the primary file path (§4.2/§4.3). **Approve before P2 entry.**
11. **D11 Atlas front door — split in round 3, because P4 both shipped and depended on a piece the runbook did not let the owner approve until P5.** **D11a (needed before P4 entry, §12a row 13c, 15 min):** the `<<<VALIDATOR {verdict, findings} VALIDATOR>>>` block in the atlas research skills, via LOOP.md §7 — P4's scope includes it and P4's exit measures "anchor coverage ≥ 80 % of graded `trading-research` jobs", where §5.3 item 5 defines that anchor *as* this block. **D11b (needed before P5 entry):** `atlas-critic` stage, per-vertical `env_files`, constitution DoD checks, the governor "cite the DISSENT" amendment, and the ≥8-decoy calibration set. The two enumerations also disagreed — §12 D11 listed four items, §12a row 19b six — so an owner answering from §12 alone approved less than the runbook charged for; both now list the same items. **Yes to both, D11a at P4 entry.**
12. **D12 Ops debt in P0**: R2 off-site backup, `pg_dump atlas`, sealed `.env`/cloudflared copies, `pmset autorestart 1`, delete 14 GB stale Ollama weights, pull `qwen3.5:4b` + `embeddinggemma`. **Yes.**
13. **D13 SDK/CLI pin bump** (0.1.81 → 0.2.x bundling a CLI that knows Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5) behind the replay gate + `model_usage` assertion + a pin-bump checklist that includes **"SDK `env=` semantics (overlay vs replacement)"**, "`setting_sources` project-scope **auth *and* `hooks`/`permissions`/`mcpServers`** keys honoured?", "`RateLimitEvent` fields (`overage_*`) still emitted", **"the `RateLimitType` literal set (`five_hour`, `seven_day`, `seven_day_opus`, `seven_day_sonnet`, `overage`) unchanged"** and **"the exact `os.environ` refusal name list (§2.4) still matches the code"**; until then no new Anthropic id enters `providers.yml`. **Schedule after P3** (§12a row 20b).
14. **D14 Claude Routines `/fire` cold standby** for repo-scoped jobs when the Mini is down, and the first-party fallback if the Agent-SDK credit split returns (§2.8/§11). **Prepare, do not schedule**: register one repo-scoped routine **in P2a — it is in that scope cell and carries §12a row 9b (an account action only the owner can do, 15 min), because round 2's fix said "registered in P2" while §9 still listed it under P6 owner-gated and no runbook row existed, leaving the named mitigation for a quantified risk non-existent during the ~13 weeks it would be needed**; it enqueues nothing and draws the window only when fired by hand. P6 keeps only "fire it / widen its scope".
15. **D15 Codex `code-project` canary targets (P5 exit, was P6)** — rescoped: on ChatGPT plan auth only owner-only artefacts (owner-read report/doc repos, atlas research memos) **inside the hardcoded `publish_allowlist`** (atlas `research/**`, `docs/**`, `*/reports/**`; never `swing/**`, `tradingcore/**`, kernel limits, CI, `.claude/`); pickem/bingo/content-forge are public-facing and, per the spec's own ToS source, need a dedicated metered `CODEX_API_KEY` (priced at the `code-project` class shape ≈ $1.5–2/job, ≈ $1–3/month only because a 10 % canary is ~1.5 jobs — §7 step 3; a D6-class metered decision, shown in §13); never ai-server or atlas kernels. **Enter at P5 exit after the `research-read` canary's ≥20 jobs; approve the owner-only list and the allowlist now; decide the metered key with D6.**

17. **D17 Definition-of-done default**: per §5.1 (the single source) a job without a `done:` block runs the class-aware default contract — a pass shows `unverified`, a fail `failed_checks`, and `verified` requires a declared contract. **Honest.**
18. **D18 Free open-weight hosts on the utility chain** (Cerebras Free, Groq Free) for `owner-private` classification/routing text — never transcript-derived text (`utility-learning` stays local/Anthropic) — on the strength of their published no-retention / no-training terms (`direct-openweight-hosts.md §3`, partly unverified). Owner check: if Cerebras requires a card on file, confirm a $0-spend card-on-file lane is inside the 2026-08-17 no-metered-spend decision before the key is added; **and confirm whether free-tier usage is billed against the "$5 credit"** (`direct-openweight-hosts.md §4.2–4.3`: "Free Trial + $5 credit", "$5 credit as buffer") — if it is, the entry is `cost_class: trial` with an exhaustion date, the balance shows on the CostCard, and a `provider_fallback` card fires at 0 (the lane *expires* rather than churns). **Approve for shadow in P3; qualify on measured agreement; owner adds the two keys by hand.**
19. **D19 Remote MCP front door** (read-only tools behind Cloudflare Access, sensitivity-gated reads per caller identity, "untrusted data" marker on every result) as part of the auth PR; the ChatGPT door exists only if D5 → Plus. **Approve with D10.**
20. **D20 Prefect 3 shadow pilot** as an optional run-history UI. **Defer; revisit only if the PWA's Jobs/Schedules views leave "what happened?" questions unanswered after P2.**
21. **D21 Cross-vendor graders/critics (reverses plan §7's R6+ deferral: "Claude reviewing GPT code is in-plan; GPT reviewing anything is not")**: allow Codex (and Gemini-free on public-class bundles only) to produce *advisory* CONCUR/DISSENT grades on `research-read` and, after D4, redacted `trading-research` memos; on `code-project` only after 30 shadow grades; never on `code-server`/`review-gate`/`_evaluate`; verdicts are recorded as dissent and never gate a merge; Anthropic remains the only LGTM. **Recommended: approve for shadow in P4 on `research-read` and `trading-research`; `code-project` after 30 shadow grades.** Until decided, `grading.py` is inert and `code-project: grader: none`. **In the D1 sign-off set from round 3, and decided at *P3 exit*, not P4 entry** — it is a 15-minute decision (§12a row 17) that halves or doubles a three-week phase, and round 2 scheduled it at the entry of the phase it sizes. **If declined:** P4's grading exit criteria (≥100 graded jobs, grader error <5 %, anchor coverage ≥ 80 %, Jaccard/κ/`dissent_precision`) are **struck**, and P4 exits on the containment, `ask codex` and `research-read` writer-canary criteria alone (≈1.5 agent-weeks rather than 3); `grading.py`, migration 010, the grader ladder and the scoreboard's grader rows move to P5; **P5 drops `atlas-critic`, the ≥8-decoy calibration set, the `critic_verdicts` mirror and its critic exit line**; `--second-opinion=<provider>` answers `provider_refused{reason: pairing}`; and D5's quota-skip rate is measured on ~20 canary jobs only, stated as such. Without that sentence two of six phases had no defined content under one of the two answers.
22. **D22 Status-line feed — a one-time setup, not a weekly chore.** The owner adds a `statusLine` command to `~/.claude/settings.json` on the Mini (and optionally the laptop) that appends `{ts, rate_limits}` to `volumes/telemetry/claude_rate_limits.jsonl` (5-min de-dup; the `claude-anthropic.md §3` recipe, line 85: "the dashboard's remaining-budget tile should be fed by a statusLine command that appends that JSON to a file"). The owner already runs interactive `claude` sessions on the Mini continuously and the window is shared across every Keychain consumer, so this gives the runner a near-continuous vendor-sourced 5h/7d gauge for zero recurring effort. The runner's quota reader takes the newest line as `quota_snapshots(source=vendor-statusline)`, shows its age on the QuotaStrip (`7d 58 % · 40 min ago`), labels "inferred" only when the newest line is older than 24 h and "uncalibrated" only when older than 7 d. The SDK *does* emit a `seven_day` transition (first observed 2026-09-25; §2.8), so the ledger-inferred figure is now the fallback between vendor readings, and the status-line series settles whether the vendor window is fixed or rolling. No runner-driven probe — these are the owner's own sessions. **Recommended: do the one-time setup (≈5 min, §12a row 9).**
23. **D23 Interfacing with the other models directly**: a bounded one-shot `ask <provider> "<text>"` verb (no tools, owner-private by default, answer as a DeliverableCard with cost) — **P3 for HTTP/local lanes** (`ollama-local`, `anthropic-utility`; `cerebras-free`/`groq-free` after D18), **P4 for `ask codex`** (through `codex_cli.py` as `_aicodex` under the stream lease, at the `public` ceiling until the D4 attestation, only after Codex leaves `blocked`; round 1 had promised `ask codex` in P3 while every prerequisite — CLI install, `_aicodex`, login, executor — was P4, and a forced P3 path would have re-opened round-1 critical #2). Conversational chat with non-Anthropic models on Telegram/PWA is **not** provided (Sonnet-low TTFT, owner-private text, training toggles). **Recommended: approve `ask`; record chat as a later option.**
24. **D24 Read-only data MCPs in research sessions** (alpaca-mcp-server, sec-edgar-mcp, mcp_massive, finnhub): the servers ship order tools, so mounting one is an LLM-reachable order path and a protected-path change under INV-22. If ever wanted: `allowed_tools` names the read tools explicitly (never `mcp__alpaca__*`), a PreToolUse deny matcher `mcp__alpaca__.*(order|position|close).*` in both guard profiles via an owner PR, `ALPACA_PAPER_TRADE` never set, weekly tripwire on a fixture `place_order`. **Recommended: no — market data stays on atlas `tradingcore` as today.**
25. **D25 The Max seat is loaded at ≈ 8–10× its price against Anthropic's "ordinary, individual usage" wording** (§2.8: ≈ $963/month list-equivalent on Max 5x; `crosscut-tos §4.3`: above ~2× the plan price is "outside ordinary, individual usage" and the fix is a second *lane*, never a second account; `§3.1`: weekly limits were introduced "explicitly to curb … continuous background usage"; enforcement observed so far = the cap itself). This is a ToS-posture decision, not a runtime rule — the runtime tripwire is alarm-only (§2.8). Options: **(a) accept the exposure** as today, with the alarm keeping drift visible; **(b) trim cadence** to the per-skill token-shape targets in §11 — **a LOOP.md §7 proposal against alpha-lab `budget.yaml` capping `alpha-research` jobs/day and ≤ 4 M/job (sized from the measured 47–86 M/day; `ALPHA_DAILY_JOB_VALVE` 12 → 6 is *not* that lever — it gates only the idle drainer's `alpha-governor` enqueue, §2.8)**, atlas-build ≤ 5 M/job, atlas-report ≤ 0.5 M/job — toward the ~2× band; **(c) open a second lane** now (branch B / D5 option (c) metered `CODEX_API_KEY`; D14 Routines; **a credit-bundled plan such as Ollama Cloud Pro, the third item in the research's own remedy list, D6(b)**). Note the metric caveat from §2.8: on `crosscut-tos §4.3`'s own formula (no cache-creation term) this ledger is ≈ $550/month ≈ 5.5× the seat; ≈ $963 is the more complete accounting but not the number the ~2× heuristic was written against. MISSION §K line 136 settles the tier as Max 5x, so §14 Q1 is closed as "confirm, not discover". **Recommended: (b) during the build phases (it also funds the build's own window cost) and revisit (c) at the P4 retro with the canary's quota-skip data.**

## 13) Effort and monthly cost

- **Effort**: P0 1 + P1 1.5 + **P2a 1.5 + P2b 1** + P3 2.5 + **P4a 1.5 + P4b 1.5 (or 1.5 total if D21 is declined)** + P5 2 = **~12.5 agent-weeks** of session time on the INV-4 lane, ~11 if D21 is declined (one gated deploy per sub-slice; P4b's ≥20-canary-job and ≥100-graded-job bars are **calendar soak, not effort**), executed by server-patch sessions that draw the same Max window as the schedules. **Build-phase window cost, stated as an absolute ceiling: ≈ 28 M tokens ≈ $30 `cost_usd_list` per week (≈ $115/month list-equivalent for ~13 weeks), `purpose=build` on the `build` lane, held like any non-governed lane and repeated in each phase's PR template** — not "15 % of the weekly window", which floated with a denominator §2.8 forbids (§9). D1 states what it displaces (an alpha-lab `budget.yaml` proposal, or N `rejected` windows/week accepted — §2.8); the idle auditor goes off in P0 regardless. D1 approves only P0–P1 (≈2.5 agent-weeks), **which contain no other vendor** (§12 D1). Owner time per the §12a footer: **≈ 11.5 h one-off (689 min over 38 rows, decisions and PR reviews included) + 4.5 h phase sign-offs + 5 min/quarter — of which the sign-off set in front of the owner now is ≈ 3.2 h (194 min)**, plus ~1 h/week reading DoneCards, DISSENTs and the first retros.
- **Monthly cost, default plan (D0 branch A as corrected): $0 incremental in cash.** Claude Max 5x as today ($100, MISSION §K); ChatGPT Free $0 (the `research-read` canary is on Free plan auth); Gemini unpaid $0; local $0; OpenRouter $0 (unused); Cloudflare R2 free ≤ 10 GB-month on a card-on-file account with the 60-d retention rule (§12a row 4 — not "free" without qualification). **The honest cost figure is the prod ledger (§2.8) at the current run-rate, not the 30-d mean: ≈ $963/month list-equivalent on the trailing 30 d at the 1-h cache-write rate the subscription uses — ≈ 8–10× the seat, and cache-*write*-led in dollars (write ≈ 42 %, read ≈ 37 %, output ≈ 22 %) although 93 % cache-read in tokens (opus-5 $414, opus-4-7 $255, sonnet-4-6 $151, opus-4-8 $143) — and the Sep 23–25 run-rate since the alpha flywheel (88 M tokens/day, ≈ 3× the 30-d mean) implies ≈ $2,050–3,400/month list-equivalent if sustained at the measured $1.121/M, i.e. the fixed 7-d window (≈ $200/week, §2.8) fills in ≈ 2.5–3 days.** The spec's belts change that by, **all at one consistent rate and taken from §2.8's net figures rather than re-derived (round 3: the three belts implied $2.00/M, $1.00–1.50/M and $0.50/M for the same provider, and 36 M cache-read at Opus 4.7's $0.50/M is $18, not $60)**: retro **−≈$15** (net −30 M/month of Opus cache-read at $0.50/M), provisioning-gap pre-check **−≈$10** (−20 M, and **unmeasured until the P3 version lands with a real key list**, §8.3), in-process utility **−≈$2** (−4 M), **Haiku→Sonnet +≈$8/month list-equivalent, 0 token change** (until P3) ⇒ **net ≈ −$19/month**, against build sessions at **+≈$115/month for ~13 weeks**; graders +$0 on Anthropic. All of it is noise against the measured ≈ +1,550 M/month flywheel step, which only an alpha-lab `budget.yaml` cap, the per-skill token-shape targets and a second lane move (D25). Grading adds ≈ 60–90 Codex runs/month under `grader_sampling` (not 30–120 fleet-wide "short runs"), ~20 canary jobs, and ~100–200 local/Cerebras/Groq/Gemini utility calls.
- **Probable on capacity, not on terms: ChatGPT Plus $20 (D5 option (b))** — at current atlas volume the sampled grader belt + canary will most likely exceed Free's bursty 5-h bands; moving to Plus adds capacity and changes nothing about the rule-(b) classification of scheduled volume on plan auth (§3 Codex row); fallback stays `skipped`, never Anthropic. **Optional, each an owner decision**: a metered `CODEX_API_KEY` for the scheduled belt + canary (D5 option (c), the rule-(b)-clean lane) ≈ $1–2 (Luna-class) or ≈ $27–40 (Sol-class) **+ ≈ $10–16 canaries at the corrected ≈ $0.29/job — an option-(c) total of ≈ $37–56/month, i.e. above Plus's flat $20**; metered `CODEX_API_KEY` for public-repo `code-project` canaries ≈ $1–3 at the class shape (D15/D6a); **xAI prepaid ≈ $5–10 at one sentiment sweep per trading day, billed per item ($5/1k posts, $10/1k profiles — §3)** (D6a); Perplexity ~$5 (D6a); **Ollama Cloud Pro $20 → $60 of credits, `cost_class: credit-bundled` (D6b — a subscription the research says fits owner policy, not a metered side-car)**; DeepInfra overflow ≈ $0.35–1 (declined); Cerebras `trial` exhaustion is a lane expiry, not a bill (D18). **Worst case with every option ≈ $115–135/month on top of Max** (re-checked in round 3 against the corrected canary line; round 2's "≈ $90–100" predated it). No new always-on daemon; ~150 MB per subprocess executor as today; one ≤3.4 GB local model resident only during its own calls.

## 14) Open questions

1. ~~Owner's Max tier (5x vs 20x)~~ — **closed in round 2**: MISSION.md:136 (§K) states Max 5x; §12a row 1 is "confirm, not discover".
2. Does the pinned CLI 2.1.139 honour `sandbox.failIfUnavailable`/`strictAllowlist` for the Claude lane? **Moved into the P0 gate list (round 3)** — the research rates `claude -p` "A (with `failIfUnavailable` + `strictAllowlist` + `--allowedTools` narrowed per job)" and makes its own rule "A-rated lanes **with the sandbox forced on**", so this answer decides whether the lane that receives every untrusted stream meets the rule §2.6 cites for every other lane; a P5/D13 answer arrives four phases late. **Also:** does the pinned CLI honour `apiKeyHelper`, `env.ANTHROPIC_*`, `env.CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from *project* scope (`setting_sources=["project"]`, `session.py:716`)? The §2.4 settings-file refusal ships in P0 regardless, and a verified "no" removes only the **auth** half — `hooks`, `permissions` and `mcpServers` are honoured from project scope regardless, which is the larger half of the hole.
3. Can the `code-review` subagent return structured output under SDK 0.1.81 `AgentDefinition`? Nice-to-have only: the P1 mechanism is the first-line `LGTM|CHANGES|BLOCKER` parse (`skills/code-review/SKILL.md:128-130`) plus the full-text `subagent_result` event; structured capture waits for D13.
4. Codex Free grader capacity in practice (bands unpublished): the P4 skip-rate measurement decides D5.
5. Will a read-only atlas DB role be acceptable under LOOP.md §6 (dependency/`[system]` ceilings) for `db_row` checks and the Atlas view? Proposal through the §7 front door.
6. Whether the runner should execute `tests_green` in atlas clones (needs the per-run venv the state map calls a recurring failure class) or keep `tests_ran` transcript evidence for atlas until the venv is cached via `env_files`.
7. Telegram edit-rate ceilings for a busy day (several concurrent JobCards): the 10 s coalescing is an assumption to measure in P1.
8. Whether `queue_wait_ms` should count time held for a red window separately (`held_ms`) so lane starvation and quota holds are distinguishable on the scoreboard.
9. Cerebras/Groq free-tier numbers that the gap-fill doc could not confirm on a primary page (Cerebras total TPM, Groq per-model buckets) — re-verify with web search enabled before P3 relies on them.
10. Whether the Claude SDK's OTEL `claude_code.cost.usage` metric is populated under Max subscription auth (`workflow-orchestrators.md §7a` could not confirm) — decides whether OTEL export adds anything over `ResultMessage` capture.
11. Whether `codex exec -c key=value` per-job config overrides exist on the pinned brew CLI — the research corpus never mentions `-c`/`--config`. **Now a P4 *entry* criterion with a behavioural assertion, not a flag-presence one (round 3)**: a fixture run must prove each of the six pinned overrides *took effect* (one `turn.completed` with goals off; no outbound telemetry connection; `curl` inside the copy fails; `auth.json` in the stream home). If `-c` is unhonoured, the per-stream `config.toml` is **mandatory** (§12a row 16b) and `codex_cli.py` refuses to dispatch without a matching parsed config. The overrides are containment, not conveniences (§2.4), so "flag present in the invocation" was a test that passed while the lane ran with goals on and telemetry phoning home.
12. ~~Whether the SDK's `seven_day` window is a fixed period ending at `resets_at` or rolling~~ — **closed 2026-09-27**: 28 `seven_day` readings over 45 h all carry `resets_at` 2026-10-02T00:00Z, so it is a **fixed** weekly period; a rolling window's reset would have advanced. The cap is calibrated in dollars from the utilization series (≈ $200/week working denominator, §2.8), and D22's remaining value is extending the series across the owner's *interactive* sessions, which the runner's own events do not see.
14. **Is the weekly bar cost-weighted?** §2.8 budgets in dollars per model family on that assumption, and it is an inference from the prod utilization series, not a vendor statement: the research establishes only that separate Opus/Sonnet weekly bars exist (`crosscut-tos` line 63) and that token budgets are unpublished (`claude-anthropic.md` line 90), and it offers a third weighting model elsewhere ("a Max user's 5-hour session now consumes 20 % of weekly capacity instead of 10 %", line 60). **The first `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet` event closes it** — the SDK already emits those types. If the bar is time- or token-weighted instead, the dollar budgets over-hold Opus lanes and under-hold Sonnet ones.
13. Whether the **setgid `ai-codex` copy dir (2770)** the runner creates with `git archive` + `chgrp -R` + `chmod -R g+rwX` is enough for `_aicodex` to write `out/` and for the runner to read it back on this APFS volume with the owner's umask — a P4 fixture check on the box, not a design question. (Restated in round 3: the wrapper no longer has a `materialise` verb, so this is about a directory the runner owns and shares by group, not one `_aicodex` created.)

## Appendix A — how this spec was produced (2026-09-24 → 2026-09-25)

1. **Research** — 28 docs in `docs/research/llm-landscape-2026-09/` (17 in the first round, 8 added by a completeness critic over two more rounds, 3 gap-fill docs after the design panel), each written by a web-research agent, then adversarially fact-checked by a second agent against primary sources, then corrected with a "Verification log" appended. `00-comparison-matrix.md` reconciles them (31 cross-doc contradictions resolved in its §5). One fact-check (`nvidia-nemotron-3.md`) did not complete because of an API rate limit; that doc carries no verification log and is not load-bearing for this spec.
2. **Current-state map** — eight parallel readers over the executor path, job lifecycle, user surfaces, skills/schedules/evals, routing/quality loop, atlas trading integration, governance, and ops; 40 of their claims were adversarially spot-checked against the code (17 corrected). The map is committed at `docs/research/llm-landscape-2026-09/design-panel/current-state-map.md`; its load-bearing findings are cited inline as "state map §n" and its §3 constraints table (C1–C26) is the compliance checklist for §2.6 and §9. One correction made during review round 1: the map's §5.3 claim that the 636 `rate_limit_status` events carry `utilization`/`resets_at` overstated the data (utilization was present on 4 of 661 at that count) — §0a row 6 and §2.8 describe what the events actually carry. **Data note, round 2:** round 1's own "no `seven_day` event exists" became false at 2026-09-25 13:57Z — job 266df9ed emitted `rate_limit_type=seven_day, status=allowed_warning, utilization=0.25, resets_at=2026-10-02T00:00Z`; the totals are now 665 events = 664 `five_hour` + 1 `seven_day`, utilization on 5 (four `five_hour` at 0.91/0.96/0.96/0.96, one `seven_day` at 0.25). Two further ledger corrections from round 2: every prod cache write is recorded as `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` (45.3 M 1-h, 0 5-m over 634 priced jobs), so list-equivalents are priced at the 1-h rate (≈ $963/month, not $809); and the alpha flywheel (commit 5448c81, 2026-09-23) tripled daily consumption after the 30-d window the round-1 table averaged (week 39 = 279 M tokens in five days), so every budget is now anchored on the trailing 7 d.
3. **Design panel** — six architects, one lens each (the seven design documents, including the synthesis, are committed under `docs/research/llm-landscape-2026-09/design-panel/`; the kernel-overhaul case that scored 7.46 and the judges' reasons for preferring the two 7.71 designs are in `design-kernel-overhaul.md` and `design-synthesis.md` there); three judges scored every design on eight criteria (ease of use across interfaces, visibility, deliverable effectiveness, safety/invariants, cost/subscription fit, feasibility on this hardware and codebase, migration risk, upside). Mean scores: interface-first 7.71, outcome-first 7.71, kernel overhaul 7.46, incremental 6.75, multi-bot chat 6.29, vendor-native hub 5.25. The judges' fatal-flaw findings that shaped this spec: Gemini's unpaid tier (trains on content) must never classify owner text; a non-Anthropic writer's diff would reach canonical repos before review because ff-sync runs before post-review (hence mandatory pre-publish review on non-Anthropic lanes — round 2 then moved the first non-Anthropic writer to a P4 `research-read` canary *behind* that gate, as the 08-17 plan had approved, rather than deferring it to P6); mutating tools inside a model-driven front door are a prompt-injection path (hence read-only `ai-mcp`); phone edits must not bypass the atlas LOOP.md §7 front door (hence `governed` rows); INV-13 blocking on Anthropic lanes would re-create the 2026-08-17 governor-dark incident (hence flag-only by default); the Telegram bot is the single human channel and must not be rewritten in place (hence new handlers beside legacy with `CONTROL_PLANE_V2=0`).
4. **Synthesis** — interface-first's control plane, phasing and lanes as the spine; outcome-first's Deliverable Contract, verdict ledger, provider ledger and redacted projection as the effectiveness core; grafts from the other four marked `[graft: …]` inline.
5. **Critical review loop** — eight reviewer lenses (safety/invariants, vendor terms/account risk, cost, engineering feasibility, owner UX, effectiveness, completeness against the ask, harsh "scrap or keep" reviewer); every critical/major finding was put to three independent skeptics and applied only if it survived. **Round 1 is complete and applied (2026-09-25; 8 critical, ~50 major, 14 minor findings). Round 2 is complete and applied (2026-09-25; 6 critical, ~45 major, 14 minor). Round 3 is complete and applied (2026-09-27; 14 verified findings with two skeptics each, ~50 reported-but-unverified majors, 19 minors — log below).** **Rounds 1–3 are complete and round 3 is terminal: the spec is frozen.** Findings after it are filed against the P0/P1 *implementation plans*, not as spec edits. The earlier rule ("the loop continues until two consecutive rounds surface nothing serious") could not terminate on this document, and round 3 says why: each round's applications *added* to the surface the next round had to review — round 1 added D0 and D21–D24 plus §12a, round 2 added D25 and §0c and split D2, round 3 added D6(b), D11a/D11b and two `cost_class` values — so across ~130 applied findings the net structural change was +6 decisions, +2 sections and zero phases removed, while the one surviving scope cut moved work to later phases rather than out. That is convergence on completeness, not on execution, and it is the mechanism by which a spec becomes too large and hedged to execute. The remedy is the freeze plus the §12 split into a **sign-off set** (six decisions, ≈3.2 h of owner time) and a **deferred register**.

## Review log

Round, lens and disposition for every finding that survived its skeptics. "Applied" means the spec text changed in the named sections. Rounds 1–2 skipped nothing; round 3 (whose verification was capped — see its header) labels two rows superseded and one row applied with a corrected closure, and explains each in place. Duplicate findings from different lenses are merged into one row (count in brackets).

### Round 1 — 2026-09-25 (8 critical · ~50 major · 14 minor; all applied)

Decision numbering, round 2: **D25** (Max-seat load vs "ordinary, individual usage") added; D2 split into PR #1a/#1b; D22 re-scoped from a weekly dump to a one-time status-line feed; D15 moved P6 → P5; D14 from "defer" to "prepare, do not schedule". Where a finding offered alternatives the spec records the chosen one: #25 design (B) in-process gate (not the event-driven child job); #1 the fixed-argv wrapper (not a bare `codex` sudoers entry) and `/Users/_aicodex/streams/` (not `/Users/Shared/ai-codex/streams/`) for the stream homes; #13 alarm-only with a 90-d-median hold on non-governed lanes only; #23 Z.ai dropped rather than given an executor.

### Round 3 — 2026-09-27 (**verification capped at 14 findings, two independent skeptics each; 61 further majors reported but unverified** · 19 minors; **round 3 is terminal — the spec is frozen, Appendix A §5**)

**Verification was capped this round: 14 findings were put to two independent skeptics each and survived; 61 further majors were reported but not skeptic-verified.** The rule applied below: a verified finding is applied (or resolved by a better fix); an unverified major is applied only where round 3 could confirm it itself in one read — against the spec's own text, the research docs, the installed SDK, the working tree or arithmetic — and the basis is named in each row; anything it could not confirm is recorded as "reported, unverified, not applied". Ground truth re-checked on the box before applying: `git ls-files .claude/` → `settings.json` (`enabledPlugins`) + `settings.local.json` (`permissions`), both tracked; `session.py:716` `setting_sources=["project"]`; dev `.git/hooks/pre-commit` enforces CHANGELOG on `^src/` only; `events.py:364-429` (the valve gates the idle drainer's `alpha-governor` enqueue, counted on `Job.kind == "alpha-research"`); `atlas/manifest.yml:18-20` `env_required: [DATABASE_URL, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY]`; `claude_agent_sdk/types.py:1181-1183` `RateLimitType = {five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_opus, seven_day_sonnet, overage}` in the installed 0.1.81; `web.py:49-63` static token with `if not token: return`, no `StaticFiles` mount, `POST /api/jobs` at `:377` and `/api/tasks` at `:526,547`; `Caddyfile:18-26` apex `file_server` + `handle /api/*`; `git -C projects/atlas remote -v` HTTPS with `credential.helper=osxkeychain`; `.env` Postgres/Redis on localhost TCP; prod audit scan 2026-09-27 → 732 `rate_limit_status` events (4 five-hour `allowed_warning`, **2** `rejected` at 09-24T14:00Z and 09-25T17:30Z, **28** `seven_day` `allowed_warning` 0.25→0.58 all with `resets_at` 2026-10-02T00:00Z).

**Nothing in this round was skipped as contradicting a hard constraint.** Two dispositions are not straight applications and are labelled above: **#9 and #14** are superseded by the P0 plan's own 2026-09-27 re-cut (verified against the working tree), so the spec records the *remaining* ten-item delta instead of re-applying fixes already in place; and **M10**'s proposed closure (a wrapper check that the copy dir is `_aicodex`-owned) is replaced by path/symlink/mode checks plus an honest residual, because the runner and every session share one uid and no in-wrapper test can distinguish them — D16 is named as the only real fix. Where a finding offered alternatives the spec records the chosen one: **#1** hash-pinning in `restraints.py` (not a blanket ban on those keys, which the repo's own tracked `permissions` block would violate); **#12** the plan's exempt-set + fail-open design (not a per-kind list *instead of* the manifest); **M34/M35** sub-slices inside P2/P4 (not renumbered phases, which would have invalidated every cross-reference); **M49** a summary block plus a retitled §0c (not relocating the section).

Decision numbering, round 3: **D6 split into (a) metered side-cars and (b) credit-bundled subscriptions**; **D11 split into D11a (P4 entry) and D11b (P5)**; **D0 reduced to a reserved pointer** (branch B is a trigger inside D5); **D21 moved into the sign-off set** and decided at P3 exit; `cost_class` gains `plan-flat` and `credit-bundled`; §12a gains rows **9b** (D14 routine), **13c** (D11a) and **16b** (mandatory per-stream `config.toml`), and the footer is re-computed at 689 min ≈ 11.5 h one-off, of which the sign-off set is 194 min ≈ 3.2 h.
